[[P1202]]CHAPTER XXIX 

Some Fundamental Doctrines of [[Purvamimāmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] 

Some of the characteristic fundamental doctrines of the [[Pūrvamimāṁsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] will now be set out with some references and a few [[renarks|remarks]] as to some of them, the views of [[Prabhakara|Prabhākara]] and his followers being also rarely referred [[to|to.]] 

(1) Veda is eternal, self-existent, not composed by any author, human or divine, and is infallible. This is the core of the [[P, M,|P. M.]] system. Vide P. M. S. [[1. 1.|I. 1.]] 27-32 and [[Sabara|Śabara]] on [[1, 1.|I. 1.]] 5 (p. 53) and [[ślokavārtika|Ślokavārtika]] ([[Vākyādhikarana,|Vākyādhikaraṇa,]] [[verges 1953|verses]] [^1953] [[366 368|366-368]]). The argument briefly is; Veda is learnt at present and [[. was|was]] studied in [[tbe|the]] past also by students from teachers, that there is no evidence about the person who first composed it or who first studied it. If it be argued that such a reasoning may be put forward as regards the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]], the reply is that people know that Vyāsa is the author of it. Similarly, the passages in [[Smrtis|Smṛtis]] and Purāṇas that [[Prajapati|Prajāpati]] created the Veda are merely [[artharadas|arthavādas]] ([[laudatory )|laudatory)]] not based on any evidence or perception and are meant only to convey the absolute [[autho ritativeness|authoritativeness]] of the Veda. If the connection between word and [[. songe|sense]] is eternal and not brought about by any person, the same reasoning holds good as to the Veda. This view is opposed to the view of the Naiyāyikas who hold that God is the author of the [[Voda|Veda]] or the interpretation of V. S. I. 1. 3 ([[Sastrayonitvāt|Śāstrayonitvāt]]) by [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] based on Bṛ. Up. [[II, 4. 10.|II. 4. 10.]] Manu [[I, 21 (|I. 21 (]]stating that Brahmā created from the words of the [[Voda|Veda]] the names and appropriate duties of all) seems to suggest that (according to 

[^1953]: [[-- ----- ------ -- - ---- - ---- 1953. OD|On]] P. M. S. I. 1. 5 [[Sabara|Śabara]] comments (pp. 52-53) '[[PATATE|तस्मान्मन्यामहे]] केनापि पुरुषेण [[शब्दानामथैः|शब्दानांमर्थैः]] सह सम्बन्धं कृत्वा [[संन्यवहत|संव्यवहर्तुं]] वेदाः प्रणीता इति। [[तदिवानीमुच्यते|तदिदानीमुच्यते]] । अपौरुषेयत्वात्सम्बन्धस्य सिद्धमिति। कथं पुनरिदमवगम्यतेऽपौरुषेय [[एंव|एव]] सम्बन्ध इति । पुरुषस्य सम्बन्धुरभावात्। कथं सम्बन्धी नास्ति । प्रत्यक्षस्य प्रमाणस्याभावात् [[तत्पूर्वकत्वाञ्चेतरेषाम् ।|तत्पूर्वकत्वाच्चेतरेषाम् ।';]] '[[घेदस्याध्ययन सर्व एर्वध्ययनपूर्वकम्।|वेदस्याध्ययनं सर्वं गुर्वध्ययनपूर्वकम्।]] [[घेदाध्ययनवाच्यवादधुनाध्ययनं|वेदाध्ययनवाच्यत्वादधुनाध्ययनं]] यथा॥ [[भारतेपि|भारतेऽपि]] भवेदेष कर्तृस्मृत्या तु बाध्यते। [[वेदेपि|वेदेऽपि]] तत्स्मृतिर्या तु [[सार्थवावनिअन्धना।|सार्थवादनिबन्धना॥]] पारम्पर्येण कर्तारं नाध्येतारं स्मरन्ति हि।' [[Fiat Risaraniitariuta|श्लोकवार्तिकवाक्याधिकरण]] verses 366-368; the [[FUCKIT|प्रकरणपञ्चिका]] [[(p. 140), remarks 'fui gareulich aqraqi 5964 FATONII ... 16 Film Pana a Hindu GTACI Argarit att. The argument when put in a syllogistic form would run like this: 27: srir : , Wwiatore raia! q ar U HETTICHE grantetFar on a. a. 1.3,29 (are ce a facha) begins bis \#loy with the words at Teoman: rva777 Forsyth|(p. 140), remarks 'कथं पुनरपौरुषेयत्वं वेदानाम् । पुरुषस्य कर्तुरस्मरणात् । ... काठकादिसमाख्यापि न कर्तृसद्भावमुपकल्पयितुमलम् । प्रवचनेनापि तदुपपत्तेः ।'. The argument when put in a syllogistic form would run like this: 'वेदाः अपौरुषेयाः, अस्मर्यमाणकर्तृकत्वात्। यन्नैवं तन्नैवं यथा महाभारतरघुवंशादि।'. शङ्कराचार्य on वे. सू. I. 3. 29 (अत एव च नित्यत्वम्) begins his भाष्य with the words 'स्वतन्त्रस्य कर्तुरस्मरणादिभिः स्थिते वेदस्य नित्यत्वे.']]

[[P1203]]Manu) the Veda was self-existent. Similarly, the Mahābhāṣya on Vārtika 3 on Pāṇini IV. 3. 101 ("tena proktam') remarks[^1954] that the Vedas were not composed by anyone, that they are eternal, that the meaning of the Veda is eternal, but the [[arrange ment|arrangement]] of the letters is not eternal and therefore we have different vedic texts like [[Kāthaka,|Kāṭhaka,]] Kālāpaka, Paippalādaka &c. The Smṛtis also sometimes state that there is no author of the Veda, that Brahmā remembers it and that Manu also has remembrance of Dharma in different Kalpas ([[Parāśarasmrti I, 21|Parāśarasmṛti I. 21]]). 

In P. M. S. I. 1. 28 ([['Anityadarsanāc-ca'|'Anityadarśanāc-ca']]) the opponent of the eternality of the Veda puts forward such passages as 'Babara Prāvāhaṇi (son of [[Pravāhapa|Pravāhaṇa]]) desired' (Tai. [[8.|S.]] VII. 1. 10. 2) and 'Kusuruvinda [[Auddalaki|Auddālaki]] desired' (Tai. S. VII. 2. [[%.|2.]] 1), which mention men named [[Prāvāhani|Prāvāhaṇi]] and [[Auddalaki|Auddālaki]] (son of Uddālaka) that are mortals and argues that Veda did not exist before those mortals and is, therefore, not eternal. To this the Siddhānta replies in P. M. S. I. 1. 31 ('param tu śrutisāmānyam') that such examples are to be explained differently viz, 'Babara' is a onomatopoetic word meaning 'rustling' and [[Prāvāhaṛi|Prāvāhaṇi]] (from 'pra' and 'vāhay' causal of [[lah|vah]]) means the wind. 

It may be noted that centuries before Jaimini and [[Yaska|Yāska]] there was a school of Vedic interpreters called [['Aitibāsikas'.|'Aitihāsikas'.]] For example, [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 98. 5 and 7 refer to Devāpi, son of [[Rṣtiṣena,|Ṛṣṭiṣeṇa,]] and to [[Santanu.|Śantanu.]] Yāska (in Nirukta II. 10), starting with the words [["tatra-itihāsamācakṣate'|'tatra-itihāsamācakṣate']] states that Devāpi and [[Santanu|Śantanu]] were brothers of the Kuru family and the younger brother [[Santanu wa8.crowned|Śantanu was crowned]] king ignoring the rights of the elder and that these verses refer to them. In [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 10 there is a dialogue between Yama and [[Yami|Yamī]] and Nir. V. 2 refers to the 8th verse of it. Those who hold that Veda is eternal would explain Yama as [[Aditya|Āditya]] and [[Yami|Yamī]] as 'Night'. [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] III. 33 is a dialogue between the sage [[Visvimitra|Viśvāmitra]] and the rivers. The Nir. (II. [[25–27|25-27]]) explains verses 5-6 and 10 of that hymn from the [[Aitihānika|Aitihāsika]] point of view and states that Viśvāmitra was son of king [[Kusika|Kuśika]] and does not mention the Nairukta interpretation of these verses. 

[^1954]: ननु चोक्तं न हि [[धम्दासि|छन्दांसि]] क्रियन्ते नित्यानि [[धन्दांसीति।|छन्दांसीति।]] [[यद्यप्यों|यद्यप्यर्थो]] नित्यो या [[वसौ|त्वसौ]] वर्णानुपूर्वी सा नित्या। [[तद्भेदाचैतजपति काठक|तद्भेदाच्चैतद्भवति काठकं]] [[कालापकं मोदकं पप्पलादकमिति ।|कालापकं मौदकं पैप्पलादकमिति ।]] [[FETHIY on and 3 op 'Strong ito. IV. 3. 87. mira|महाभाष्य on वार्तिक 3 on 'अधिकृत्य कृते ग्रन्थे' पा. IV. 3. 87. पाणिनि]] emphasizes this distinction between being the author of a work and being only the expounder or transmitter in the two sūtras [['ata Armia (IV. 3, 101)|'तेन प्रोक्तम्' (IV. 3. 101)]] and [['gaypot (IV, 3. 116).|'कृते ग्रन्थे' (IV. 3. 116).]] The [[FTATATEE I. 3,30 Ton Ta '|पूर्वमीमांसासूत्र I. 3. 30 'आख्या प्रवचनात्']] also emphasizes the difference between [[0 and irri.|कृत and प्रोक्त.]]

[[P1204]]About the [[Asvins|Aśvins]] the Nirukta (XII. 1) states several views viz. they are Heaven and Earth, or Day and Night, or the Sun and the Moon and remarks that according to the [[Aitihasikas|Aitihāsikas]] they were kings who had accumulated great [[morit.|merit.]] Probably the Nairuktas were divided among themselves and explained that the [[Asvins|Aśvins]] represented different natural phenomena. About [[Vrtra|Vṛtra]] (occurring in such verses as [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 32. 11) the Nairuktas (Etymologists) said in Nirukta II. 16 that the word means 'cloud', while the [[Aitihasikas|Aitihāsikas]] say that he ([[Vitra|Vṛtra]]) was an [[Agura,|Asura,]] son of [[Tyastr.|Tvaṣṭṛ.]] As to the hymn [[Rg. 1, 105|Ṛg. I. 105]] of 19 verses (in the first 18 of which the refrain is [['vittam me asya rodasi')|'vittam me asya rodasī')]] the Nirukta IV. 6 states that the hymn was seen by Trita who was thrown into a [[well In|well. In]] [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VII. 33. 11 there is mention of [[. Urvasi|Urvaśī]] and Vasiṣṭha ([[Maitrā.Varupa|Maitrā-Varuṇa]]) as born of [[Urvasi|Urvaśī]] and Nirukta (V. 13-14) explains that verse and remarks that [[Urvasi|Urvaśī]] was an apsaras and [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 95 is a dialogue between Aila [[Purū ravas|Purūravas]] and [[Urvash.|Urvaśī.]] But the interpretation of the Nairuktas and [[Aitihasikas|Aitihāsikas]] on this story are not expressly set out. Probably the Nairuktas would explain [[Urvasi|Urvaśī]] as lightning and Purūravas as the roaring wind. The hymn [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 108 is a dialogue between [[Sarama|Saramā]] (Indra's bitch) and the Paṇis and the Nirukta XI. 25 explains the first verse and remarks that there is a story (Ākhyāna) therein viz. that there was a dialogue between Saramā sent by Indra and the Paṇis who were Asuras. In all such legends the Nairuktas would insist that they are to be taken allegorically as referring to natural phenomena, while the Aitihāsikas would insist that there is historical basis in all these. Though it is not stated by the [[Ninukta|Nirukta]] expressly that the [[Aitibāsikas|Aitihāsikas]] do not hold the doctrine of the eternality of the Veda it implicitly follows from their explanations that they did not subscribe to that doctrine. 

2. The connection between word and sense is eternal.[^1955] This is explained by the words of [[Sabara|Śabara]] (in n. 1953) that there is no person who could be put forward as [[baving|having]] brought about 

[^1955]: The sūtra (P. M. [[S,|S.]] I. 1. 5) contains several conclusions. The first is [[Si : (fara:) space sa fapt,|औत्पत्तिकः (नित्यः) शब्दस्य अर्थेन सम्बन्धः,]] the 2nd is [[a THEY|तस्य ज्ञानमुपदेशः]] ([[exbortation|exhortation]] is the means of knowing it i. e. [[dbarma);|dharma);]] [[bere ira|here ज्ञान]] means [[yua ita ( pronat. ilgrei|ज्ञायते येन (श्लोकवा. औत्पत्तिकसूत्र]] verse 9); the next part is [[related set (it is dafailing|अव्यतिरेकश्चार्थेऽनुपलब्धे (it is unfailing]] or infallible as to all that is not perceptible); [[ATATTATHATT i e|तत्प्रमाणमनपेक्षत्वात् i. e.]] Vedic injunction is a valid means of knowledge since it is independent; [[T&TT40774|बादरायणस्य]] (this is the view of [[Badarāyaga|Bādarāyaṇa]] also). The question 'what is a word' is answered differently by [[differeot|different]] writers. The revered [[Upavarsa|Upavarṣa]] said that in a word like 'gauḥ' it is the letters that constitute the word (vide [[Sabara|Śabara]] quoting [[Upavarsa|Upavarṣa]] p. 45 on I. 1. 5 and [[Śhaṅkara on V.S, 1. 3. 28).|Śaṅkara on V. S. I. 3. 28).]] The other view is that the letters manifest the sphoṭa and it is the sphoṭa that conveys the sense. This subject cannot be dealt with in this work.

[[P1205]]the connection of word and sense. Vide [[P. M. 8.|P. M. S.]] I. 1. 6-23, [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] bhāṣya thereon, the elaborate treatment in 444 [[yerses|verses]] in Ślokavārtika (pp. [[728-8'5|728-845]]) and [[Prakarana-pañoikā|Prakaraṇa-pañcikā]] pp. 133-140 (seventh [[Prakarana|Prakaraṇa]]). On the question "what does a word like 'Gauḥ' denote," the [[P. M. 8.|P. M. S.]] replies that a word denotes [['aksti'|'ākṛti']] (or jāti) i. e. the universal, the class. Briefly, the [[Mimārgakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] hold that the word, the denotation and the relation of these two are eternal. Vide [[P. M, S,|P. M. S.]] I. 3. 30-35. 

3. The individual soul. The [[P. M. 8.|P. M. S.]] does not expressly affirm the existence of the soul in any [[sutra.|sūtra.]] [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] on V. S. III. 3. 53 refers to this fact, states that [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] the [[Bhāṣya kāra,|Bhāṣyakāra,]] propounded the existence of the soul and that the venerable [[Upavarga|Upavarṣa]] refrained from going into that question in his [[exposi tion|exposition]] of the [[Pārvamimamga|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] with the remark that he would expound it when dealing with [[Sāriraka|Śārīraka]] (i. e. the [[Vedanta-sūtra|Vedānta-sūtra]]). It was probably due to this fact of the absence of a statement about the soul that the Pūrvamīmāṁsā was interpreted by some as favouring atheism. Kumārila complains that the [[Minārsā,|Mīmāṁsā,]] though not really atheistic, was put forward as Lokāyata (atheistic)[^1956] by some and that therefore he endeavours (in the 

[^1956]: [[Are ore pe fHRT la genteari ariantù a rengat मया॥|प्रायेणैव हि मीमांसा लोके लोकायतीकृता। तामास्तिकपथे कर्तुमयं यत्नः कृतो मया॥]] श्लोकवा. verse 10. The न्या. र. notes that [[भर्पमित्र|भर्तृमित्र]] put forward several wrong doctrines about the Mīmāṁsā such as the one that the performance of obligatory acts or of prohibited acts leads to [[do|no]] desirable or undesirable results. Vide H. of Dh. vol. III. pp. 46-47 note 57 and vol. II. pp. 358-359 on लोकायतिक and नास्तिक. The meaning of लोकायत [[bad|had]] changed from time to time. कौटिल्य (I. 2) included लोकायत under आन्वीक्षिकी along with सांख्ययोग. [[aurora|पाणिनि]] appears to have [[knows|known]] the word [[gefirga,|लौकायतिक;]] his sūtra (IV. 2. [[CD)|60)]] is [['क्रतुक्थादिसूत्रान्ताडक'|'क्रतूकथादिसूत्रान्ताट्ठक्']] and लोकायत is the 2nd word in the [[उक्थादिगण,|उक्थादिगण;]] the [[Kafka|Kāśikā]] on this sūtra mentions [[are cre: ;|लौकायतिकः;]] at least before the 6th century A. D. [[orarefas|लोकायतिक]] had come to be identified with one who did not believe in the existence of a soul independent of the body. The [[area (paragraph 153) basterrafataqdardare:r.|कादम्बरी (paragraph 153) has 'लोकायतिकविद्यामिवोपजातदुर्मरुचेः'.]] [[Farar on harm|शङ्कराचार्य on वेदान्तसूत्र]] III. 3. 54 says that the Lokāyatikas do not admit any principle other than the four elements (earth, water, heat and wind) and do not believe in the existence of a [[soal|soul]] apart from the body. Vide Prof. Das-gupta's 'Indian Philosophy' vol. III, [[P. 512-533|pp. 512-533]] and 'Lokāyata' by Dr. W. Ruben (Berlin 1954). From the Chān. Up. [[VIII, 8|VIII. 8]] it appears that Asura Virocana held that there was no soul apart from the body and that the body alone was the soul. There is a recent book published (while these pages were being sent to the press) viz. 'Lokāyata' (study of ancient Indian materialism) written by Shri. [[Deva. prasad|Devaprasad]] Chattopadhyaya (New [[Delbi,|Delhi,]] 1959), which deals with the subject of Indian materialism in detail.

[[P1206]]Ślokavārtika) to show that it treads the path of belief (in soul and another world). The souls are many, eternal, all-pervading (omnipresent) and different from the body, the senses and the mind. The soul resides in the body, is the agent [[( karl)|(kartṛ)]] as well as [[blɔkir|bhoktṛ]] (enjoyer), is of the nature of [[puro|pure]] consciousness, and is cognised by itself ([[svasamvedya|svasaṁvedya]]). Though the P. M. S. does not expressly deal with the existence of the soul, there are indications that the P. M. S. itself must have impliedly accepted the existence of the soul. The reward of many religious rites is Svarga and the P. M. S. refers to numerous Vedic [[paysages|passages]] where svarga is the reward of rites and assumes that the reward goes to the performer (e. g. in the [[adhiburana|adhikaraṇa]] III. 7. 18-20 beginning with 'Śāstraphalam prayoktari' that expounds the meaning of such vedic passages as 'agnihotram juhuyāt [[svargakāmak').|svargakāmaḥ').]] [[Sabara|Śabara]] on I. 1. 5 expounds at great length the existence of the soul as different from the body, [[sepse3|senses]] &c. (vide particularly [[pp. - 60–72|pp. 60-72]] of Ānan. ed.). The Ślokavārtika [[devotos|devotes]] 148 verses to this subject and the Tantravārtika also briefly deals with it on P. M. S. II. 1. 5 (pp. 402-403). The [[Slokavāritka|Ślokavārtika]] ([[Atmavāda,|Ātmavāda,]] [[Verse|verse]] 148) has this interesting verse [^1957] 'the Bhāṣyakāra ([[Sabara|Śabara]]) desirous of refuting atheism has established here (i. e. in [[bbāṣya|bhāṣya]] passages) by reasoning the existence of the soul; the

(HFFATIE) 148.|इत्याह नास्तिक्यनिराकरिष्णुरात्मास्तितां भाष्यकृदत्र युक्त्या। दृढत्वमेतद्विषयस्य बोधः प्रयाति वेदान्तनिषेवणेन॥ श्लोकवा. (आत्मवाद) 148.]] About the soul [[belog|being]] स्वसंवेद्य, शबर says [[स्वसंवेद्यः स भवति, नासान्येन शक्यते वण्र्यु कथमसौ निर्विश्यतेति । यथा च कश्चिञ्चक्षुष्मान स्वयं रूपं पश्यति न च शक्नोत्यन्यस्मै जात्यन्धाय तनिदर्शयितुम् । न 118 974 Faasiin racoara Tirrigazgat,|'स्वसंवेद्यः स भवति, नासावन्येन शक्यते वर्णयितुं कथमसौ निर्दिश्यतेति । यथा च कश्चिच्चक्षुष्मान् स्वयं रूपं पश्यति न च शक्नोत्यन्यस्मै जात्यन्धाय तन्निदर्शयितुम् । न च तस्य शक्यते निदर्शयितुमित्येतावता नास्तीत्यवगम्यते']] and relies upon [[certaio|certain]] passages of the Bṛ. Up. such as III. 9. 26, IV. 5 [[15 (3fue a RT ), Iv.|15 (अगृह्यो न हि गृह्यते), IV.]] 3. 6 [[(rarEY ज्योतिर्भवति).|(आत्मैवास्य ज्योतिर्भवति).]] As the words आत्मास्तिता and नास्तिक्य are put in juxtaposition in श्लोकवार्तिक it follows that, acc. to कुमारिल, a नास्तिक is primarily one who does not believe in the existence of the soul. [[momnia|पाणिनि]] has the sūtra [['st नारित विष्टं मति:। IV. 4.60 0n which the महाभाग्य explains 'अस्तीस्यस्य मतिरास्तिकः। TENTAFE Argaltiin:', the Finant explains 'Trostatstila 4 HARPET \# i : aggirait aurtean', so armani|'अस्ति नास्ति दिष्टं मतिः' IV. 4. 60 on which the महाभाष्य explains 'अस्तीत्यस्य मतिरास्तिकः। नास्तीत्यस्य मतिर्नास्तिकः', the काशिका explains 'परलोकोऽस्तीति यस्य मतिरस्ति स आस्तिकः तद्विपरीतो नास्तिकः'; so नास्तिक]] primarily means 'one who does not believe in the existence of the soul (and as a consequence in a world other than the physical)'. The [[77971. (pp. 402-404 on II. 1, 5 establishes the following propositions about the soul: तत्र निरयः सखात्मा शरीराभ्यन्तरवती (नाणुमात्रः, न शरीरपरिमितः), सर्वगतः, आत्मनानाले स्वदोषः, सर्वगतस्वास्सिनमारमनी FOI.|तन्त्रवा. (pp. 402-404 on II. 1. 5) establishes the following propositions about the soul: तत्र नित्यः स्वात्मा शरीराभ्यन्तरवर्ती (नाणुमात्रः, न शरीरपरिमितः), सर्वगतः, आत्मनानात्वे स्वदोषः, सर्वगतत्वात्सिद्धमात्मनो निश्चलत्वम् ।]]

[[P1207]]knowledge on this subject (viz. existence of the soul) becomes sound and enduring by the study of Vedānta passages'. The Padma-purāṇa states that Jaimini composed a vast, but useless Śāstra by expounding the view of there being no god.[^1957a] 

4. God and the devatās in sacrifices. [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] position is that the Vedas were not created by God, nor was the connection between word and sense created by Him. The [[Prakararapancikā|Prakaraṇapañcikā]] also (pp. 137 ff) denies [[&|a]] creator for the whole universe. Kumārila's position[^1958] also is peculiar and startling. He says in the Ślokavārtika that it is difficult to prove that God first created the world together with Dharma and Adharma and the means of attaining these, words, [[sepses,|senses,]] and their relations and the Veda. Thus it appears that he does not expressly deny the existence of a supreme power or God but [[simpily|simply]] ignores such power or God. In spite of this he begins his Ślokavārtika with an obeisance to Śiva.[^1959] The Nyāyaratnākara says that the verse can also be an apotheosis of Yajña. But in that case Kumārila would have to be charged with duplicity. It is better to say that the tradition of beginning a work with a [[mangala verso|maṅgala verse]] could not be discarded even by Kumārila. 

The consideration of the question of what is meant by the [[deratā|devatā]] with reference to which an offering is cast into the sacred fire leads to startling results. Jaimini (in VIII. 1. 32-34) takes the position that in a sacrifice the havis (offering) is the principal matter while the devatā is a subordinate [[ono (guna)|one (guṇa)]] and that if there be a conflict between the havis and the devatā, the final conclusion is to be arrived at by relying on the havis. The reasoning is that the Veda connects the deity with the sacrificial act, as in 'one desirous of progeny should offer an havis for Indra and Agni cooked on eleven potsherds and then Indra confers on him progeny' (Tai. S. [[II, 2. 1. 1.|II. 2. 1. 1]]). Even though 

[^1957a]: [[19578. वेदार्थवन्महाशास्त्र मापया यदवैदिकम् । मयैव रक्ष्यते देवि जगतां नाशकारणात् । विजन्मना जैमिनिना पूर्व वेद (चद?)मपार्थकम् । निरीश्वरेण वादेन कृतं शास्त्रं महत्तरम् ॥ Tucu VI, 263, 74-76.|वेदार्थवन्महाशास्त्रं मायया यदवैदिकम् । मयैव रक्ष्यते देवि जगतां नाशकारणात् । द्विजन्मना जैमिनिना पूर्वं वेद (चेद्?)मपार्थकम् । निरीश्वरेण वादेन कृतं शास्त्रं महत्तरम्॥ पद्मपुराण VI. 263. 74-76.]]

(\#Huqo|प्रतीयताम् ॥ श्लोकवा. (सम्बन्धाक्षेप०]] verses 43-45.

The [[भ्या. र.|न्या. र.]] remarks 'सोमस्य [[अर्घ|अर्धं]] स्थानं ग्रहचमसादि [[तदारिणे ETA THIG 17.|तद्धारिणे इति यज्ञपक्षेऽपि सङ्गच्छते ।'.]] [[He says: ASHTeramat... Fragrat a faula i|मनु says : मङ्गलाचारयुक्तानां ... विनिपातो न विद्यते ॥]] IV. 146. 

[[P1208]]it is so, the phala (the reward) is conferred by the sacrifice and not by the deities (Indra and Agni in this case) and the words 'Indra [^1960] and Agni confer progeny on the sacrificer' are purely laudatory. P. M. S. IX. 1. 6-10 are very important in this connection. [[Sabara|Śabara]] quotes Vedic passages like [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 47. 1, III. 30. 5, VIII. 17. 8 (where Indra's right hand, fist, neck, stomach and arms are mentioned), [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 95 10, [[VIIL,|VIII.]] 77. 4 (where Indra is said to have put in his stomach all foods and to have drunk thirty [[yessels|vessels]] full of soma), [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VIII. 32. 22 and X. 89. 10 where Indra is said to be lord of the world, of heaven and earth, waters and [[nountains|mountains]] and then remarks that these are all [[artharūdas,|arthavādas,]] though at first sight they appear to postulate that Vedic deities have bodies, eat and drink. The [[Sastradipika|Śāstradīpikā]] advances the argument that if the devatā had a body, ate, drank and was pleased, devatā will be anitya (ephemeral) and could not in that case be treated of in the Veda that is eternal. He further asserts that men of limited intelligence not knowing the real import of the Vedic passages may hold wrong opinions. [[Sabara|Śabara]] notes (on X. 4. 23) that there are various opinions as to who the [[Dovatās|Devatās]] are to whom either a hymn (sūkta) is addressed (as [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 94) or to whom 'havis' is directed by the Veda to be offered as in 'one should offer to Agni an offering cooked on eight potsherds'; that the devatā becomes connected with the sacrifice not in its own nature but by the word used with reference to an offering and that where the Veda directs an offering to be made to Agni, one cannot use a synonym for Agni such as '[[Suci,|Śuci,]] Pāvaka, [[Dhumaketu,|Dhūmaketu,]] [[Krśanu,|Kṛśānu,]] [[Vaisvānara|Vaiśvānara]] or [[Sāpdilya.|Śāṇḍilya.]] Thus Devatā is a matter of words, as admitted by [^1961] 

[^1960]: Note the following sūtras; [[augait eram fagsta formaggrey cara VIII. 1, 32; Guia T 79TIT The muri fuigua emargfa: 1 IX. 1. 9. 7*78|विप्रतिपत्तौ हविषा नियम्येत कर्मणस्तदुपारूढत्वात्। VIII. 1. 32; अपि वा शब्दपूर्वत्वात् यज्ञकर्म प्रधानं स्याद्गुणत्वे देवताश्रुतिः। IX. 1. 9. शबर's]] words on VIII. 1. 34 are quite frank and clear: स्यादेतदेवं यदि देवतातः फलं स्यात्। यागात्तु फलं स्वर्गकामो यजेतेति श्रूयते। यत्तु 'तृप्त एवैनमिन्द्र' इति [[तवमुच्यते|तन्नेवमुच्यते]] गुणत्वेन देवताश्रुतिः। यागे गुणभूता देवता। तस्या दातृत्वं स्तुत्या उच्यते। यथा [[अमारपेन|अमात्येन]] मे ग्रामो दत्तः, यथा सेनापतिना मे ग्रामो दत्त इति। न चामात्यः सेनापतिर्वा ग्रामस्य प्रभवति। राजैव प्रभवति। इतरस्मिन् गुणभूते स्तुत्या [[दातृववादः।1. I meads|दातृत्ववादः।'. गुणभूत means]] 'that has become a [[subordipate|subordinate]] factor' as opposed to [[guerra,|प्रधान.]] 

[^1961]: तस्मान्मन्त्रत्वे विधिशब्द उपादातव्य [[इति। उस्यते।|इति । उच्यते।]] नन्वेवं शब्द एव देवता प्राप्नोति। अत्रोच्यते। नेतदस्माभिः परिहर्तव्यम्। न [[छीवमुच्यमानमरमत्पक्षं|हीदमुच्यमानमस्मत्पक्षं]] बाधते। शबर on X. 4. 23; [[atngiat daariaega|यागादीनां देवताराधनहेतुत्वे]] प्रमाणाभावात् । ... अतो [[देवतोग्देगेन|देवतोद्देशेन]] [[44TT OTT|द्रव्यत्यागो याग]] इति गौणं [[देवतापूजात्मकत्वमगमयितव्यम्।|देवतापूजात्मकत्वमवगमयितव्यम्।]] प्रकरणपञ्चिका pp. 185-186; [[याप दवता|यद्यपि देवता]] विग्रहवती परिगृह्य भुक्त्वा तृप्यति प्रसीदति च तथापि यागादेव फलं विग्रहवती चानित्या स्यात्। [[aanut|शास्त्रदीपिका]] p. 580 on PMS IX. 1. 6-10.

[[P1209]]Śabara. The [[Prakaranapancikā|Prakaraṇapañcikā]] also states that there is no proof that a sacrifice [[(vāga)|(yāga)]] is a means of pleasing the deity and that to say that there is worship of a deity in a yāga is really metaphorical. From this and the [[proceding|preceding]] doctrine (No. 1) it follows that [[P. M. 8.|P. M. S.]], [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] and Kumārila discard the idea that the Veda is the word of God or that rewards of religious acts are due to the favour or grace of God. It is therefore that the Padmapurāṇa quoted (in note 1957a) above dubs Jaimini's doctrine [['girīśvaravāda'|'nirīśvaravāda']] (Godless). 

When the Veda says 'one desirous of heaven should perform a sacrifice', three ākāṅkṣās (expectancies) arise. The first is 'what is to be brought about'; this is satisfied by the word svarga, stating the reward or the purpose. The 2nd is 'by what means' is the first to be brought about. That is satisfied by the meaning of the root 'yaj' (i. e. by yāga); the third is 'what is the procedure or in what manner'; that is satisfied by the [[eata blishment|establishment]] of the sacred [[firus|fires]] and other rites that are described in the context of the passage 'svargakāmo yajeta'. It is learnt from this passage that the reward or purpose (svarga) springs from (is brought about by) yāga and not by the [[dovatā.|devatā.]] [^1962] 

Later writers could not digest all these ideas about the deities in sacrifices. [[Venkatanātha|Veṅkaṭanātha]] (or [[Venkatadeśika,|Veṅkaṭadeśika,]] 1269–1369 A. D.) composed a work called [['Seśvaramimārsa',|'Seśvaramīmāṁsā',]] in which he [[oriticizes|criticizes]] both [[Bhatta|Bhāṭṭa]] and Prābhākara schools and being an ardent Vaiṣṇava of the [[Ramānuja|Rāmānuja]] school attempts [[&|a]] synthesis of the two [[mimāmgās|mīmāṁsās]] and brings in the idea of God as the dispenser of the reward of sacrifices against the combined testimony of [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] Kumārila, [[Salikanātha|Śālikanātha]] and others. Vide Dr. [[Radha krisnan|Radhakrishnan]] in 'Indian Philosophy,' vol. II pp. 424-429 for 'God and world according to [[Pūryanimāṁsā'.|Pūrvamīmāṁsā'.]] 

5. There is no real creation nor dissolution of the whole universe. The constituent parts may come and go but the universe as a whole has no beginning and no end. The [[descrip tions|descriptions]] of creation and dissolution are only meant to illustrate the power of [[daita|daiva]] (fate, destiny) and the vanity of human effort and urge men to perform duties enjoined by the Veda. Without any human effort the world may come into being and in spite of 

[^1962]: [[यजेतेत्येवमादयः साकाहा पजेत कि केन कथमिति, स्वर्गकाम इत्यनेन प्रयोजनेन for T: 1 Ton II.1.1. Vide great on IX 1. 9 auf degra: quoted in aote 1960.|यजेतेत्येवमादयः साकाङ्क्षा यजेत किं केन कथमिति, स्वर्गकाम इत्यनेन प्रयोजनेन निराकाङ्क्षाः। शबर on II. 1. 1. Vide टुप्टीका on IX. 1. 9 अपि वा...देवताश्रुतिः quoted in note 1960.]]

[[P1210]]all effort it may come to an end. The world is real and has always been there and will go on for all time.[^1963] Vide [[Slokavārtika|Ślokavārtika]] (V. 112-117), [[Prakaranapancikā|Prakaraṇapañcikā]] pp. 137-140 and [[Nyayaratna kara|Nyāyaratnākara]] on the former. The [[Slokavāratika|Ślokavārtika]] goes so far as to say 'it must be admitted that all these (worlds &c.) existed before the creator, and again such a creator may be proved (by anumāna) to have been preceded by the [[Voda|Veda]] on account of his being an intelligent being, just as ourselves are [[praceded|preceded]] by the Veda.' 

It would be noticed that the [[Mimāınsa|Mīmāṁsā]] idea of creation and dissolution is opposed to the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] and [[Gita|Gītā]] (X. 8 [['aham sarvasya prabbaro mattaḥ sarvam pravartate').|'ahaṁ sarvasya prabhavo mattaḥ sarvaṁ pravartate').]] 

6. The doctrine of [[upūria|apūrva]] (invisible mysterious or subtle potency). The Veda lays down that one desirous of heaven should perform a sacrifice. But the reward heaven comes off only after a long time, while the sacrifice lasts only for a short time. There would thus be no direct connection between the sacrifice (the cause) and heaven (the reward) or purpose. It must be presumed from the Vedic injunction that there is some connecting link between the activity of man to perform a yāga and the reward. Before the principal and subordinate [[acta|acts]] in a sacrifice are performed men have no capacity for svarga and sacrifices (described in the [[texta)|texts)]] are also incapable of producing the effect 'svarga'. The principal and subsidiary [[sots|acts]] in a sacrifice when carried out remove the incapacity and produce a certain capacity or potency for svarga. This must be admitted by all. Unless such a capacity exists there would be the [[unac ceptable|unacceptable]] conclusion that not doing the acts and doing them are on the same level. This capacity or potency either [[rosiding|residing]] in the man (agent) or arising from the sacrifice that is performed is called [[apūrvu|apūrva]] in this [[sāstra.|śāstra.]] It is true that this capacity cannot be proved by direct perception or other means of knowledge except by [[śrutārthāpatis.|śrutārthāpatti.]] When we are told that a man 

[^1963]: तस्मादद्यत्वदेवात्र सर्गप्रलयकल्पना। समस्तक्षयजन्मभ्यां न [[सिध्यस्यममाणिका।|सिध्यत्यप्रमाणिका॥]] [[सर्वज्ञवानिषेध्या|सर्वज्ञत्वानिषेध्या]] च [[भ्रष्दुः|स्रष्टुः]] [[सद्भावकल्पना॥...|सद्भावकल्पना॥ ...]] तस्मात् प्रागपि सर्वेऽमी [[सन्दुरासन|स्रष्टुरासन्]] पदादयः। स्यात्तत्पूर्वकता चास्य चैतन्यादस्मदादिवत्॥ एवं ये युक्तिभिः प्राहुस्तेषां दुर्लभमुत्तरम् । [[अन्वेन्यो SYURTTTTH tara: 0 41. (\#TETO|अन्वेष्यो व्यवहारोऽयमनादिर्वेदवादिभिः॥ श्लोकवा. (सम्बन्धाक्षेप०]] verses 113–117). [[90|बुद्ध]] was called सर्वज्ञ, as the अमरकोश states 'सर्वज्ञः सुगतो [[बुद्धो/|बुद्धो']] &c The न्या. र. remarks on verses 113-114 'यथा च बुद्धादेः सर्वज्ञत्वं पुरुषत्वादस्मदादिवन्निषिद्धम्, एवं प्रजापतेरपि स्रष्टृत्वं निषेध्यमित्याह सर्वज्ञवदिति । तेन [[देवप्रभावकथनार्थोय|दैवप्रभावकथनार्थोयं]] [[सुटिपलयवादः।|सृष्टिप्रलयवादः।]] समस्तपुरुषकाराभावेऽपि [[दृष्टिकाले|सृष्टिकाले]] [[दैवषशेनैव|दैववशेनैव]] सर्वं प्रवर्तते, प्रलयकाले च सत्यपि पुरुषकारे [[देवोपरमा देवोपरमति|दैवोपरमाद् देवोपरमति]] तस्माद्धर्मानुष्ठान एव [[यतितापमित्येतत्परं सटिमलपवचनामिति । ग्या. १. 00 श्लोक 9. (Framarquite|यतितव्यमित्येतत्परं सृष्टिप्रलयवचनमिति।' न्या. र. on श्लोकवा. (सम्बन्धाक्षेपपरि०]] verse 112). 

[[P1211]]who is fat does not eat by day we presume, in order to account for this, that he must be eating food at night. Similarly, the Veda brings sacrifice and svarga together; we have to presume that sacrifice gives rise to a subtle potency, though the sacrifice itself comes to an end in a short time, and that this potency is the cause of bringing about the reward svarga and that we may look upon it as abiding in the soul of the sacrificer or as an invisible effect (a reaching forth of the yāga itself that has vanished).[^1964] [[Mimāmsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] do not admit that the results of religious acts are given by God. The [[V. S. III 2. 40|V. S. III. 2. 40]] states that this is the view of Jaimini ([[dharmam Jaiminir-ata eva|dharmaṁ Jaiminir-ata eva]]) and is opposed to the view of [[Bādarayana,|Bādarāyaṇa,]] [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] and the [[Bbāmati|Bhāmatī]] that it is God who confers the reward. According to the [[Prakaranapanoikā|Prakaraṇapañcikā]] ([[p. 186 )|p. 156)]] the invisible force or potency is not in the agent but arises as a subtle form from the action itself. Four kinds of [[Apūrvalos|Apūrva]] [^1965] (with further sub-divisions) as regards Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifice are stated by [[Madhavācārya.|Mādhavācārya.]]

The idea is that every rite as a whole produces an apūrva, and that each subordinate part ([[anga|aṅga]]) of the rite has also an [[(apūrva)|apūrva]] which is subordinate to the apūrva of the whole rite.

The Tantravārtika explains why it is called apūrva. The invisible potency was not in existence before the performance of

[^1964]: [[चोवना|चोदना]] पुनरारम्भः । पू. मी. [[..|सू.]] II. 1. 5; [[चोवनेत्यपूर्व ब्रमः ।|चोदनेत्यपूर्वं ब्रूमः ।]] अपूर्वं पुनरस्ति [[थत|यत]] आरम्भः शिष्यते स्वर्गकामो यजेतेति । इतरथा हि विधानमनर्थकं स्याद्भङ्गित्वाद्यागस्य । [[यद्य न्यवापाच पागो|यद्यन्यदनुत्पाद्य यागो]] विनश्येत् फलमसति निमित्ते न स्यात् । तस्मादुत्पादयतीति । [[शपर,|शबर;]] फलाय विहितं कर्म क्षणिकं चिरभाविने। [[तसिद्धिनान्यथेत्येवमपूर्व|तत्सिद्धिरनान्यथेत्येवमपूर्वं]] प्रति गम्यते ॥ [[वेदवाक्योपात्तस्वर्ग यागादिसम्बन्धाचपपल्या|वेदवाक्योपात्तस्वर्गयागादिसम्बन्धानुपपत्त्या]] [[चोदनयेवापूर्वमपि|चोदनयैवापूर्वमपि]] चोदितं भविष्यतीति प्रमाणवत्। [[तस्मादस्त्यपूर्व मिति।|तस्मादस्त्यपूर्वमिति।]] तन्त्रवार्तिक p. 390; [[प्रधानकर्मणामकर्मणां पा पाकरणात्स्वर्गाविमाप्त्ययोग्याः|प्रधानकर्मणामङ्गकर्मणां वा प्राक्करणात्स्वर्गादिप्राप्त्ययोग्याः]] पुरुषाः [[क्रसवश्च|क्रतवश्च]] स्वर्गकार्यायोग्याः। [[तासुभीमप्ययोग्यता|तासुभयीमप्ययोग्यतां]] व्युदस्य [[प्रधानैरङ्गैच|प्रधानैरङ्गैश्च]] योग्यतोपजन्यत इत्यवश्यं सर्वेणाभ्युपगन्तव्यम् । [[असत्या|असत्यां]] [[तस्यामकृतसमलप्रसङ्गात् ।|तस्यामकृतसमत्वप्रसङ्गात् ।]] सैव च पुरुषगता [[ऋतुगता पा|क्रतुगता वा]] योग्यता [[शानेऽस्मिापूर्वमित्युपदिश्यते|शास्त्रेऽस्मिन्नपूर्वमित्युपदिश्यते]] । [[यत्तुं|यत्तु]] [[प्रत्यक्षादिगम्यसमस्य|प्रत्यक्षादिगम्यत्वमस्य]] नास्तीति [[सत्य,|सत्यं,]] [[शुतार्थापत्ति ज्यतिरिकैर्न|श्रुतार्थापत्तिव्यतिरिक्तैर्न]] गम्यते स [[खदोषः।|स्वदोषः।]] तन्त्रवा. p. 394; In the श्लोकवा. [[(अर्थापतिः)|(अर्थापत्ति०)]] [[Kumarila|Kumārila]] says 'पीनो दिवा न [[भुक्त|भुङ्क्ते]] [[चेत्येवमादिवचःशुतो।|चेत्येवमादिवचःश्रुतौ।]] रात्रिभोजनविज्ञानं [[भुतार्थापत्तिरिष्यते ।|श्रुतार्थापत्तिरिष्यते ॥]] v. 51. तन्त्रवा० further proceeds 'एवं [[स्वर्गादयोप्यङ्कराविस्थानीयां|स्वर्गादयोप्यङ्कुरादिस्थानीयां]] [[ताववपूर्वावस्या|तावदपूर्वावस्थां]] [[प्रतिपचन्ते|प्रतिपद्यन्ते]] तया चोत्पद्यमानया त [[एवोत्यक्षा|एवोत्पन्ना]] भवन्तीति '। तस्मादस्त्यपूर्वम्। [[सुत्रस्यापि|सूत्रस्यापि]] च न [[मकृतोपथिकमन्यमर्थ|प्रकृतौपयिकमन्यमर्थं]] पश्यामः । [[तेनारम्भचोदनवापूर्वस्यापि|तेनारम्भचोदनैवापूर्वस्यापि]] चोदनेत्ययमेवार्थः। तन्त्रवा. p. 400. शङ्कराचार्य [[on.स.|on वे. सू.]] III. 2. 40 puts briefly and clearly [[Jaimiat's|Jaimini's]] view [['श्रुतिधेद प्रमाण पथार्य|'श्रुतिश्चेत् प्रमाणं यथायं]] कर्मफलसम्बन्धः श्रुत उपपद्यते तथा कल्पयितव्यः। न [[चानुत्याय|चानुत्पाद्य]] किमप्यपूर्वं कर्म [[विनश्यकालान्तरितं|विनश्यत्कालान्तरितं]] फलं [[वातुं|दातुं]] शक्नोति। अतः कर्मणो वा [[सुक्ष्मा|सूक्ष्मा]] काचिदुत्तरावस्था फलस्य [[पा|वा]] पूर्वावस्थाऽपूर्वं नामास्तीति [[तयते।, ride|तर्क्यते ।', vide]] भामती on [[वे..III.|वे. सू. III.]] 2. 41.

[^1965]: [[सशापूर्व पर्शपूर्णमासयोरनेकविधम् ।|तच्चापूर्वं दर्शपूर्णमासयोरनेकविधम् ।]] [[फलापूर्वे, ससदायापूर्व, उत्पत्यपूर्व, अड़ा. पर्वति ।|फलापूर्वं, समुदायापूर्वं, उत्पत्त्यपूर्वं, अङ्गापूर्वं चेति।]] [[पेन|येन]] स्वर्ग आरभ्यते तत्फलापूर्वम् । This is [[जै. न्या. मा. वि.|जै. न्या. वि.]] This [[फलापर्व also|फलापूर्वं is also]] called परमापूर्व.

[[P1212]]the sacrifice and arises [[8s|as]] something new after its performance and hence apūrva has a purely etymological [[sepgo.|sense.]] [^1966]]

If one holds the view that apūrva is some potency coming to reside in the performer of a sacrifice one's views would be like those of some modern writers who hold that real prayer is not mere repetition of certain words deemed to be sacred, but it is an upward movement or a real increase in the intensity of spiritual power in the man who prays, it being immaterial to ask to whom he prays (vide W. James' 'Varieties of religious experience' p. 467 ).

7. [[Svatah-prūmānya|Svataḥ-prāmāṇya]] ([[self-Falidity|self-validity]] of cognition). It has already been stated that the [[pramūnas|pramāṇas]] are six (five according to Prabhākara). The P. M. system postulates that all cognitions as cognitions are intrinsically valid in themselves, do not require any extraneous help to establish their validity but [[in validity|invalidity]] of cognitions is ([[parath|parataḥ]]) established extraneously by showing that there was defect in the organ that produced the cognition or it is established later on that a particular cognition was wrong.[^1967] Prabhākara goes much further and holds that every experience as such is valid and no experience can be said to be wrong or illusory.

8. [[Starga.|Svarga.]] The ideas about heaven entertained by Jaimini, [[Sabara|Śabara]] and Kumārila are different from the description of heaven in the Veda and Purāṇas. Vide H. of Dh. vol. IV. pp.

[^1966]: यागादिजनितं च [[पुंसा|पुंसां]] [[फलप्राप्तिसामर्थ्यमपूर्वशरदवाय|फलप्राप्तिसामर्थ्यमपूर्वशब्दवाच्यं]] [[यागाइहानात्पूर्वमभूतम. नहानोत्तरकालं|यागानुष्ठानात्पूर्वमभूतानुष्ठानोत्तरकालं]] चापूर्व जायत इति [[यौगिकत्वादेवापूर्वशग्दाभिधानं|यौगिकत्वादेवापूर्वशब्दाभिधानं]] सर्वत्र [[लम्पते।|लभ्यते।]] [[तन्त्रणा.|तन्त्रवा.]] p [[299|399]] on I. 3. [[30%;|30;]] [[ODY. मी.छ.|on पू. मी. सू.]] VII. 4. 1 [[शयर|शबर]] says [['अवमपि चोकं यजिसपूर्व|'इदमपि चोक्तं यजिरपूर्वं]] साधयति [[ततश्चापूर्वाकालान्तरे|ततश्चापूर्वात्कालान्तरे]] फलं भवतीति चोदना पुनरारम्भ [[इत्यत्र|इत्यत्र'।]]

[^1967]: सर्वं विज्ञानविषयमिदं तावत्प्रतीक्ष्यताम्। [[प्रमाणलापमाणले|प्रमाणत्वाप्रमाणत्वे]] स्वतः किं [[परवोऽधवा।।|परतोऽथवा ॥]] स्वतः सर्वप्रमाणानां प्रामाण्यमिति गम्यताम् । न हि स्वतोऽसती [[शकिः कमन्येन|शक्तिः कर्तुमन्येन]] शक्यते ॥ ...... [[तस्मादोधात्मकत्वेन|तस्माद्बोधात्मकत्वेन]] प्राप्ता [[सुद्धेः|बुद्धेः]] प्रमाणता। [[अर्थाग्यधास्वाहेत्त्यदोषज्ञानादपोपते|अर्थान्यथात्वहेतूत्थदोषज्ञानादपोद्यते]] ॥ [[श्लोकवा (चोदनासत्र)|श्लोकवा० (चोदनासूत्र)]] verses 33, [[47,53;|47, 53;]] the last two verses are respectively quoted by शान्तरक्षित and कमलशील in [[खसंग्रह|तत्त्वसंग्रह]] pp. [[743|745]] and 788. On verse 33 the [[च्या...|न्या. र.]] comments [['तदिहेव|'तदिहैव]] चिन्ता । किं ज्ञानस्य [[प्रामाण्यममामाण्यं|प्रामाण्यमप्रामाण्यं]] च स्वत एव [[निर्णापते ।|निर्णीयते ।]] किं [[पोभ यमपि|चोभयमपि]] कारणगुणदोषादिना निरूपयितव्यम् , [[उतामामाण्यं|उताप्रामाण्यं]] स्वतः [[प्रामाण्ये|प्रामाण्यं]] परतो विपरीतं [[यति।.|वेति।']] Here four possible views are put forward about [[मामाण्य|प्रामाण्य]] and अप्रामाण्य. The [[HATHA|मीमांसक]] view is that [[cogaition|cognition]] is [[FRA:TATOT,|स्वतःप्रमाण,]] while its invalidity has to be established by other means [[(अमामायं| (अप्रामाण्यं]] परतः). [[शपर|शबर]] on I. 1. 5 says [['तस्मायस्य|'तस्माद्यस्य]] च [[दुई|दुष्टं]] करणं [[याच मिध्येति|यत्र च मिथ्येति]] प्रत्ययः स [[पवासमीचीन:|एवासमीचीनः]] [[प्रत्यपो भाग्य इति पत्र|प्रत्ययो नान्य इति।'; 'यत्र]] प्रयत्नेनान्विष्यमाणोऽपि कारणदोषो [[बाधकशानं या|बाधकज्ञानं वा]] नोपलभ्यते तत् प्रमाणम्, [[इवरचा ममाणम् । शानदीपिका|इतरदप्रमाणम्' शास्त्रदीपिका]] on I. 1. 5 p. 50 (of [[Nirm.ed.|Nirn. ed.]] of 1915).

[[P1213]]165-167 and 168-171 for descriptions of the joys of heaven in the Vedic literature, the Epic and the Purāṇas. From [[considera tions|considerations]] of space the remarks will have to be brief. In [[Rg. IX, 113, 7-11|Ṛg. IX. 113. 7-11]] the sage prays to Soma to place him in that immortal world where there is continuous light, where all desires are fulfilled, where there are joys of various grades. Heaven was deemed to be the place where the souls of valiant men that fought battles and sacrificed their lives [[wont|went]] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] VI. 46. 12) and in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X [[154. 2-4|154. 2-5]] the soul of the dead is asked to join those departed men that had become invincible by great tapas, that had died in battle, that had made gifts of a thousand cows, that had led a life of right and virtue and that were wise sages.

The Atharvaveda IV. 34. 2 and 5-6 [[say|says]] that in heaven there are many women, they [[got|get]] edible plants and flowers of various kinds, there are streams of ghee, honey and wine ([[sura),|surā),]] milk, curds and there are lotus ponds all round. The [[Satapatha|Śatapatha]] Br. (XIV. 7. 1. 32–33) states that the joys of heaven are 100 times greater than those on earth. Vide [[Macdonnell's|Macdonell's]] 'Vedic Mythology' pp. 167-168 and A. B. Keith's [['Religion and philo sophy of the Veda'sto.|'Religion and philosophy of the Veda' &c.]] pp. 403-406 (1924). Even the Upaniṣads refer to the joys of heaven e. g. [[Chan.|Chān.]] VIII. 5. 3. speaks of two lakes in the world of Brahmā, aśvattha tree showering down Soma and the city of [[Bṛahmā|Brahmā]] [[calld|called]] Aparājitā; the [[Kausitaki|Kauṣītaki]] Up. (I. 3 and 4) elaborates this and adds that five hundred Apsarases go out to meet him (who reaches heaven), one hundred with garlands in their hands, one hundred with ointments, one hundred with perfumes, one hundred with garments and one hundred with fruits. Poets like Kālidāsa love to dwell upon the instantaneous arrival of the soul of the warrior whose head was cut off in battle and who was at once joined by an apsaras in heaven (vide [[Raghuvamsa|Raghuvaṁśa]] VII. 51 [['Vāmāógasamsaktasuran gunaḥ svam prtyat-kabandham samare dadarś&').|'Vāmāṅgasaṁsaktasuraṅganaḥ svaṁ nṛtyat-kabandhaṁ samare dadarśa').]] The Purāṇas furnish graphic descriptions of the joys of heaven. Vide [[Brahma purāṇa|Brahmapurāṇa]] 225. 6, Padma II. 95. 2-5, Mārkandeya 10. 93-95, which enumerate Nandana garden, aerial cars with bevies of Apsarases, golden beds and seats, absence of sorrows, all pleasure &c. [[Sabara|Śabara]] on P. M. S. VI. 1. 1 refers to two popular views about svarga; one is, whatever gives pleasure to a person such as silken garments of fine texture, sandalwood, damsels sixteen years old is designated [[starga.|svarga.]] Another popular view is that svarga is a certain place where there is no heat nor cold, no hunger nor thirst, no dissatisfaction, no tiredness. 

[[P1214]][[Sabara|Śabara]] and Kumārila say that popular [^1968] ideas about svarga are invalid, that the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] and [[Purīgas,|Purāṇas,]] being composed by men, need not be considered and that the Vedic descriptions of svarga are [[more|mere]] arthavādas for [[praise,|praise.]]

[[___P. M. S.|P. M. S.]] IV. 3. 15. provides that svarga [^1969] is the reward of all religious acts (such as Viśvajit) for which no express reward is provided by the texts. [[Sabara|Śabara]] states 'happiness is svarga and all seek that'; an old [[verze|verse]] says 'that state of happiness in which there is no mixing of pain, which is not immediately overwhelmed by pain, that comes to one when one desires it, is called by the word [[srar|svar]] (svarga)'.

Medhātithi remarks that [[smitis|smṛtis]] sometimes state [^1970] that the gift of a single cow yields all rewards and relieves from sins with the result that fruits of great religious acts and of slight ones would be deemed to be the same but it should be [[under stood|understood]] that the rewards [[diffor|differ]] in their duration; otherwise, no one would engage in great or difficult rites.

Some Vedic rites expressly declare fruits of sacrifices that are other than svarga. For example, the [[Tai, 8.|Tai. S.]] provides one desirous of (plenty of) cattle should offer the [[catrd|citrā]] sacrifice' ([[II. 4.6. 1.|II. 4. 6. 1-2]]) or one desirous of ([[leadership or control over )|leadership or control over)]] a village should offer the [[Isti|iṣṭi]] called [[Sangrahant'|Saṅgrahaṇī']] ([[Tal.|Tai.]] S. II. 3. [[9.2.).|9. 2).]]

[^1968]: ननु च [[लोकादास्यानेभ्यो|लोकादाख्यानेभ्यो]] [[वेदाचावगम्यते|वेदाच्चावगम्यते]] देश एवंजातीयकः स्वर्ग इति। [[तस।|तन्न।]] [[पुरुषाणामेवविधन|पुरुषाणामेवंविधेन]] देशेनासम्बन्धादप्रमाणं वचः। आख्यानमपि पुरुषप्रणीतस्वादनादरणीयम् । [[पैविकमपि|वैदिकमपि]] [[स्वर्गाख्यान|स्वर्गाख्यानं]] विधिपरं नास्त्येव भवति तु [[विष्यन्तरेणैकवाक्यभूतं|विध्यन्तरेणैकवाक्यभूतं]] स्तुतिपरम् । शबर on VI. 1. 1 p. 1348.

[^1969]: स स्वर्गः स्यात्सर्वान्प्रत्यविशिष्टलात्। पू. मी. [[E.|सू.]] IV. 3. 15; शबर says 'सर्वे हि [[पुरुयाः|पुरुषाः]] स्वर्गकामाः कुत एतत् । [[मोतिर्हि|प्रीतिर्हि]] स्वर्गः सर्वश्च [[प्रीति|प्रीतिं]] प्रार्थयते।' [[स्वर्ण|स्वर्ग]] is the [[माग्य|साध्य]] and याग is साधन acc. to दुपटीका on P. M. S. VI. 2. 4; [[यस|यन्न]] दुःखेन [[सम्भि|सम्भिन्नं]] न च [[प्रस्तमनन्तरम्|ग्रस्तमनन्तरम्]] । अभिलाषोपनीतं च तत्सुखं [[स्वापदास्पदम् ॥|स्वःपदास्पदम् ॥]] [[१.|q.]] by [[सांख्यतत्वकोटदी|सांख्यतत्त्वकौमुदी]] of वाचस्पति p. 45 (Chow. edition) and by नीलकण्ठ [[०० उधोगपर्व 33.72.|on उद्योगपर्व 33. 72.]] Some quote this verse as from the विष्णुपुराण. The प्रकरणपञ्चिका (pp. 102-3) appears to [[scho|echo]] the words of the verse 'ज्योतिष्टोमेन स्वर्गकामो यजेतेत्येवमादि समाम्नायं [[सकलदुःखसम्भे दरहिताभिलाषोपनीतदीर्घतरसुखसाधनस्वेनार्थवादः|सकलदुःखसम्भेदरहिताभिलाषोपनीतदीर्घतरसुखसाधनत्वेनार्थवादैः]] स्तूयमानं कर्म [[पश्यते।...तथा|दृश्यते । ... तथा]] च [[यावत्तावा खसाधने|यावत्तावत्सुखसाधने]] स्वर्गशब्दं न [[प्रयुसते|प्रयुञ्जते]] किन्तु [[सातिशयनीतिजनके।|सातिशयप्रीतिजनके ।;]] मेधा० on मनु IV. 87 where narakas are said to be 21 remarks 'नरकशब्दो [[निरतिशय खवचनः।|निरतिशयदुःखवचनः।]] एकविंशतिसंख्या [[अर्थवादः1.|अर्थवादः ।]] The printed विष्णुपुराण [[1.6.46|II. 6. 46]] is: [[मनःमीतिकरः स्वः नरकस्त|मनःप्रीतिकरः स्वर्गो नरकस्त-]] द्विपर्ययः । [[नरकवर्गसंज्ञे|नरकस्वर्गसंज्ञे]] वै पुण्यपापे द्विजोत्तम ॥

[^1970]: स्मृत्यन्तरे [[सफलता|सर्वफलता]] पापमोचनार्थतापि [[गोवानस्य|गोदानस्य]] [[सुता यावतामल्पोपकाराणी|श्रुता यावतामल्पोपकाराणां]] महोपकारैः [[फलसाग्यमुच्यते|फलसाम्यमुच्यते]] तेषां [[लोकपपरिमाणतः फालविशेषोऽवगन्तव्यः|लोकवत्परिमाणतः फलविशेषोऽवगन्तव्यः]] । प्राप्यते तदेव फलं न तु चिरकालम् । [[आवायो|आपाण्यो]] [[यं|ह्ययं]] न्यायः । [[पणलण्यं|पणलभ्यं]] हि [[तमाशाकीणाति|सम्प्राज्ञः क्रीणाति]] दशभिः [[पले-ति|पलैः—इति]] [[समामफलत्वे|समानफलत्वे]] [[महाप्रयासामर्थक्यं मामोति|महाप्रयासानर्थक्यं प्राप्नोति]] । मेधा० on मनु III. 95.

[[P1215]][[Sabara|Śabara]] says the Veda does not say that the result of such sacrifices cannot be had in this very life. This the [[Tupṭikā. 19|Ṭupṭīkā]] [^1971] expands in an interesting note. The remedy declared by the Veda for securing desired objects (son's birth and the like) is bound to yield the result in this life or in the next. If a man has committed evil deeds in a previous life he has to undergo the effects of those [[sing|sins]] and while he is undergoing the effects of sins the fruits of sacrifices are postponed. But if the effects of sins have been extremely reduced [[be|he]] secures the fruits of kāmya rites in this very life. The words of the Veda laying down an injunction state only so much that the fruit of the rite performed does come about but they do not say that the fruit follows immediately ( after the rite is performed). Therefore, there is no definiteness (about the time when the fruit will follow). But svarga is to be enjoyed only in another life (as a fruit of rites performed in this life). It [[(avarga)|(svarga)]] means unequalled [[happin488|happiness]] and is achieved in a degree proportionate to the actions; but it cannot be enjoyed in this life, since men experience in this world every moment both happiness and misery. Every happiness is not the fruit of [[Jyotiṣtoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] and every being does not perform [[Jyotistoma.|Jyotiṣṭoma.]] But some happiness does come to a man. Therefore, it (happiness ) is natural. The existence, however, of another body has to be imagined for the experience of unequalled [[happi ness,|happiness,]] there being no other reasonable explanation. That unequalled happiness does not come to a man unless he dies; therefore svarga is to be enjoyed in another life.

9. [[Mokṣa (liberation):|Mokṣa (liberation).]] Neither the P. M. S. nor [[Sabara|Śabara]] nor Prabhākara deals with the topic of Mokṣa. Kumārila and

[^1971]: [[garath Ahtapoterit fastgai syrga faragha wageni|पुत्रादीनि कामयमानस्योपायो विधीयते। उपाये च कृते नियतमुपेयेन भवितव्यम् ।]] [[तवा|तदा]] पूर्वजन्मन्यशुभं कृतम् । [[तश्चानुभाग्यं|तच्चानुभाव्यं]] तस्मात्पूर्वजन्मकृतमनुभूयते । [[सत्र|तत्र]] यदि जन्मान्तरकृतोऽधर्मः प्रक्षीणस्तत इहैव जन्मनि फलम् । अथाक्षीणस्ततस्तेन [[पद्धसाधकं|बद्धसाधकं]] [[फलमुत्कष्यते|फलमुत्कृष्यते]] । फलं भवतीत्येतावति विधिशब्दोऽस्ति न त्वनन्तरत्वे तस्मादनियमः । स्वर्गस्तु जन्मान्तर [[एवासहि|एव । स हि]] निरतिशया [[भीतिः|प्रीतिः]] कर्मानुरूपा चेति न शक्येह [[जम्मन्यनुभवितुम् ।|जन्मन्यनुभवितुम् ।]] [[यतोऽस्मिलोके क्षणे,क्षणे मुखदुःखे|यतोऽस्मिँल्लोके क्षणे क्षणे सुखदुःखे]] अनुभवन्ति। न च प्रीतिमात्रं ज्योतिष्टोमफलम् । प्राणिमात्रस्य च सा विद्यते न च [[पाणि मात्र|प्राणिमात्रं]] ज्योतिष्टोमं करोति । [[तस्मात्स्वाभाविक्यसो। देहारतरं|तस्मात्स्वाभाविक्यसौ । देहान्तरं]] तु [[निरतिशयपीस्यनुभवनायाग्यथानुप TV4 fecuai a Ty wafirevat paa potile|निरतिशयप्रीत्यनुभवनायान्यथानुपपत्त्या कल्प्यते । तच्चामृतस्य न भवतीत्यतो जन्मान्तरे स्वर्गः। दुपटीका on]] IV. 3. 28. It should be noticed that a [[distintion|distinction]] is drawn here between [[priti ( a momont|prīti (a moment]] of happiness) and niratiśaya-prīti. The [[EcoT|दुपटीका]] on VI. 1. 1 says that the [[siddbānta|siddhānta]] view is that svarga means [['priti|'prīti']] (happiness), while the [[pūrva pakpa|pūrvapakṣa]] holds that svarga signifies the means for things that cause happiness, but both do not hold that svarga is some place [['एकस्य भीतिः|'एकस्य प्रीतिः]] स्वर्गशब्दवाच्या, [[अपरस्य भीतिमद् अयम् ।|अपरस्य प्रीतिमद् द्रव्यम् ।]] विशिष्टो देश [[उभयोरण्यवाग्य:' हुपीका|उभयोरप्यवाच्यः' दुपटीका]] on पू. मी. [[ए.|सू.]] VI. 1. 1 p. 1348.

[[P1216]][[Prakaranapancikā|Prakaraṇapañcikā]] deal with it. They both say that liberation consists in not having to assume a body again.[^1972] The [[śloka vārtika|Ślokavārtika]] states 'one desiring Mokṣa should not do any [[aot|act]] that is forbidden or is [[Kumya|kāmya]] ([[proscribed|prescribed]] for those who desire an object like riches, son &c); he should perform obligatory rites ([[euch|such]] as agnihotra) and naimittika (like bath, japa and gifts on eclipse &c.) in order to avoid the sin that would accrue owing to [[non performance|non-performance]] of those two; if he does not desire the rewards of doing [[pitya|nitya]] and naimittika acts they will not come to him, as such a reward comes to him who seeks it. The results of [[aots|acts]] in a former life would be eliminated by undergoing them in the life in which he seeks liberation. This differs from [[Saṅkarācārya's|Śaṅkarācārya's]] position who holds on V. S. IV. 3. 14 that, as declared in the [[Svet.|Śvet.]] Up. III. 8, there is no other way to [[Moksa|Mokṣa]] except knowledge and realization of the Ātman. In [[bis bhāsys|his bhāṣya]] on the same sūtra he seems to attack [^1973] [[Kumarila's|Kumārila's]] position. According to Kumārila Upaniṣad passages about the knowledge of the Atman are only arthavādas as [[thoy|they]] convey to the performer the knowledge that he has a soul and that the soul has certain characteristics, while [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] maintains (on V. S. I. 1. 1) that the phala, the subject of investigation and the Vedic exhortation (codanā) are different in [[Pūrvamināmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] and in [[Brahamaml māmsā.|Brahmamīmāṁsā.]] There exist [[smrtis|smṛtis]] that ridiculed the idea that the mere

[[(सम्बन्धाक्षेप)|(सम्बन्धाक्षेप०)]] verses 103-104 [['आत्मा ज्ञातव्य हरयतम्मोक्षार्थ न चचोदितम् ।|'आत्मा ज्ञातव्य इत्येतन्मोक्षार्थं न च चोदितम्।]] [[कर्ममवृत्तिहेतुत्वमात्मज्ञानस्य|कर्मप्रवृत्तिहेतुत्वमात्मज्ञानस्य]] लक्ष्यते। विज्ञाते चास्य [[पाराध्य|पारार्थ्ये]] यापि नाम [[फलपतिः।|फलश्रुतिः।]] साऽर्थवादो [[भवेदेवन स्वोदेः|भवेदेव न स्वर्गादेः]] [[फलान्तरम्॥ सुरेश्वर वातिक|फलान्तरम्'॥; सुरेश्वर's वार्तिक]] on तैत्तिरीयोपनिषद्भाष्य [[(शिक्षापाया,| (शिक्षाध्याय I.]] 9-10) quotes the verses मोक्षार्थी न [[पवतेत|प्रवर्तेत]] &c. cited in note 1972 and remarks इति मीमांसकंमन्यैः [[कक्ति|कर्मोक्तं]] मोक्षसाधनम् । 

[[P1217]]knowledge of the Self would lead to Mokṣa. For example, [^1974] the [[Br. Y. Y.|Bṛ. Y. Y.]] affirms that both knowledge and actions lead to [[Liberation,|liberation,]] that to say that knowledge alone leads to liberation is a sign of indolence, that out of the fear of bodily labour ignorant men do not want to engage in actions.

The doctrines of the early and principal writers on [[Pūrya mimāṁsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] are rather quaint and startling. Their arguments about the eternality and self-existence of the Veda are fallacious and were not accepted even by other ancient Indian systems. Both Prabhākara and Kumārila have in their scheme no place for God as the dispenser of rewards or as the ruler of man's destiny, as being pleased with men's prayers. They do not expressly deny the existence of God, but they assign to God or the deities mentioned in the Vedic texts a secondary role or rather [[practi cally|practically]] no role at all. They raise [[yajia|yajña]] to the position of God and their dogmas about yajña seem to be based upon a sort of commercial or business-like system, [^1975] [[viz,|viz.]] one should do so many acts, dispense gifts to priests, offer certain offerings, observe certain ethical rules and other rules of conduct (such as not eating flesh, [[su bsisting|subsisting]] on milk) [^1976] and then the reward would follow without the intervention of God. There is hardly any appeal to religious emotions, there is no omniscient [[Boing,|Being,]] no Creator and no creation of the world. The [[Pūrvam māmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] no doubt lays emphasis on the [[rights and) duties|rights and duties]] of man in life. The other darśanas are far more concerned with the [[ques tion|question]] of escaping permanently from the world and with man's destiny after death. The P. M. S., [[Sabara|Śabara]] and Kumārila, [[how ever,|however,]] make substantial contributions to the exegesis of Vedic

[[P1218]]texts. There are about three thousand quotations in [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] [[bhāsya,|bhāṣya,]] out of which several hundred have not been yet identified. At least one thousand of these are taken from the Tai. S. and Br. About twelve [[adhikarapas|adhikaraṇas]] are concerned with the [[Adhri. gupraiṣa|Adhrigupraiṣa]] (a long formula) [[wbich|which]] is quoted in H. of [[Dh,|Dh.]] vol. II. p. 1121 note 2504. Some [[adhikarapas|adhikaraṇas]] are devoted to the [[explana tion|explanation]] of certain words in that [[praisa|praiṣa]] [[e. g. PMS IX, 4. 22|e. g. P. M. S. IX. 4. 22]] explains urūka as 'vapā', IX. 4. 23-24 explain 'praśasā', [[IX. 4, 25-27|IX. 4. 25-27]] explain five words ([[syena,|śyena,]] [[śalā,|śaśā,]] kaśyapa, kavaṣa, [[srekaparna).|srekaparṇa).]] Similarly, X. 1. 32 explains the word bṛsuya as meaning [['bphat.'|'bṛhat'.]] [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] and Kumārila's stands on the individual soul and [[moksa|mokṣa]] confer on the P. M. system [[soine|some]] status as [[& philoso phical|a philosophical]] one. For a systematic and exhaustive study of [[Sabara's|Śabara's]] contribution to Vedic and [[post-vedic|post-Vedic]] exegesis, vide Dr. S. V. Garge's citations in [['Sābara-bhāsya'|'Śabara-bhāṣya']] pp. 140-213 (Poona, 1952).

The doctrine that the Veda is eternal and of absolute and unquestioned authority has led to certain undesirable tendencies. Propounders of new doctrines make great efforts to show that there is Vedic authority for what they hold. For example, the [[gutras|sūtras]] in V. S. I. 1. 5-18 are concerned to show that the Upaniṣads do not postulate [[Pradhana|Pradhāna]] as the cause of the world, as the Sāṅkhyas imagine. [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] expressly states that the Sāṅkhyas explain Vedānta passages as favourable to their theories and therefore a refutation of their arguments was [^1977] provided in [[V, 8. I. 1. 5-18.|V. S. I. 1. 5-11.]] It has been shown above how followers of [[Sākta|Śākta]] worship try to interpret a verse like [[Rgveda V, 47. 4|Ṛgveda V. 47. 4]] ([[catvāra Im bibhrati &c|catvāra īm bibhrati &c.]]) as referring to the [[Sakta|Śākta]] doctrines and how [[treatisos|treatises]] dignified with the title of Upaniṣads were produced by [[sākta|Śākta]] writers such as the Bhāvanopaniṣad. [[Sabara|Śabara]] in his bhāṣya on [[PMS. 1. 1. 5|P. M. S. I. 1. 5]] states that the Vijñānavādi Bauddhas put forward [[Bṭ. Up.|Bṛ. Up.]] IV. 5. 13 (Vijñānaghana evaitebhyo [[bhūtebbyaḥ|bhūtebhyaḥ]] samutthāya tānyevānuvinaśyati na pretya sanjñāsti) as supporting their position. One of the most glaring examples of this tendency is the explanation of [[Rg. L. 141. 1-3|Ṛg. I. 141. 1-3]] by [[Anandatirtha,|Ānandatīrtha,]] also called [[Madhyācārya,|Madhvācārya,]] who in [['Mahābhārata-tātparya-nirṇaya'|'Mahābhāratatātparyanirṇaya']] claims to be the third [[incarna tion|incarnation]] of [[Vayu,|Vāyu,]] the other two being Hanūmat and [[Bhimasena,|Bhīmasena,]] one

[^1977]: सांख्यादयस्तु [[परिनिष्ठित|परिनिष्ठितं]] वस्तु प्रमाणान्तरगम्यमेवेति मन्यमानाः प्रधानादीनि [[u ma THATATERT Parte tarefa dari TFT on a. I. 1.5;|कारणान्तराण्यनुमिमानास्तत्परतयैव वेदान्तवाक्यानि योजयन्ति। शङ्कर on वे. सू. I. 1. 5;]] सांख्यादयः स्वपक्षस्थापनाय [[वेदान्तवाक्यान्यप्युवाहत्य|वेदान्तवाक्यान्यप्युदाहृत्य]] [[स्वपक्षावगुण्येनेष|स्वपक्षानुगुण्येनैव]] योजयन्तो व्याचक्षते । [[111 on 1 E. II. 2. I.iladhvācārya's claim as avatāra of Vayu|शङ्कर on वे. सू. II. 2. 1.]]

[[P1219]]of the [[Pāṇdaves,|Pāṇḍavas,]] and who endeavours to interpret [[Rg. I. 141; 1-3|Ṛg. I. 141. 1-3]] as referring to these three [[avatāras. 1918|avatāras.]] [^1978] The words [['*Madhvaḥ'|'Madhvaḥ']] and [['Mātariśva'|'Mātariśvā']] (meaning 'wind-god') occur in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 141. 3. This was deemed sufficient to claim that [[Madhya,|Madhva,]] the propounder of the Dvaita system, was mentioned in the [[Rgveda.|Ṛgveda.]] I do not know how Madhvācārya would have met the charge of Veda not being eternal if it referred to [[Bhimasena|Bhīmasena]] ([[who oven'|who even]] according to the traditional account in the Mahābhārata lived at the end of Dvāpara age i. e. about 5000 years ago) and to Madhva himself who lived barely seven centuries ago. The Veda would be later than those dates. The argument that this refers to a previous Kalpa would not be available to rebut the charge, as the Kalpa, Manvantara and Mahāyuga when Bhīma and Madhvācārya flourished and in the present day are the same. There was no pralaya (dissolution of the world) at the end of Dvāpara and Kaliyuga immediately set in. The Bhārata war was fought between Dvāpara and Kali (Ādiparva 2. 13) and Kaliyuga was about to start at the time of that war (vide Vanaparva 'etat kaliyugam nāmācirād-yad pravartate' and Salya 60. 25 (prāptam Kaliyugam viddhi)'. It is on account of this pretention that he was very roughly handled by eminent writers like [[Appayya-dikṣita|Appayyadīkṣita]] who charges [[Madhya|Madhva]] with citing [[fabrioated|fabricated]] Vedic and other texts in support of his doctrines. Vide [[I.. A.|I. A.]] vol. 62 at p. 189 where the writer, Shri Venkatasubbiah, sets out over thirty works mentioned by [[Madhya|Madhva]] which he states occur nowhere else. M. M. [[Chinna swami,|Chinnaswami,]] who has edited (Benares, 1941) Appayya's work in [[60 Verses|60 verses]] with the commentary of Appayya called [[Madhyamata vidhyamsana|Madhvamata-vidhvaṁsana]] and notes of his own, sets out on p. 4 thirty-six unknown works and the sūtras where they are cited by Appayya.

[^1978]: तनुस्तृतीया पवनस्य सेयं सद्भारतार्थप्रतिपादनाय । [[अन्य चकारेममुवीर्णविद्या|ग्रन्थं चकारेममुदीर्णविद्या]] यस्मिन् रमन्ते हरिपादभक्ताः ॥ तृतीयमस्य वृषभस्य दोहसे [[दशममति|दशप्रमतिं]] जनयन्त योषणः । निर्यदीं [[पुग्नोन्महिषस्य पर्पस|बुद्ध्नोन्महिषस्य वर्पस]] ईशानासः शवसा क्रन्त सूरयः। यदीमनु [[मदिवो मध्न|प्रदिवो मध्व]] आधवे गुहा [[सन् मात. रिश्चा|सन्तं मातरिश्वा]] मथायति [[॥|॥ ;]] यस्य त्रीण्युदितानि वेदवचने रूपाणि [[विग्यान्यल बद् तदर्शतमित्यमेव|दिव्यान्यलं यद् तद्दर्शनमित्थमवे]] निहितं देवस्य भर्गो महत् । वायो रामवचोनयं प्रथमकं [[पक्षो|पृक्षो]] द्वितीयं वपुर्मध्वो [[यत्त|यत्तु]] तृतीयमेतदधुना [[ग्रन्थ,|ग्रन्थः]] [[a: are al AETATI TAATSTAF ora|कृतः केशवे ॥ महाभारततात्पर्यनिर्णय]] chap. 32 verses 176-181 [[(Niro,| (Nirn.]] ed. of 1911; [[geta ...thepon:|तृतीय ...योषणः]] form latter half of [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 141. 2 and [[fittat....ergīa|निर्यदीं...मथायति]] is [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 141. 3. [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 141. 1 begins with [[a focu.|बळित्था.]] In the [[offee on the verse ut etc.|भाष्य on ब्रह्मसूत्र the verse यस्य etc.]] quoted here reads the last line as [['AVT Th ban Tarha|'मध्वो यत्तु तृतीयकं कृतमिदं भाष्यं हरौ तेन हि'.]] The celebrated [[Appayyadikṣita|Appayyadīkṣita]] delivered a [[beavy|heavy]] onslaught on [[Madhva cārya|Madhvācārya]] in his [[Away ( Ānan.)|मध्वतन्त्रमुखमर्दन (Ānan.)]] for this claim of [[avatārasbip.|avatāraship.]]
[[P1220]]It is remarkable that great [[acaryas|ācāryas]] like [[Śhaṅkara|Śaṅkara]] and [[Ramanuja|Rāmānuja]] never claimed in their own works that they [[wore|were]] the avatāras of some deity. It was left to their disciples to say so, if at all.

Having established that Veda is eternal and [[self existent|self-existent]] the [[Mimāınsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] give free rein to their ingenuity, powers of [[reason ing|reasoning]] and logic. They have a logic of their own which governed not only the interpretation of Vedic passages but also of the [[Smartis|Smṛtis]] and the medieval works on [[Dharmaśāstra (including|Dharmaśāstra ( including]] law and procedure). As Colebrooke, one of the most accurate and level-headed of Western scholars of Sanskrit, said more than 130 years ago 'the disquisitions on the [[inimāinsā|mīmāṁsā]] bear therefore a certain resemblance to juridical questions; and in fact, the Hindu law being blended with the religion of the people, the same modes of reasoning are applicable and are applied to the [[ono|one]] as to the other. The logic of the [[Mimansa|Mīmāṁsā]] is the logic of the law; the rule of interpretation of civil and religious [[ordinances, Each|ordinances. Each]] case is examined and determined upon and from the cases decided the principles may be collected. A well-ordered [[arrange ment|arrangement]] of them would constitute the philosophy of law and this is in truth what has been attempted in the [[Mimadga'|Mīmāṁsā']] ([[Miscella. neous|Miscellaneous]] Essays, Vol. I. pp. 316-317, Madras [[ed, of 1837).|ed. of 1837).]]

The first division of Vedic material is into Mantra and [[Brāhmana.|Brāhmaṇa.]] It has been already stated above (p. 1096 note 1776) that those are mantras that are recognized as such by the learned. [[P. M. 8.|P. M. S.]] (II. 1. 31-32) provides [^1979] that mantra is that which simply asserts (and is not [[hortatory )|hortatory)]] or (to say the same thing in another way) 'those are mantras that are so called on account of their asserting [[something's|something'.]] On [[P. M. S. I 4. 1 Sabara|P. M. S. I. 4. 1 Śabara]] describes mantra as one that at the time when the procedure of [[&|a]] sacrifice is being followed, recalls or reminds or makes clear to the performer the matter that is prescribed [[0.g.|e. g.]] 'I cut (the blades of) [[kusa|kuśa]] grass that is an abode for the deity'. This is only a general description of mantra and not an accurate [[defini tion.|definition.]] Mantras are not supposed to be useful in sacrifices by

[[..|सू.]] II. 1. 31; शाबरभाष्य 'प्रयोगे क्रियमाणेऽस्य सामर्थ्यं विद्यते गोदानं [[गोयाच|गोयागं च]] प्रत्याययितुं न [[विधातुम् ।|विधातुम्' ;]] [[arenany H EY! %. Át. L. II. 1. 32 ; Tea 'f ira fericidi gotiatetit gratuits Ararat, HATAEYTYTA:, Arant thing fa';|तच्चोदकेषु मन्त्राख्या। पू. मी. सू. II. 1. 32; शाबरभाष्य 'अभिधानस्य चोदकेष्वेवंजातीयकेष्वभियुक्ता उपदिशन्ति मन्त्रानधीमहे, मन्त्रानध्यापयामः, मन्त्रा वर्तन्त इति' ;]] the [[people man|तन्त्रवार्तिक]] dissolves [['aging as a writer area se faca Twitte:|'तच्चोदकेषु' as तत् अभिधानं चोदकं प्रयोजकं येषामिति बहुव्रीहिः।]] [[कश्चिमन्त्रो|कश्चिन्मन्त्रो]] यो विहितमर्थं [[पयोगकाले|प्रयोगकाले]] प्रकाशयति । यथा [[दिवसवन वामि इत्येवमादि। पर|बर्हिर्देवसदनं दामि इत्येवमादिः। शबर]] on [[g. . a. I4,|पू. मी. सू. I. 4. 1.]] [[occurs|बर्हिर्देव० occurs]] in [[A. H.|तै. सं.]] I. 1. 2.

[[P1221]]their mere utterance (uccāraṇa) but are really 'abhidhāyaka' (reminders of what is to be done or is being done). [[Sabara|Śabara]] remarks that lakṣaṇa is the only way for the recognition of mantras and not an enumeration of some characteristics of mantras as done by the Vṛttikāra such as these viz. [^1980] some end in 'asi' (thou art) or 'tvā' as in Tai. S. I. 1. 1 'iṣe tvā', prayer or wish (as in 'āyurdhā asi' in Tai. S. I. 6. 6. 1), praise ('agnir mūrdhā divaḥ' in Tai. S. IV. 4. 4). [[Sabara|Śabara]] points out that 'asi' and 'tvā' occur even in the middle of mantras, the other characteristics such as blessing and praise are found in [[Brāhma - nas|Brāhmaṇas]] also. The M. B. P. remarks that there are (p. 66) one hundred kinds of mantras and that if we take into account the fourteen Vedic metres and their sub-varieties there would be 273 different varieties of ṛk mantras alone (p. 67). There are certain texts regarded as mantras (e. g. 'Vasantāya kapiñjalān-ālabhate' Vāj. S. 24. 20) which are not merely assertive but rather relate to the procedure of a yāga (in the case of Vāj. S. 24. 20, of Aśvamedha).

Mantras are classified under three heads viz. Ṛk, Sāman and Yajus. These are defined in P. M. S. II. 1. 35-37; the name [[șk|ṛk]] is applied to those [^1981] mantras that are divided into [[feat,|feet,]] metrical pādas (often) based on the meaning. Sāman is the name applied to Vedic mantras that are sung. It has been established in P. M. [[S:|S.]] VII. 2. 1-21 and IX. 2. 1-2 that the mantra [^1982] texts are not called Sāman but only the [[musio,|music,]] that

II. 1. 35-37.]] [[lo spalat gant|In अग्निमीळे पुरोहितं]] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 1. 1) the first pāda [[bas|has]] a [[completo sease,|complete sense,]] but in [[so erliertterit on|अग्निः पूर्वेभिरृषिभिरीड्यो नूतनैरुत]] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 1. 2) the meaning is not complete in the first pāda. Therefore, the only definition is [['arreu'|'पादव्यवस्था']] and [[quante|अर्थवशेन]] is only illustrative as [[7|शबर]] says [['ant arustafa ray reu,|'यतो नार्थवशेनेति वृत्ताविवक्षाशङ्कयाऽभ्युच्चयैः,]] किं तर्हि अनुवाद एष प्रदर्शनार्थः। ... [[तस्मात्रपादकता व्यवस्था साकामिति,|तस्मान्नार्थपादकृता व्यवस्था साङ्कामति ']]

[^1982]: [[aurrera: Firmala a tena na pret|तस्माद्गीतयः सामानि न प्रगीतानि मन्त्रवाक्यानि । शबर]] on IX. 2. 2; [[NTA वेदे सहनं|सामवेदे सहस्रं]] [[गीत्युपायाः ।|गीत्युपायाः।]] ... [[गीति म किया।|गीतिर्नाम क्रिया।]] सा [[आम्यन्सरप्रयत्नजमिततरविशेषाणाम.|आभ्यन्तरप्रयत्नजनितस्वरविशेषाणाम-]] [[भिव्यखिका।|भिव्यञ्जिका।]] सा सामशब्दाभिलप्या। सा [[मिथतपरिमाणा।|नियतपरिमाणा।]] [[अधिक|ऋचि च]] गीयते। शबर on पू. मी. [[५. Ix.|सू. IX.]] 2. 29. In the महाभाष्य on the [[vartika|Vārtika]] सर्व देशान्तरे [[In theirat आहिक|in the first आह्निक]] we read चत्वारो [[पेवाः|वेदाः]] साङ्गाः सरहस्या बहुधा [[विभिचा|भिन्ना]] [[पकाशतमचर्यशामा|एकशतमध्वर्युशाखाः]] [[HH4Cl FTA a|सहस्रवर्त्मा सामवेद]] [[genazir quruduit atq:|एकविंशतिधा बाह्वृच्यं नवधाथर्वणो वेदः]] &c. Here the word [[virus|शाखा]] is not used [[ia|in]] relation to [[ATAU|सामवेद]] but [[tho word in|the word वर्त्मन्]] (way). As [[per|शबर]] expressly says that in सामवेद there are a thousand [[गीत्युपायs, सहनवा|गीत्युपायाः, सहस्रवर्त्मा]] means [['महनगील्यु पायवान्।|'सहस्रगीत्युपायवान्']] and it is not correct to render [[सहनवरा|सहस्रवर्त्मा]] as [[सहनशाखः,|सहस्रशाखः,]] as many scholars do. The [[faggamo|विष्णुपुराण]] III. 6 gives a confused account of the [[recessions|recensions]] of the [[Fre,|सामवेद,]] verses 3 and 6 speaking of 1000 [[picars|संहिताs]] propagated by [[grat|सुकर्मा]] and 24 [[Hitats|संहिताs]] propagated by a pupil of [[fegugrana|हिरण्यनाभ]] respectively.

[[P1222]][[giti|gīti]] is an action which manifests various tunes owing to the internal effort made by the singer and in order to bring about the musical effect the singer has to resort to modifications of the letters of the ṛk, to disjunction (of parts of it), to omission (of [[&|a]] letter), to repetition, to pauses, to [^1983] stobha. In [[VII.2.|VII. 2.]] 1-21 the PMS establishes that the words 'Rathantara-sāman', [['Bphat sāman'|'Bṛhat-sāman']] refer only to the music and not to the ṛk or basic text set to music. Yajus means those Vedic mantras that are neither [[k nor sūman.|ṛk nor sāman.]] There is another word 'nigada' applied to certain mantras that are addressed to others as directions to do certain acts and are uttered loudly such as [['agaidagain vihara',|'agnīdagnīn vihara',]] [['proksapīrāsādaya,|'prokṣaṇīrāsādaya',]] 'idhmābarhirupasādaya'. They are Yajus (i. [[e,|e.]] in prose), the only difference being that they are loudly uttered (in order that the persons to whom they are addressed may [[bear|hear]] them), while ordinary Yajus is uttered inaudibly. Vide P. M. S. II. 1. 38-45 for discussion on Nigadas and [[Mai. 8. HII, 6.5|Mai. S. III. 6. 5]] for [['uccair-pcā kriyata uccaiḥ sāmopāmśu yajuṣā'.|'uccair-ṛcā kriyata uccaiḥ sāmopāṁśu yajuṣā'.]]

Mantra and [[Brābmapa|Brāhmaṇa]] constitute the Veda. The [[P.M. S.|P. M. S.]] [^1984]] states that all those parts of the Veda that are not or cannot be called Mantra constitute Brāhmaṇa. [[Sabara|Śabara]] notes that the [[Vettikāra,|Vṛttikāra,]] in order to enable students to decide what texts are Brāhmaṇa texts, pointed out certain characteristics found in Brāhmaṇa texts such as abounding in the word 'iti' or [['ityāba|'ityāha']] (so it is said), [[anegdote,|anecdote,]] stating the reason for an injunction, etymology, condemnation, praise, doubt, injunction, instance where another did something of the [[game|same]] kind, what happened in

[^1983]: [[Stobbas aro|Stobhas are]] musical interjections and [[iourishes|flourishes]] added or introduced in the chants such as 'hāū, [[bai,|hāi,]] [[i, ū, bum'.|ī, ū, hum'.]] Vide Chān. Up. [[(1. 13. 1-3)|(I. 13. 1-3)]] where [[bum|hum]] is said to be the 13th [[stobba|stobha]] and undefinable (identified with highest [[Brabma)|Brahma)]] and the other 12 [[stobhag|stobhas]] are [[specifed|specified]] among [[wbich|which]] are [[FR, ETC, 4, 5 &c.|हाउ, हाइ, ई, ऊ &c.]] Vide Jai. IX. 2. 39 [[(adhikam ca vivargam ca Jaimisob stobbasabdatvāt).|(adhikam ca vivarṇam ca Jaimineḥ stobhaśabdatvāt).]]

[^1984]: [[18 MENUT:|शेषे ब्राह्मणशब्दः।]] पू. मी. [[E.|सू.]] II. 1. 33; [[' म Tigot it मन्त्रलक्षण उक्त परिशेषसिद्धत्वात् बामणलक्षणमवचनीय मन्त्रलक्षणवचनेनेव सिद्धम् । शबर.|'मन्त्राश्च ब्राह्मणं च वेदः । तत्र मन्त्रलक्षण उक्त परिशेषसिद्धत्वाद् ब्राह्मणलक्षणमवचनीयं मन्त्रलक्षणवचनेनैव सिद्धम्' शबर.]]

[[P1223]]past ages, changing the meaning after considering the context. [^1985] [[Sabara|Śabara]] also quotes two verses summarising under ten heads the characteristics of Brāhmaṇa texts, points out that all this is only illustrative and that the characteristics mentioned by the [[Vșttikāra|Vṛttikāra]] sometimes occur in mantras also, such as 'iti' in [[Rg, X. 119. 1,|Ṛg. X. 119. 1,]] [['ityāha' in Rg. VII, 41, 2,|'ityāha' in Ṛg. VII. 41. 2,]] [['akhyāyika' as in Rg. I. 116. 3,|'ākhyāyikā' as in Ṛg. I. 116. 3,]] reason (hetu) in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 2. 4. The [[Rgveda|Ṛgveda]] alone contains over ten thousand mantras. Hardly one-third of them are employed in all the Vedic rites. The rest are employed in japa. Besides, the other Vedas also have thousands of mantras. Therefore, no formal definition of mantra is attempted and all that is stated is that mantras are those which have been recognised as such by the learned. [^1986]

Brāhmaṇas are attached to each Veda, such as the Aitareya and [[Kausitaki|Kauṣītaki]] Brāhmaṇas to the [[Rgveda,|Ṛgveda,]] the [[Taittiriya|Taittirīya]] to the [[Krsna|Kṛṣṇa]] Yajurveda, the [[Satapatha|Śatapatha]] to the [[Sukla|Śukla]] Yajurveda, the [[Tāṇdya|Tāṇḍya]] to the [[Samaveda|Sāmaveda]] and Gopatha to the Yajurveda. The Brāhmaṇas contain the oldest known specimens of [[probe|prose]] in Indo-European languages, though prose formulas (probably earlier in date than those in the Brāhmaṇas) are found in the [[Kṭsṇa|Kṛṣṇa]] and [[Sukla|Śukla]] Yajurveda [[Samhitās.|Saṁhitās.]] They are the primary source of information about sacrifices, ritual and priests. They contain numerous myths and legends put forward for illustrating ritual and sacrifices. They often mention contests between gods and asuras (titans) and abound in etymologies. Their contents can be divided into two broad classes, viz. injunctive [[(hortatory )|(hortatory)]] passages (vidhis) and explanatory (or laudatory) passages [[( arthavādas).|(arthavādas).]] The scope and purpose of [[arthavadas|arthavādas]] will be explained later on. But one important point to be noted is that the [[Mimāṁsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] are strongly opposed to holding that any part of the [[Voda,|Veda,]] even the smallest, is useless or meaningless.

It is now time to see how the [[Mimāmsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] deal with the contents of the [[Voda.|Veda.]] The extant Vedic literature is of

[[P1224]]enormous extent and of greatly varied [[content, Once|content. Once]] it is admitted that the Veda is self-existent and not the work of any author, [[bugan|human]] or divine, every sentence in it apparently becomes of infallible authority. The Veda being the only means of knowing Dharma, the [[Mimimsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] had to accept that whatever the Veda says was authoritative and binding on them, as [[Sabara|Śabara]] [^1987] admits. But many Vedic texts are apparently in conflict with each other and often are in conflict with ordinary experience. A [[fow|few]] striking instances may be given to show how difficulties arose. The Tai. S. V. 2. [[2.7|2. 7]] and Mai. S. state 'one [[ghould|should]] not pile the fire altar on (bare) earth, nor in the sky, nor in heaven'. [^1988] No one can build a fire altar in the sky, nor in [[heaven, The|heaven. The]] Veda apparently prohibits something which is quite impossible and therefore this prohibition seems at first sight senseless. The Tai. Br. (III. 8. [[10.5)|10. 5)]] states that by offering the Pūrṇāhuti the performer secures all desired [[objecte.|objects.]] If [[Pārṇāhuti|Pūrṇāhuti]] yields all objects, then one need not perform any further rites of Agnihotra &c. Does the Veda mean this? The Veda contains stories and legends about individuals e. g. [[Tai, S.|Tai. S.]] mentions Babara [[Prāvāhasi|Prāvāhaṇi]] who desired to be an eloquent speaker and therefore performed [[&|a]] sacrifice called [[Pancarātra|Pañcarātra]] and secured his object. The Veda would then be later in time than this Babara and this would forfeit the claim of being eternal [[&o.|&c.]] Therefore, [[Sabara|Śabara]] states that a story that never happened is described for the sake of praise. This may be an escape from a tight corner but the explanation does not redound
