This is a stuti (laudation) of gold. [[going sa|पूर्णाहुति]] is explained as [[goi9191 argfa:|पूर्णया स्रुचा आहुतिः]] by the [[com, on fireareant. .|com. on कात्यायनश्रौ. सू.]] IV. 10. 5. [[बबरः मावाहणिरकामयत|बबरः प्रावाहणिरकामयत]] वाचः प्रवदिता स्यामिति स एतं [[पश्चरात्रमाहरत्|पञ्चरात्रमाहरत्]] तेनायजत ततो [[वैस|वै स]] वाचः प्रवदिताऽभवत् । य एवं विद्वान् [[पश्चरात्रेण|पञ्चरात्रेण]] यजते [[प्रभवितव वाची|प्रभवितैव वाचो]] भवत्यथो एनं वाचस्पति [[Reg: 1 2). #|रित्याहुः। तै. सं.]] VII. 1. 10. 2-3. [[graeco|प्रावाहणि]] would mean [['son of gargo,|'son of प्रवहण'.]] Vide P. M. S., I. 2. 6 and 18 for [[this,|this. शबर]] remarks [['armaraerat fra मर्शसाया गम्यमानस्वात्!|'असद्भूतान्वाख्यानं स्तुत्यर्थेन प्रशंसायां गम्यमानत्वात्']] on I. 2. 10, on which तन्त्रवा. remarks 'एवं [[वेदेपि|वेदेऽपि]] विधिना [[तापतालमवगमितमर्थवादारवसत्येन|तावत्फलमवगमितमर्थवादैरवसत्येन]] नाम प्ररोचयन्तु न [[तदते|तद्गते]] सत्यासत्यत्वे किञ्चित् [[इपयतः sparare CTC AFISAT&T THEYEPATUTI 74.|दुष्यतः प्रवर्त्तनमात्रोपकारित्वात् । तस्मादुपसंख्यानमसत्यत्वमतन्त्रम्।' तन्त्रवा.]] on I. 2. 10.

[[P1225]]to the credit of the Veda which is here admitted to have manufactured a non-existent story [[fcr|for]] pushing up one of its injunctions; besides, if people come to know from what [[Sabara|Śabara]] says that the story is false, people would not be induced to go in for performing that rite. A true story might encourage persons to follow the prescription of the Veda in this case. The [[Tantravartika|Tantravārtika]] (p. 125) on this explanation of [[Sabara|Śabara]] tries to meet the objection raised here. Sometimes Veda is divided into three portions viz. Vidhi, [[Arthavada|Arthavāda]] and Mantra, names of yāgas like [[udblid|udbhid]] and [[visvajit|viśvajit]] being placed under vidhi. The Ślokavārtika refers to this threefold division in its last verse. [^1989] Though the whole Veda is the only means of right knowledge about what Dharma is i. e. what should be done and what should not be done, the different parts of the Veda are not all directly related to right knowledge about Dharma. Large portions of the Veda are related only mediately to the principal part. [^1990] In one place [[Sabara|Śabara]] very [[succintly|succinctly]] but clearly defines the three classes of Vedic texts and illustrates them. The Veda is also divided into five parts viz. vidhi, [[arthavada,|arthavāda,]] mantra, [[namadheya|nāmadheya]] and [[prati ṣedha.|pratiṣedha.]] These five have already been mentioned above. Here some details will have to be added on each of these. Vidhi is an injunction (or exhortation) that is meaningful on account of enjoining a matter that has [[&|a]] (useful) purpose and it prescribes

[^1989]: इति प्रमाणत्वमिदं प्रसिद्धं [[युक्त्यह|युक्त्यैह]] [[धर्म|धर्मं]] प्रति चोदनायाः। अतःपरं तु विभज्य वेदं त्रेधा ततो वक्ष्यति यस्य [[योर्थः ॥|योऽर्थः ॥]] [[१. in मी. पा. प्र. p. 73;|q. in मी. बा. प्र. p. 7;]] on this न्या. [[.|र.]] remarks [['तेन सिद्धेपि|'तेन सिद्धेऽपि]] [[चोदनापामाण्ये|चोदनाप्रामाण्ये]] [[ततःपर|ततःपरं]] [[विध्यर्थवावमन्त्रात्मना|विध्यर्थवादमन्त्रात्मना]] वेदं त्रेधा विभज्य [[तत्त्यावि|तत्स्मृत्यादि-]] प्रयोजनप्रतिपादनेन कृत्स्नस्य वेदस्य तन्मूलयोश्च [[स्मृत्याचारयोर्धम|स्मृत्याचारयोर्धर्मं]] प्रति [[प्रामाण्यपरितने|प्रामाण्यमुपरितने]] पादत्रये प्रतिपादयिष्यत इति [[समस्तोभ्यायः|समस्तोऽध्यायः]] प्रमाणलक्षणं, नैवेह [[समासमिति".|समाप्तमिति'.]] On the पूर्वपक्षसूत्र [['उक्त समाम्नायैदमध्य|'उक्तं समाम्नायैदमर्थ्यं]] तस्मात् सर्वं [[तदर्थ|तदर्थं]] [[स्थात्'|स्यात्']] ([[पू. मी. छ.|पू. मी. सू.]] I. 4. 1.) शबर states 'कश्चिदस्य (वेदस्य) भागो [[विधियोऽविदितमर्थ|विधियोंऽविदितमर्थं]] वेदयति यथा सोमेन [[बजेतेति ।|यजेतेति ।]] कश्चिदर्थवादो यः [[परोचयन विधि स्तोति|प्ररोचयन् विधिं स्तोति]] यथा [[धायुर्व|वायुर्वे]] क्षेपिष्ठा देवता [[इति।|इति ।]] कश्चिन्मन्त्रो यो [[विहितमथै|विहितमर्थं]] प्रयोगकाले प्रकाशयति यथा [[बहिर्देवसदनं|बर्हिर्देवसदनं]] [[दामि-इत्येवमादिः।|दामि-इत्येवमादिः ।]] [[अयं अर्थ: यस्य सः इवमर्थः तस्य भावः ऐदमर्यम् ।|अयं अर्थः यस्य सः इदमर्थः तस्य भावः ऐदमर्थ्यम् ।]] समाम्नाय means [[वेद, उक्कं|वेद. उक्तं]] refers to [[पू.मी. ए.|पू. मी. सू.]] I. 2. 1 (आम्नायस्य क्रियार्थत्वात् &c).

[^1990]: The [[शानदीपिका|शास्त्रदीपिका]] on I. 4. 1 states [['तत्र चोदनैव साक्षात्प्रमाणम्। अर्थ वादमन्त्रस्मृतिनामधेयानि तशेषत्वेन तन्मूलत्वेन च प्रमाणे भवन्तीति धर्मममितेरितिकर्तग्यता स्थाने नियतं निपतन्ति ।।|'तत्र चोदनैव साक्षात्प्रमाणम् । अर्थवादमन्त्रस्मृतिनामधेयानि तच्छेषत्वेन तन्मूलत्वेन च प्रमाणं भवन्तीति धर्मप्रमितेरितिकर्तव्यतास्थाने नियतं निपतन्ति']] (p. 54). That विधि means something not [[knowa|known]] before or from another source follows from the [[पूर्वपक्षच|पूर्वपक्षसूत्र]] I. 2. 19 [[विधियों स्थावर्षलात्|विधिर्वा स्यादपूर्वत्वात्]] &c. [[OnI.|On I.]] 4. 4 (p. 333) [[शवर|शबर]] says [['अविदितवेदन च विधिरियुज्यते।, अज्ञातस्य|'अविदितवेदनं च विधिरित्युच्यते', अज्ञातस्य]] हि [[ज्ञापन विधिः।|ज्ञापनं विधिः।]] [[शपर|शबर]] on [[x. 3. 20%; On|X. 3. 20; on]] I. 4. 8 p. 339 [['यज्ञातस्ततो विधिः, पदि ज्ञातस्ततोमुवाद:। ... नयाख्यातमन्तरेण कस्य पा नामशब्दार्थण्यापारो विधीयते,|'यज्ञातस्ततो विधिः, यदि ज्ञातस्ततोऽनुवादः । ... न चाख्यातमन्तरेण कस्य वा नामशब्दार्थव्यापारो विधीयते' ;]] this last states that ordinarily a [[vidbi|vidhi]] is laid down by a [[vorbal|verbal]] form or by a [[potontial|potential]] passive participle (ending in [[य, तम्य, or अनीप).|य, तव्य, or अनीय).]]

[[P1226]]something that does not follow (or is not established by) from any other authority. [[Sabara|Śabara]] himself describes in many places what vidhi means. For example, the injunction 'one desirous of (attaining) Heaven should offer Agnihotra' prescribes (the [[per- . formance|performance]] of) [[boma|homa]] that does not follow from any other authority and that has a beneficial purpose, its meaning being 'by the [[Agni. hotra|Agnihotra]] offering one should effect (the attainment of) [[svarga', Where.|svarga'. Where,]] however, a rite is otherwise established, all that can be done is only to enjoin an accessory with regard to it. Thus in the [[sentonce|sentence]] 'one should offer an oblation with curds' the homa being already established by the words 'one should offer Agnihotra (if one desires heaven)' all that is enjoined is the offering of curds with regard to it, the meaning being 'by curds one should effect the oblation'. Vide [[Tup-tikā|Ṭup-ṭīkā]] on P. M. S. VI. 3. 17 and M. N. P. p. 17 ([[B.O. R. I|B. O. R. I]] ed.). [^1991]

The collection of [[vidbis|vidhis]] in the Vedic texts forms the core of the Veda and refers to many specific rites. The central [[olemont|element]] in a vidhi is the verb or verbal form, which will be analysed later on. The question is: how is one to recognize a vidhi. [[Sabara|Śabara]] quotes a verse which those who know the propositions about the meanings of words and sentences declare traditionally, viz. in all the Vedas the fixed (or [[defipite )|definite)]] sign of a vidhi is the words 'one shall do', 'it should be done', 'it ought to be done', 'it should be so', [['it ought to be so!. 192From|'it ought to be so'.]] [^1992] From this it follows that a vidhi is ordinarily couched in the optative form (called [[vidhi ling|vidhiliṅ]] in Sanskrit), and that a verb in the present tense cannot ordinarily be taken [[48|as]] laying down a vidhi. [^1993] But sometimes a vidhi may be inferred even from a text in which the verb is in the present tense. For example, there is a [[vedic|Vedic]] text occurring in the [[Mahāpitryajña|Mahāpitṛyajña]] which states 'he should follow holding the

[^1991]: यत्र तु कर्म प्रकारान्तरेण प्राप्तं तत्र [[तदेशेन|तदुद्देशेन]] गुणमात्रविधानम्। यथा [['दाना|'दध्ना]] [[जुहुयात् । इस्यत्र|जुहुयात्' इत्यत्र]] होमस्य 'अग्निहोत्रं जुहुयात्' इत्यनेन प्राप्तत्वात् [[होमोद्देशेन दधिमात्रषिधानम्,|होमोद्देशेन दधिमात्रविधानम्,]] [[HAT EVH pada hari At. 41. 4. P. 17.|दध्ना होमं भावयेत् इति । मी. न्या. प्र. p. 17.]]

[[P1227]]fuelstick below the handle of the ladle in the [[pitryajña,|pitṛyajña,]] since the performer of rites for the gods holds the fuel-stick above the [[handle. 1991|handle.]] [^1994] This has been construed as a [[vidbi|vidhi]] (exhortation) and not as [[& more arthayāda.|a mere arthavāda.]] Another example is that of Rātrisattras (Soma sacrifices occupying more than twelve [[days ).|days).]] A passage in the context of Rātrisattras states 'those who perform rātrisattra attain stability (or fame), are endowed with brahma [[lustro|lustre]] and eat or possess food'. This looks like a mere praise (arthavāda) of the performance of rātrisattras, but really it is a vidhi about the reward of rātrisattra mentioned in the above passage and states an exception to the rule that svarga is the reward of a rite in which no reward is specified by the [[Vedic. texts.|Vedic texts.]] This is employed in the Mit. on [[Yaj. III, 226|Yāj. III. 226]] which states that the sin committed through ignorance is removed by the performance of penances. Ordinarily, a sinful act is deemed to be destroyed by undergoing the penalty that God may inflict, but [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] states a special rule. Medhātithi on Manu V. 40 (stating that animals, birds and herbs dying by being offered in yajñas reach higher levels) says that this is merely an [[arthavada|arthavāda]] and no vidhi can be imagined from this by the maxim of Rātrisattra. [^1995] Vide [[Par,|Par.]] M. (I. 1. p. [[149 )|149)]] which holds that an

[^1994]: [[विष्टगताग्निहोत्रे|विष्टुगताग्निहोत्रे]] महापितृयज्ञे वा [[भूयते ।|श्रूयते ।]] [[अवस्तारसमिधं धारयमनवेदुपरि हि aaxat untura i|अधस्तात्समिधं धारयन्नन्वेयादुपरि हि देवेभ्यो धारयति ।]] quoted by [[apsat,|तन्त्रवा.]] on III. 4. 6 ([[Sabara|Śabara]] has dropped this [[sutra|sūtra]] along with five preceding ones); it then proceeds [['fe À SETETE Fun समिद्धारयितच्या।'|'पित्रे होमेऽधस्तात् सुग्रुदण्डस्य समिद्धारयितव्या ।']] [[देवे|दैवे]] च [[पुनरूपरिष्टादिति|पुनरुपरिष्टादिति]] । विधित्वे [[चैचमावीमामुक्त|चैवमादीनामायुक्तः]] [[कल्पनामकार।|कल्पनाप्रकारः ।]] [[dentaraita l'|तस्माद्विधिरिति॥']] p. 899. It may be noted that the [[sataa.|स्मृतिच.]] (I. pp. 72–73) mentions this Vedic passage and the conclusion in its discussion about the validity of marriage with one's maternal uncle's daughter or one's paternal aunt's daughter, after quoting [[& Satapatba|a Śatapatha]] text [[I, 8. 3, 6|I. 8. 3. 6]] [['तस्मात्समानादेव पुरुषावत्ता चाथश्च जायते इवं हि चतुर्थे पुरुषे तृतीये संगच्छामहे इति विदेव दीग्यमाना जात्या आसवे|'तस्मात्समानादेव पुरुषादत्ता चाद्यश्च जायेते इदं हि चतुर्थे पुरुषे तृतीये संगच्छामहे इति विदेवं दीव्यमाना जात्या आसते']] (where the verbs are in the present tense and not in the optative mood and yet the [[Sm, C,|Sm. C.]] [[bolds|holds]] that it is not [[a more|a mere]] anuvāda, but it leads to the formulation of a vidhi). Vide also [[qu. At|परा. मा.]] I. 2. pp. 66-67 for a similar treatment. It was held that the use of words like 'hi' indicating a reason or [['val"|'vai']] (indicating what is [[well-known )|well-known)]] is not generally allowed in a vidhi. Vide [[errong, . &. IX. 1, 41 (p. 1690 )|शबर on पू. मी. सू. IX. 1. 41 (p. 1690)]] [['न च विधीयमाने वैशब्दो भवति प्रसिद्धवचनो शेष सधा, मवैश्रेणानि सत्यानि सन्ति-ति 9911'. a &c,|'न च विधीयमाने वैशब्दो भवति प्रसिद्धवचनो ह्येष शब्दः, न वै स्त्रैणानि सख्यानि सन्ति—इति यथा ।'. न वै &c.]] occurs in [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 95. 15.

[^1995]: [[रात्री|रात्रीः]] प्रकृत्य [[थूयते ।|श्रूयते ।]] प्रतितिष्ठन्ति [[हवा|ह वा]] य एता [[रात्रीरुपयन्ति । पर्थविनो HTET Pata u GAT Guiai|रात्रीरुपयन्ति । ब्रह्मवर्चस्विनोऽन्नादा भवन्ति य एता उपयन्ति ।]] The words [[प्रति... an|प्रति...यन्ति]] occur in [[WHITUITIA|ताण्ड्यमहाब्राह्मण]] 23. 2. 4. This is considered in [[q. ft. q.|पू. मी. सू.]] IV. 3. 17-19. The [[Theater holda|दुपटीका holds]] that there is a विधि here. The [[मिता. on Yajnt. 226|मिता० on Yāj. III. 226]] says [['न घेवमर्षवादमात्रम् । अधिकारिविशेषणाकांक्षायो रात्रिसत्रन्यायेनार्थवादिकफलस्पैव कल्पनापा न्याय्यत्वात्। अतो युक्तं प्रायश्चित्रपैत्येन इति।. This अधिकरण|'न चेदमर्थवादमात्रम् । अधिकारिविशेषणाकाङ्क्षायां रात्रिसत्रन्यायेनार्थवादिकफलस्यैव कल्पनाया न्याय्यत्वात्। अतो युक्तं प्रायश्चित्तैरैत्येन इति।' This अधिकरण]] contains an exception to the general rule in पू. मी. सू. IV. 3. 15 'स स्वर्गः स्यात्सर्वान्प्रत्यविशिष्टत्वात्'. About such special passages Śabara remarks 'ननु वर्तमानापदेशोऽयम् । सत्यमेवमेतत् । आनर्थक्यपरिहाराय फलचोदना सम्बन्ध एषितव्यो भवति।' on पू. मी. सू. III. 4. 24 (p. 955) and the तन्त्रवार्तिक on the same states 'सत्यपि चार्थवादसरूपत्वे पारार्थ्यानवधारणादेवंजातीयकेषु फलविधित्वं वक्ष्यति 'फलमात्रेयो निर्देशात्' इति।' फलमा० is the सिद्धान्त in पू. मी. सू. IV. 3. 18 (about रात्रिसत्र).

[[P1228]]adhikāravidhi about one desirous of stability has been assumed from the passage about rātrisattra. Raghunandana in Ekādaśītattva (p. 28) explains the sūtras (P. M. S. IV. 3. 17-19) and illustrates this nyāya.

Following the Vedas, the Smṛtis also lay down numerous vidhis by means of verbal optative forms or participles ending in 'ya, tavya &c.'. For example, vide Manu IV. 25 'agnihotram ca juhuyāt' and XI. 53 ('caritavyam-ato &c.') as instances of the two ways. From different points of view vidhi is classified in various ways. One classification is into four, Utpattividhi (originative injunction), Viniyogavidhi (applicatory), prayoga vidhi (performance), adhikāra-vidhi (eligibility). Utpattividhi is one which shows in a general way the nature of the rite, as in 'agnihotram juhoti' (he offers the agnihotra oblation); an applicatory injunction is one which conveys the connexion of a subsidiary matter with the main rite as in 'dadhnā juhoti' (he offers oblation with curds) and this is dealt with in the 3rd chapter of the P. M. S.; a prayogavidhi is an injunction that governs the order of parts in a rite and suggests non-delay or promptness in the performance, though often times it is implied rather than expressly stated. This is considered in the chapters IV and V of the P. M. S. An Adhikāravidhi (injunction of qualification or eligibility) is one that points to the ownership of the fruit of an action as in 'svargakāmo yajeta' (he who desires to secure heaven should perform a yāga). [^1996] This is the subject of the 6th chapter of P. M. S.

[^1996]: The मी. न्या. प्र. gives the following definitions 'तस्सिद्वं विधिः प्रयोजनवन्तमप्राप्तार्थ विधत्ते । तत्र कर्मस्वरूपमात्रबोधको विधिरुत्पत्तिविधिः, यथा अग्निहोत्रं जुहोतीति । अङ्गप्रधानसम्बन्धबोधको विधिर्विनियोगविधिः, यथा दध्ना जुहोतीति । ... प्रयोगप्रांशुभावबोधको विधिः प्रयोगविधिः । स चाङ्गानां क्रमैकतामापन्नः प्रधानविधिरैव । ... फलस्वाम्यबोधको विधिरधिकारविधिः । फलस्वाम्यं च कर्मजन्यफलभोक्तृत्वम् । स च यजेत स्वर्गकाम इत्येवंरूपः।' pp. 66, 72, 171, 193 (BORI ed. of 1937). The आश्व. गृ. (I. 2. 1) prescribes 'सायं प्रातः सिद्धस्य हविष्यस्य जुहुयात्.' Here जुहुयात् is उत्पत्तिविधि, सिद्धस्य हविष्यस्य would be विनियोगविधि. The अर्थसंग्रह gives another definition of प्रयोगविधि as 'अङ्गानां क्रमबोधको विधिः प्रयोगविधिरित्यपि लक्षणम्' (p 11 text) i. e. prayogavidhi is one that conveys the order of the subsidiaries in a principal rite,

[[P1229]]Another and an important classification is apūrva-vidhi (injunction of something quite [[now|new]] and not otherwise [[establish ed|established]] as in [['gvargakāmo gajeta'),|'svargakāmo yajeta'),]] [[niyamavidbi|niyamavidhi]] ([[(&|a]] restrictive injunction) as in 'he pounds the rice' and [[parisankhyāvidhi|parisaṅkhyāvidhi]] (an injunction in form where two alternatives being [[simul taneously|simultaneously]] possible one is precluded). The Tantravārtika defines these three in a well-known verse.[^1997] For [[&|a]] sacrifice a plot of land is required which may be quite flat or may have ups and downs. Here there are two alternatives only and both cannot be availed of at the same time (i. [[e,|e.]] a man cannot perform a sacrifice at the same time on even as well as on uneven ground). Therefore, [['same dese yajeta'|'same deśe yajeta']] (one should offer a sacrifice on an even spot) is a niyama (it restricts the performance to flat land only) and excludes the use of uneven land for [[&|a]] sacrifice. 'Five five-nailed animals are to be eaten'. This is [[& parisankhyā.|a parisaṅkhyā.]] This sentence is not a vidhi, as eating flesh is already established by men's appetites. Nor is it a niyama, [[sinco|since]] one can eat the flesh of five-nailed animals as well as of other animals at the same time. It is a [[parisankhyā. I, e,|parisaṅkhyā, i. e.]] it prohibits the eating of the flesh of animals other than of five five-nailed animals. In form the sentence is a vidhi (as it employs the word [['bhaksyāḥ'|'bhakṣyāḥ']] (which is optative or potential passive participle), but in substance it is a prohibition of eating the flesh of animals other than the five five-nailed ones. The

[^1997]: विधिरत्यन्त[[मपाते|मप्राप्ते]] नियमः पाक्षिके सति । तत्र चान्यत्र च [[पाते परिसंसपेवि कीर्यते॥|प्राप्ते परिसंख्येति गीयते ॥]] विधिरेव हि केनचिद्विशेषेणैवं भिद्यते । तत्र योऽत्यन्तमप्राप्तो [[नचमाप्स्यति माग्वचनादि स्यवगम्यते|न च प्राप्स्यति प्राग्वचनादित्यवगम्यते]] तत्र नियोगः शुद्ध एव विधिर्यथा [[वीहीन्मोक्षतीति।|व्रीहीन प्रोक्षतीति ।]] यत्र तु [[माग्वचनात् पाक्षिकी माति:|प्राग्वचनात् पाक्षिकी प्राप्तिः]] [[सम्भाग्यते तत्रामाप्तिपक्षं पूरयन्यो|सम्भाव्यते तत्राप्राप्तिपक्षं पूरयन् यो]] विधिः प्रवर्तते स [[नियन्तृत्वालियम इत्युच्यते|नियन्तृत्वात्नियम इत्युच्यते]] यथा [[बीदीन .Entitati 194.|व्रीहीनवहन्तीति । तन्त्रवा.]] on I. 2. 34 p. 152. On P. M. S. VI. 3. 16 the [[gyerent Says|डुपूटिका says]] [['यस कदाचिदपि पामोति|'यन्न कदाचिदपि प्राप्नोति]] स विधिः। यथा, अग्निहोत्रं [[शुहुयात-इति ।|जुहुयात्—इति ।]] [[पूतीका प्रामुवन्ति PATUTE Payard: zelena:1). "Auto|पूतीकाः प्राप्नुवन्ति तस्माद्युक्तमुक्तं नियमार्थः क्वचिद्विधिः ।' 'मेधा०]] on [[HE, III, 45 (ROFII e )|मनु. III. 45 (ऋतुकालाभिगामी०)]] reads प्राप्तौ in both [[balves|halves]] and [[परिसंख्या नविधिव, नखिब्धिव|परिसंख्या नखिविधिवत्. नखिविधि]] refers to the sentence [[पञ्चपञ्चनखा|पञ्च पञ्चनखा]] भक्ष्याः. If these words are taken as परिसंख्या the three [[taults are:|faults are;]] [['पक्ष भक्ष्या इति यदान्धयतः पशनखविषयं भक्षणं प्रतीयते तवा तश्यक्तं भवति, तण्यतिरिक्तनिषेध परत्वाद्वाक्यस्य । अमृतच निषेधः, अतः परार्थकल्पना। अथित्वाच सर्पविषयभक्षणं पत्यासं तस्य ery: 1 HET ON AG. III. 45.|'पञ्च भक्ष्या इति यदाश्रुत्यतः पञ्चनखविषयं भक्षणं प्रतीयते तदा तत्यक्तं भवति, तद्व्यतिरिक्तनिषेधपरत्वाद्वाक्यस्य । अश्रुतश्च निषेधः, अतः परार्थकल्पना । अर्थित्वाच्च सर्वविषयभक्षणं प्राप्त्याप्तं तस्य बाधः ' मेधा on मनु. III. 45.]] The sprinkling of water on rice in a rite is not established by any other means and is therefore a vidhi. Rice can be unhusked with the nails or by being pounded by the use of a mortar and pestle. Therefore [['vrihin avabanti'|'vrīhīn avahanti']] is a niyama, since it does not allow the use of [[Dails|nails]] and puts the restriction of pounding.

[[P1230]]word [[parisankhyā|parisaṅkhyā]] occurs in P. M. [[8|S.]] X. 7. 4 and 7 and [[Sabara|Śabara]] notes that it is liable to three faults.[^1998]

[[Dharmasastra|Dharmaśāstra]] writers make frequent use of the doctrine of niyama and [[parisankhyā.|parisaṅkhyā.]] Medhātithi on Manu III. 45 ([[tuka. lābhigāmi syat|(ṛtukālābhigāmī syāt]]) has a long note on niyama and [[parisankhya,|parisaṅkhyā,]] quotes the verse of Tantravārtika and explains the passage about five five-nailed animals. The Mit. also on [[Yaj. on|Yāj.]] on I. 79 (tasmin yugmāsu [[sainviset|saṁviśet]] 'the husband should approach the wife on every even night next to the 4th night up to the [[¡16th|16th]] after her monthly illness starts) and on I. 81 holds a lengthy discussion whether there is a [[vidbi|vidhi]] or niyama or [[parisankhya.|parisaṅkhyā.]] The Mit. defines the three in prose, cites examples and tells us that some thought that there was only a [[parisankhyā,|parisaṅkhyā,]] while [[Bbāruci|Bhāruci]], Viśvarūpa, and others (including the Mit.) hold that there is a niyamavidhi only in I. 79 and 81. [[Ap. Dh. S. II, 1. 17|Āp. Dh. S. II. 1. 17]] deals with the same subject as Yāj. [[I,|I.]] 79 and 81 and Haradatta says it is a niyama, while others thought it was a [[parisankhyā: and that|parisaṅkhyā and that]] in any case it is not a pure vidhi. On Gautama V. 2 Haradatta comments that the ācārya (i. e. Gautama) holds that there is a [[parisankhyā|parisaṅkhyā]] only (the sūtra is [['garvatra vā pratisiddhavarjam '|'sarvatra vā pratiṣiddhavarjam']]). Compare [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 81 ([[yathākāmi|yathākāmī]] bhaved-vāpi &c.), on which the Mit. emphatically says that in both Gautama and Yāj. there is a [[restriotive|restrictive]] rule (niyama). Gautama prescribes that a brāhmaṇa should eat at the houses of persons of the three higher classes that are commended for the performance of their duties and should receive gifts also from such persons. Haradatta treats these two rules as [^1999][[parisankhyāvidhis,|parisaṅkhyāvidhis.]] [[Ap. Dh. 8. II.|Āp. Dh. S. II. 1. 1. 1-2]] provides the rules of conduct for husband and wife after their marriage, the first being 'taking food two times (morning and [[evening).|evening)'.]] Haradatta takes it as a [[parisankhya,|parisaṅkhyā,]] meaning that eating [[&|a]] third time is prohibited (but they may or may not eat twice in the day), while others take it as a niyama meaning 'they must eat twice in the day'.

[^1998]: On [[g...|पू. मी. सू.]] X. 7. [[5. 1|5.]] [[शबर says '|Śabara says 'न चैतदस्ति परिसंख्येति ।]] [[afa oftai Remot|तत्र हि त्रयो]] दोषाः। स्वार्थपहाणं परार्थकल्पना प्राप्तबाधश्चेति.' [[vide शबर|Vide Śabara]] on III. 7. 33 for the same words. As said in the [[mig til graa: 9at a TFT prufuria gier विषयः|भट्टदीपिका 'शश्वतः फलतो वा यस्य शास्त्रस्यात्यन्तनिवृत्तिविषयः]] स परिसंख्याविधिः। [[तत्र चारपत्रच प्रातिर्न त सापि लक्षणपटिकतिपेयम्।।|तत्र चान्यत्र च प्राप्तिर्न तु सापि लक्षणघटिकेति ध्येयम् ।']] (p. 9. B. I. ed.)

[[P1231]]Niyamavidhis are of three classes, viz. those concerned with pratinidhis (substitutes), those about pratipatti ([[concluding aot|concluding act]] or final disposal of certain materials employed in a sacrifice), and those that are concerned with matters other than the preceding two. The [[Tāpdya-brāhmaṇa|Tāṇḍya-brāhmaṇa]] says [^2000] 'if one is not able to obtain the soma plant, one may extract juice from [[putikas'. Jai, III, 6. 40|pūtīkas'. Jai. III. 6. 40]] and VI. 3. 13-17 deal with this matter and Jai. and [[Sabara|Śabara]] prescribe that if no soma plant is available in a soma sacrifice, the sacrificer should substitute [[putikas|pūtīkas]] for Soma and no other substance, even if the latter [[bo|be]] more similar to Soma, can be used.[^2001] The word pratipatti is used in several sūtras by Jai; vide IV. [[%.|2.]] 11, 15, 19, 22). The casting into water of all utensils and things smeared with [[some|soma]] (such as the [[drogs|dregs]] left after Soma is extracted, the stones, two wooden boards and the post of [[adumbara|udumbara]] in the midst of the sadas) at the time of the final purificatory bath ([[avabhrtha)|avabhṛtha)]] in [[Jyotiṣtoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] is said to be pratipattikarma (P. [[M S.|M. S.]] IV. 2. 22). This terminology is employed in Dharmaśāstra works. Manu (III. 262-263) provides that out of the three [[pindas|piṇḍas]] (balls of boiled rice) that are offered to the three male ancestors of the performer of [[$rāddha|śrāddha]] the wife desirous of a son should eat the middle [[pinda|piṇḍa]] (meant for the paternal grandfather) and Devala provides that the [[piṇdas|piṇḍas]] may be given to a brāhmaṇa or may be eaten by [[&|a]] goat or a cow or cast into fire or water.[^2002] [[(This|This]] is the pratipatti of the piṇdas according to Aparārka (on Yāj. I. 256) and the Sm. C. II p. 486. Vide H. of Dh. vol. IV pp. 480-481. The word pratipatti is opposed to arthakarma. For example, in [[Tai. 8.|Tai. S.]] we read 'after the soma plant is bought, he gives the staff to the [[Maitrāvarupa|Maitrāvaruṇa]] priest'. Here the staff had been first given to the performer (Yajamāna) at the time of [[Diksa|Dīkṣā]] and then transferred to the [[Maitrāvarupa|Maitrāvaruṇa]] who has several [[uges|uses]] for it, viz. he can walk with its help in darkness, may enter water, can prevent cows and snakes from coming near him and he can

[^2000]: [[यदि सोभ न विन्देयुः पूतीकानभिषुणुयुयदि न पूतीकानर्जुनानि च। ताण्डव IX. 5.3.|यदि सोमं न विन्देयुः पूतीकानभिषुणुयुर्यदि न पूतीकानर्जुनानि च । ताण्ड्य IX. 5. 3.]]

HATT À FATH? क्रियते । पूतिका अभिषोतण्या इति । तस्मात्मतिनिधिमुपादाय प्रयोगः कर्तव्य इति।।|नियमार्थों गुणश्रुतिः । पू. मी. सू. III. 6. 40; नियमार्थः क्वचिद्विधिः । पू. मी. सू. VI. 3. 16; on which Śabara comments 'सोमाभावे बहुषु सदृशेषु प्राप्तेषु नियमः क्रियते । पूतिका अभिषोतव्या इति । तस्मात्प्रतिनिधिमुपादाय प्रयोगः कर्तव्य इति ।'.]]

[^2002]: [[L. 2002. ga qefruita Aryar foreggento Tauit: fruft 2437 p. 550, (quotes #ty 16. 52-53 which are similar tom. I. 256).|तेन पत्नीप्राशिते मध्यमपिण्डे पिण्डद्वयस्यापि प्रतिपत्तिः कार्या । अपरार्क p. 550. (quotes मत्स्य 16. 52-53 which are similar to याज्ञ. I. 256).]]

[[P1232]]support himself on it. So this is [[difforent|different]] from pratipatti, where the substance is finally disposed of and no further use is made of it. This is dealt with in P. M. S. IV. 2. 16-18. This (giving of the staff) is arthakarma as opposed to pratipatti-karma. This is mentioned in [[Tai, S. VI. I. 4.2|Tai. S. VI. 1. 4. 2]] ([[krite|krīte]] some [[maitrāvaruṇāya dandam|maitrāvaruṇāya daṇḍaṁ]] prayacchati &c.). Another example of pratipatti is the casting of the horn of the black antelope on the [[cūtvāla|cātvāla]] ([[Tai. S. VI, I. 3.8|Tai. S. VI. 1. 3. 8]] and PMS IV. 2. 19). PMS XI. 2. 66-68 deal with an example of arthakarma. Cremating a sacrificer when dead with his implements of sacrifice (mentioned in Tai. S. I. 6. 8. 2-3) is pratipattikarma of the implements (P. M. S. XI. 3. 34). Manu V. 167 provides that if the wife of an āhitāgni dies before him she is to be cremated with the sacred fires set up by him and with the sacrificial implements. An example of the third kind of niyama (which is not concerned with pratinidhi or pratipatti) is 'one should take [[big|his]] meal,[^2003] facing the east'. A man can take food facing any direction but this restricts him to the east alone. Here no question of a pratinidhi or of pratipatti [[arisons.|arises.]]

Vidhis are also divided into Kratvartha (for the rite) and Puruṣārtha (for the man) They are concerned with 'Prayukti' (motivating force) which is the subject of chapter IV of the P. M. S. P. M. S. IV. 1. 2 defines [^2004] puruṣārtha and [[Sabara|Śabara]] advances three interpretations of that sūtra, one of which is '(Puruṣārtha) is that matter on doing which follows the happiness of man, as its understanding is due to man's desire for obtaining it (happiness) and puruṣārtha (man's purpose) is not different [[(from happiness)',|(from happiness)'.]] From this rather obscure and clumsy definition it appears to follow that puruṣārtha is what a man ordinarily undertakes for securing the reward of happiness, while kratvartha is that which helps in the accomplishment of [[purūṣārtha|puruṣārtha]] and does not itself directly yield any [[toward|reward]] to the performer. All principal [[sacri fices|sacrifices]] like Darśa-pūrṇamāsa are included under puruṣārtha, while under kratvartha are put all those auxiliary acts that have the

[^2003]: [[d etsema aslia-occurs in 314. 9. 6. 1. 11.31. 1.|प्राङ्मुखोऽन्नानि भुञ्जीत—occurs in आप. ध. सू. I. 11. 31. 1.]]

[^2004]: [['Thaitta: gora eu fosse kontserra" . #. #.|'यस्मिन्प्रीतिः पुरुषस्य तस्य लिप्साऽर्थलक्षणाऽविभक्तत्वात्।' पू. मी. सू.]] IV. 1. 2. The meaning of the sūtra as given by [[#WATERT on Tadiftant|मयूखमालिका on शास्त्रदीपिका]] is 'यस्मिन् पदार्थे कृते पुरुषस्य प्रीतिः स्वर्गादिलक्षणोपजायते स [[फारिशपूर्णमासादिः|दर्शपूर्णमासादिः]] पुरुषार्थः [[CAFEY FT Brgeri sesuot gone a semana.|यतस्तस्य लिप्सा अनुष्ठानं अर्थलक्षणं पुरुषार्थस्य प्रीत्या अविभक्तत्वात् ।']]. The words of the sūtra are underlined in this explanation. [[PICHT|लिप्सा]] is a desiderative [[DOUB|noun]] from the root [['labb'(to|'labh' (to]] get) and means [[you .|लब्धुमिच्छा.]] The [[han,|मिता.]] on [[ut.|या.]] II. 114, therefore, calls it [[FOCHTE.|लिप्सासूत्र.]]

[[P1233]]purpose of accomplishing the principal rite itself e. g. the five [^2005] prayājas that are auxiliary to the [[Darśapūrpamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] are [[Kratvar tha,|Kratvartha,]] while [[Darbapūrṇamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] itself is puruṣārtha. The importance of this distinction lies in this that if what is kratvartha is not followed the rite itself becomes defective, while if what is puruṣārtha is not followed, the result is that the man himself becomes blamable or sinful but the undertaking or rite does not become defective. One set of the three interpretations of P. M. [[8.|S.]] IV. 1. [[%|2]] given by [[Sabara|Śabara]] is that it refers to the rules about the acquisition of wealth [[viz,|viz.]] that a brāhmaṇa should earn wealth by receiving gifts, a kṣatriya by conquest and a [[veibya|vaiśya]] by agriculture and the like (vide Gaut. X. 40-42, Manu X. 76-79). These are in the nature of niyamas. If the [[aoquisition|acquisition]] of wealth is kratvartha and, if one acquires wealth by means other than those prescribed by the Śāstra and performs a sacrifice with such wealth, the sacrifice itself would be defective and would not yield the desired reward. But if the acquisition of wealth be puruṣārtha, then, by whatever means wealth with which [[&|a]] yāga is to be performed be obtained, the sacrifice would not be affected. The Mit. on [^2006] [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 114 quotes a passage from Guru Prabhākara (vide note 1947) which is quoted by the Dāyabhāga also (II. 67) but without name, while the Sm. C. II. pp. 257-58, Madanaratna (on Vyavahāra pp. 324-325) and [[Vyavabāraprakāśa|Vyavahāraprakāśa]] (p. 420) quote a similar passage from the Nayaviveka. Viśvarūpa also on Yāj. II. [[144|114]] holds that rules about acquisition of wealth are 'puruṣārtha'. To acquire wealth is natural to man and acquisition of wealth does not depend upon [[Sastra.|Śāstra.]] Besides, every one can directly perceive that wealth when earned confers pleasure on the acquirer. Therefore, wealth is puruṣārtha and sacrifices which are performed with wealth are also puruṣārtha. The general rule is that all [[angas ( auxiliary|aṅgas (auxiliary]] rites) are kratvartha and all principal rites (like

[^2005]: The Tai. S. III. 6. 1. 1 mentions five prayājas (oblations of clarified butter) introductory to the principal offerings of [[Darśapūraamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] viz. [['Samidbo yajatl, tanīgapātam yajati, iờo yajati, barbir-yajati, svābākāram yajati.'|'Samidho yajati, tanūnapātaṁ yajati, iḍo yajati, barhir-yajati, svāhākāraṁ yajati.']] There is a difference of view as to whether these are [[dames|names]] of rites or of devatās. Vide H. of Dh. [[Pol.|Vol.]] II p. 1057 note 2361.

[^2006]: The [[fare|मिता०]] explains '[[wat oerfafarqaratterist agr farmheiter बग्येण ऋतसिद्धिर्न नियमातिकमाजितेन इयेणेति न पुरुषस्य नियमातिकमदोषः पूर्वपक्षे। सिद्धान्ते त्वर्जननियमस्य पुरुषार्थस्वातपतिक्रमेणार्जितेनापि बन्येण क्रसिद्धिर्भवति, पुरुषस्यैष नियमातिक्रमदोष इति नियमाविक्रमार्जितस्यापि स्वत्वमङ्गीकृतम् । अन्यथा क्रसिद्धचभावात् ।।|यदा द्रव्यार्जननियमानां क्रत्वर्थत्वं तदा नियमातिक्रमेणैव द्रव्येण क्रतुसिद्धिने नियमातिक्रमार्जितेन द्रव्येनेति न पुरुषस्य नियमातिक्रमदोषः पूर्वपक्षे । सिद्धान्ते त्वर्जननियमस्य पुरुषार्थत्वादतिक्रमेणार्जितेनापि द्रव्येण क्रतुसिद्धिर्भवति, पुरुषस्यैव नियमातिक्रमदोष इति नियमातिक्रमार्जितस्यापि स्वत्वमङ्गीकृतम् । अन्यथा ऋतुसिद्ध्यभावात् ।']] Intro. to [[TT. II, 114.|या. II. 114.]]

[[P1234]][[Darbapūrpamisa,|Darśapūrṇamāsa,]] [[Somayaga)|Somayāga)]] are puruṣārtha; all texts [[presori bing|prescribing]] the rewards of rites are puruṣārtha. Some individual examples may be given.

In the [[Saṅkhāyana|Śāṅkhāyana]] Br. (VI. 6) after saying that certain observances (vrata) must be followed by the sacrificer, it is provided that he should not see the sun rising or setting. These observances are called 'Prajāpativratāni' by [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] and are declared by him to be puruṣārtha, the meaning being that the sacrificer should make a resolve not to see the rising or setting sun.

This distinction [[betwaon|between]] Kratvartha and Puruṣārtha is often taken over into Dharmaśāstra matters. For example, Yāj. I. 53 provides that one should marry a girl who is free from diseases, has a brother and who does not belong to the same gotra or pravara as that of the bridegroom. The Mit. [^2007] explains that the status of being a wife does not at all arise [[it|if]] the girl is a [[sapinda|sapiṇḍa]] or is of the same gotra or pravara as the bridegroom (i. [[e,|e.]] marriage itself is null and void), but a girl who has some disease when married becomes a wife, the only result being that there is a visible defect (such as a diseased wife is a [[. constant|constant]] source of anxiety and [[unbappiness).|unhappiness).]] Kullūka on Manu [[IL, 7|III. 7]] (providing that one should not marry a girl from a family in which [[provail|prevail]] diseases like phthisis, epilepsy, white and black [[leprosy, refers|leprosy) refers]] to this doctrine of [[Sabara|Śabara]] elaborated above. The Mit. on Yāj. III. 280 quotes a verse 'a wise man should not marry a girl who has no brother, [[por|nor]] father, for fear that she may be a [[putrika|putrikā]] (daughter appointed as a son).' Here the prohibition is like the prohibition of marrying a deformed girl i. e. it is a perceptible purpose. Therefore, the marriage would be valid i. e. the prohibition is puruṣārtha. Manu says (IX. 168) 'that is known to be an adopted son whom the mother or father might give (to another) with water in a time of distress [[&o.'.|&c.'.]] The [[Mit,|Mit.]] on [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] II. 130 quotes this verse and remarks that, as the word 'distress' is specifically mentioned, [[&|a]] son is not to be given in adoption to another when there is no distress and that this prohibition would affect only the giver (but not the act of adoption) i. e. this prohibition would be

[^2007]: [[सपिण्डा-समानगोत्रा-समानप्रवरास भार्यात्वमेव नोत्पते रोगिण्यादिषु है HT41 sa relatie ger faus, on 9.|सपिण्डा-समानगोत्रा-समानप्रवरासु भार्यात्वमेव नोत्पद्यते रोगिण्यादिषु तु भार्यात्वे उत्पन्नेऽपि दृष्टविरोध एव । मिता. on या.]] I. 53; this means that the provision against [[marrylog|marrying]] a [[sapiṇda,|sapiṇḍa,]] sagotra or sapravara girl is [[Yu,|क्रत्वर्थ,]] while that about not marrying a [[discased|diseased]] girl [[to|is]] only [[guard.|पुरुषार्थ.]]

[[P1235]]puruṣārtha and not kratvartha.[^2008] It may be noted that the [[Vyavahāramayūkba|Vyavahāramayūkha]] disagrees and holds that the prohibition is [[kratyartha.|kratvartha.]] It may be said in a general way that those [[prescrip tions|prescriptions]] that have an invisible, spiritual or [[othor-worldly|other-worldly]] result or reward are kratvartha, while those that [[liave|have]] a visible result are puruṣārtha.

Before proceeding further it is necessary to analyse the notions underlying the word 'yajeta' used in such Vedic sentences as 'one desiring heaven should offer [[& sacrifics'|a sacrifice']] (svargakāmo yajeta). In the word 'yajeta'[^2009] there are two parts viz. the root 'yaj' (to sacrifice) and the termination (of injunctive third person singular). In the termination also there are two parts [[(elements ) viz,| (elements) viz.]] general verbal nature and injunctiveness (or injunctive force). General verbal nature is found in the ten [[lakūras|lakāras]] (verbal [[formg in ton|forms in ten]] moods and tenses), but injunctiveness is found only in the injunctive form. Both general verbal nature and injunctiveness express only bhāvanā (force that brings into being something, efficient force). Bhāvanā (lit.) means a particular operation or activity of an agent which conduces to the coming into being of the result ([[Lit.|lit.]] which is to come into being). And this bhāvanā is of two [[kirds, sābdi ( word force) and arthi|kinds, śābdī (word force) and ārthī]] (result-producing force).

It has been already stated that [[vidhia|vidhis]] are the very core of the Veda. The doctrine of [[bhāvana|bhāvanā]] is the very heart of vidhis and is therefore one of the most important doctrines of the [[mimamsa.|mīmāṁsā.]]

In ordinary life when a person says to another 'this should be done by you', the prompting to do something comes from a person. But Veda has according to the [[Mimarisi|Mīmāṁsā]] no author, human or divine. Therefore, in a Vedic vidhi the [[promp ting|prompting]] arises from the injunctive or the optative form of the word; there is no person, human [[of|or]] divine, behind the [[injunotion;|injunction;]] hence the bhāvanā is said to be [[sābdi|śābdī]] (i. e. based on the word itself and not on the will or direction of a person). There-

[^2009]: [[June pierderea, a forras 84981 tea sunt ETE, भाख्यातत्व लिकत्वं च। आख्यातत्वं च दश लकारेषु विद्यते। लिस्वंयपुनः केवल लिम्वेव। वत्रालयातत्वलित्वाम्यां भावनेवीच्यते। भावना नाम भवितुर्भवमालकलो भावकन्यापारविशेषः। faint air , mut at Muft.|यथाहि यजेतेत्यत्रास्त्यंशद्वयम्, यजिर्धातुः प्रत्ययश्च । तत्र प्रत्ययेऽप्यस्त्यंशद्वयम्, आख्यातत्वं लिङ्गत्वं च। आख्यातत्वं च दशसु लकारेषु विद्यते। लिङ्गत्वं पुनः केवलं लिङ्येव। तत्राख्यातत्वलिङ्गत्वभ्यां भावनेवोच्यते । भावना नाम भवितुर्भवनानुकूलो भावकव्यापारविशेषः। सा च द्विविधा शाब्दी भावना, आर्थी भावना चेति ।]] [[91. 9.|मी. न्या. प्र.]] pp. 4-6 (BORI ed.).

[[P1236]]fore, [[sabdi-bhavanā|śābdī-bhāvanā]][^2011] is defined as a particular operation (or [[aotivity|activity]]) of an agent (here the word of the [[Voda)|Veda)]] that conduces to the activity of a man; and it is expressed by that part or element which is optative. It is called [['śābdi'|'śābdī']] because it is [[* sabdaniṣtha|śabdaniṣṭha]] (centred in the word of the Veda) and not [[puruṣaniṣtha|puruṣaniṣṭha]] (centred in a person). In a [[śābdibhāvanā|śābdībhāvanā]] there are three elements viz. (1) there is prompting of the agent to activity, (2) the injunction is the instrument and (3) the manner is furnished by the desirableness proclaimed by [[artha vāda|arthavāda]] passages. The [[sābdi-bhāvanā|śābdī-bhāvanā]] gives rise to [[arthi-bhiyanā.|ārthī-bhāvanā.]] In an [[arthi-bhāvanā|ārthī-bhāvanā]] (that aims at or seeks the artha, the phala) also there are three elements viz. (1) svarga is the phala that is to be brought about, (2) the means or instrument is [[yaga,|yāga,]] (3) there is also the procedure or method (itikartavyatā) of the yāga. All this is based on the P. M. S. sūtra II. 1. 1, on [[Sabara's bhāsya|Śabara's bhāṣya]] and certain important verses of the Tantravārtika quoted below. This whole discussion leads on to the idea of [[apūrra.|apūrva.]] Yāga lasts for a short time, while [[avarga|svarga]] comes to a man after death, which may be years after the performance of the sacrifice. What is the connecting link between yāga and svarga (the cause and the result)? It is the force or potency generated by [[yāgs|yāga]] that brings about svarga.

Briefly, the idea is; both the root and the affix together express the sense of the affix and as the bhāvanā is the [[prinopal|principal]] element in this, it is held to be the sense of the affix. There are numerous [^2010] 'bhāvaśabdas' such as yajati, juhoti, [[dadati,|dadāti,]]

[^2010]: [[Tauf: FAṣaretra: A parte qui fai g. fi. &|भावार्थाः कर्मशब्दास्तेभ्यः क्रिया प्रतीयेतैष ह्यर्थो विधीयते । पू. मी. सू.]] II. 1. 1; in the [[मयुखमालिका o शानदीपिका|मयूखमालिका on शास्त्रदीपिका]] this is explained as follows: [[भावार्थाः भावना. प्रयोजनका ये|भावार्थाः भावनाप्रयोजनकाः ये]] [[कर्मशग्दा:|कर्मशब्दाः]] धातवस्तेभ्योऽपूर्व प्रतीयेत एष हि धात्वर्थः [[पवत्या|पद्धत्या]] भावनाकरणत्वेन विधीयते. [[कर्मशग्दा:|कर्मशब्दाः]] means कर्मप्रतिपादकाः. कः पुनर्भावः के ते पुनर्भावशब्दा इति । [[पजति|यजति]] ददाति जुहोत्येवमादयः ।...यजेतेत्येवमादयः [[साकारक्षा पनेत कि केन कमिति वर्गकाम इत्येवेन geraaa fa71:1 4t (q. above ja pote 1962);|साकाङ्क्षा यजेत किं केन कथमिति स्वर्गकाम इत्येनेन प्रयोजनेन निराकाङ्क्षाः । शबर (q. above in note 1962);]] [[sifa TATE PATHE|अभिधाभावनामाहुरन्यामेव]] [[लिहावयः।|लिङादयः ।]] अर्थात्मभावना [[स्वच्या|त्वन्या]] सर्वाख्यातेषु गम्यते । तन्त्रवा. p. 378; [[शाचे सर्वत्र|शास्त्रे तु सर्वत्र]] प्रत्ययार्थों भावनेति व्यवहारः । तत्रायमभिप्रायः । प्रत्ययार्थ सह [[अतः|ब्रूतः]] प्रकृतिप्रत्ययौ [[सवा। *741 Fra da qeyrisauna # 77*.|सदा । प्राधान्याद्भावना तेन प्रत्ययार्थोंऽवधार्यते ॥ तन्त्रवा.]] p. 380. The [[HET #704 OD 2 OR|महाभाष्य on वार्तिक 2 on]] पा. III. 1. 67 has the maxim [[प्रकृतिप्रत्ययो|प्रकृतिप्रत्ययौ]] प्रत्ययार्थ सह [[पत:|ब्रूतः]] and [[शबर|Śabara]] refers to it [[ad arr o|as आचार्योपदेश]] on III. 4. 13 p. 922. Pāṇini coins special [[tecbolcal dames|technical names]] for tenses and moods and does not employ words expressive of meaning, present, past or future. They begin with [[and therefore are called TT.|ल and therefore are called लकाराः.]] They [[Are:|are:]] [[J (TATA,|लट् (वर्तमान,]] present); [[(Vedic subjunctive ), Pose (atent for,|लेट् (Vedic subjunctive), लिट् (परोक्षे लिङ्,]] perfect), [[UF (Aorist ), MF ( n Imperfect), for (optative, . III. 3. 161, 173), e (blessing or prayer, 97. Ill. 1. 173), 2 (distant fatore (A|लुङ् (Aorist), लङ् (अनद्यतनभूत Imperfect), लिङ् (optative, पा. III. 3. 161, 173), आशीर्लिङ् (blessing or prayer, पा. III. 1. 173), लुट् (distant future in]] ता), [[लद|लृट्]] (future in स्य), [[लक्|लृङ्]] (conditional). The words भावार्थाः कर्मशब्दाः echo the निरुक्त I. 1 भावप्रधानमाख्यातम्.

[[P1237]]dogdhi, [[pinasti.|pinaṣṭi.]] These are of two sorts, viz. [[pradhana|pradhāna]] (principal) and [[gunabhūta,|guṇabhūta.]] [^2012] Those by which a substance is not intended to be produced or embellished (or made fit) for a religious rite, are expressive of principal karma (such as [[prayājas ),|prayājas),]] while those which are meant to produce a substance or to make a substance fit are [[gunabhuła|guṇabhūta]] (such as pounding rice grains or chiselling a piece of wood for making a sacrificial post, wiping [[stuc|sruc]] ladle). Therefore the latter kind of actions are called [[gunakarma.|guṇakarma.]] There are two kinds of verbal forms (1) those in which the form only conveys that the kartā (doer of the action) exists such as the forms 'asti, bhavati, vidyate'; (2) there are others where not only there is the existence of the kartā but there is also the understanding of some activity that produces a result such as 'yajati' (meaning [['yagam karoti'),|'yāgaṁ karoti'),]] [[' dadati'|'dadāti']] (i. e. [[dānam|dānaṁ]] karoti), 'pacati' ([[pakam|pākaṁ]] karoti), 'gacchati' i. e. [[gamanam|gamanaṁ]] karoti). That is, in such cases the sense of karoti is also understood. Jaimini (in P. M. S. II. 1. 4) divides words into two classes viz. [[Namāni|Nāmāni]] (nouns) and [[Karmasabdoh|Karmaśabdāḥ]] (verbs). Under the [[firat Sabara|first Śabara]] would include pronouns and adjectives. The 2nd is called 'ākhyāta'. [[Sabara|Śabara]] (on II. 1. [[3.) paraphrases (namani|3) paraphrases nāmāni]] as [['dravya-gunasabdah'|'dravya-guṇaśabdāḥ']] and remarks that in the [[sutra|sūtra]] (II. 1. 3) the word 'nāmāni' is employed in the sense of [['dravyagunasabdah ' . Sabara|'dravyaguṇaśabdāḥ'. Śabara]] [^2013] remarks that [[dhatvartha|dhātvartha]] (the

[[P1238]]meaning of a verbal root) [[oan|can]] have no [[akānkṣa|ākāṅkṣā]] (expectancy) for Dharmas but it is the pratyayārtha that has [[akāñksā|ākāṅkṣā]] for the procedure (itikartavyatā).

We must now turn to the 2nd great division of Vedic [[texto,|texts,]] viz. Arthavādas. They are dealt with in P. M. S. first adhyāya, [[ind|2nd]] pāda. There are numerous [[Vodic|Vedic]] passages such as 'he roared; because he roared he came to be called Rudra' ([[Tai. $.|Tai. S.]] I. 5. 1. 1.), 'Prajāpati cut out his own fat' (Tai. S. [[II,|II.]] 1. 1. 4), 'the gods, having reached the sacrificial ground, did not know the directions' (Tai. S. VI. 1. 5. 1.); 'who knows whether one lives in the other world [[cr|or]] not' [[(Tai. S. VI. I. 1. 1.);|'(Tai. S. VI. 1. 1. 1.);]] [['thore|'there]] should be no piling of fire altar on the earth nor in the sky nor in heaven' (Tai. S. [[V,|V.]] 2. 7. 1). The objector says 'you have yourself declared that enjoining religious rites is the purpose of the Veda' (P. M. S. I. 1. 2). The above and similar passages do not serve any purpose in the matter of religious acts, they are therefore [[uselegg|useless]] and are non-eternal (or do not refer to any permanent matter). The reply is that these passages form one syntactical or connected whole with hortatory passages of the Veda and they [[serye|serve]] the purpose of commending (or glorifying) the hortatory passages. [[Sabara|Śabara]] cites on I. 2. 7 the passage 'one desiring prosperity [[gbould|should]] sacrifice a white animal in honour of Vāyu; Vāyu indeed is the swiftest deity; he runs near Vāyu with the share proper (to Vāyu); he (Vāyu) leads the sacrificer to prosperity'. [^2014] These words form one whole passage; the first

[^2014]: [[2014. आम्नायस्य क्रियार्थत्वादानर्थक्यमतदर्थानां तस्मादनित्यमुच्यते (पूर्वपक्ष) । विधिना त्वेकवाक्यत्वात्स्तुत्यर्थेन विधीनां स्युः । पू. मी. सू. I. 2. 1 and 7. The passage quoted on I. 2. 7 is 'वायव्यं श्वेतमालभेत भूतिकामः । वायुर्वे क्षेपिष्ठा देवता वायुमेव स्वेन भागधेयेनोपधावति । स एवैनं भूतिं गमयति।' This अर्थवाद (वायुर्वे क्षेपिष्ठा देवता) is an auxiliary (शेष) to the विधि 'वायव्यं ... लभेत' etc.; this occurs in तै. सं. II. 1. 1. 1. The भाष्य on I. 2. 10 (गुणवादस्तु) points out the passages of which the three passages cited under I. 2. 1 are अर्थवादs. For example, the passage 'सोऽरोदीद्यदरोदीत्-|आम्नायस्य क्रियार्थत्वादानर्थक्यमतदर्थानां तस्मादनित्यमुच्यते (पूर्वपक्ष) । विधिना त्वेकवाक्यत्वात्स्तुत्यर्थेन विधीनां स्युः । पू. मी. सू. I. 2. 1 and 7. The passage quoted on I. 2. 7 is 'वायव्यं श्वेतमालभेत भूतिकामः । वायुर्वे क्षेपिष्ठा देवता वायुमेव स्वेन भागधेयेनोपधावति । स एवैनं भूतिं गमयति।' This अर्थवाद (वायुर्वे क्षेपिष्ठा देवता) is an auxiliary (शेष) to the विधि 'वायव्यं ... लभेत' etc.; this occurs in तै. सं. II. 1. 1. 1. The भाष्य on I. 2. 10 (गुणवादस्तु) points out the passages of which the three passages cited under I. 2. 1 are अर्थवादs. For example, the passage 'सोऽरोदीद्यदरोदीत्-]] तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वम्' is an अर्थवाद of 'बर्हिषि रजतं न देयम्' । तै. सं. I. 5. 1. 1-2. This arthavāda (सोऽरोदीत् etc.) is an auxiliary (शेष) to the प्रतिषेध 'बर्हिषि रजतं न देयम्. The word अनित्य in the sūtra is purposely used. Veda is nitya and therefore pramāṇa. Hence the passages not referring to any religious rite are different from the portion dealing with rites and anitya i. e. apramāṇa (not authoritative). Vide p. 927 note 1482 about calling पुराणs as merely arthavādas.

[[P1239]]part [['vāyavyam..bhūtikāmaḥ'|'vāyavyaṁ...bhūtikāmaḥ']] is clearly a vidhi, as the word 'ālabheta (in the optative form) shows. The latter portion is an arthavāda for the purpose of commendation. People are familiar with the fact that Vāyu is swift. Therefore, the latter portion (Vāyurvai &c.) merely repeats what is already well known (that is, it is an anuvāda). In sūtras 19-25 of I. 2 the P. M. S. considers certain passages that look like vidhis but are declared to be arthavādas. For example, (Tai S. II. 1. 1. 6) 'the sacrificial post is to be of udumbara wood; the udumbara wood is indeed vigour (or food or sap); cattle are vigour; by this strong (or juicy) post, he (sacrificer) secures cattle, for 'the securing of strength'. The objector says that this is a phalavidhi (an injunctive text about a result) as the words 'ūrjo'varuddhyai' contain the purpose and there is no word of commendation. The reply is that there is only commendation.

There are some passages in the Veda in which occur words like 'hi' (because) such as in 'one should offer into fire the oblation with the winnowing basket, because food is prepared by means of it' (Tai. Br. I. 6. [[5 ).|5).]] [^2015] The question that arises is

[^2015]: [[2015. हेतुर्वा स्यादर्थवत्त्वोपपत्तिभ्याम् । स्तुतिस्तु शब्दपूर्वत्वादचोदना च तस्य । पू. मी. सू. I. 2. 26-27 ; अथ ये हेतुवन्निगदाः शूर्पेण जुहोति तेन ह्यन्नं क्रियत इत्येवमादयः । तेषु सन्देहः । किं स्तुतिस्तेषां कार्यमुत हेतुरिति । अस्मत्पक्षे पुनः शूर्प स्तूयते । तेन ह्यन्नं क्रियत इति वृत्तान्तान्वाख्यानं न च वृत्तान्तज्ञापनाय किं तर्हि प्ररोचनायैव । तस्माद्धेतुवन्निगदस्यापि स्तुतिरेव कार्यमिति । शबर on I. 2. 30. शूर्प was used like a juhu ladle to make a homa of Karambhāpātras (pots full of husked grains of yava slightly fried, ground and mixed with curds) in the Varuṇapraghāsa, one of the cāturmāsyas. The position of the P. M. S. is that what Veda declares is authoritative; there is no need to cite reasons for the statements of the Veda. It may cite a reason for its declaration, even though no necessity exists. As stated by the Bhāṭṭacintāmaṇi "अनेन वेदविहितेऽर्थे हेत्वपेक्षा नास्तीति पार्थसारथिप्रतिपादितानपेक्षत्वं हेतुवादस्येति सूचितम् । उक्तं च 'न हि वेदेनोच्यमानं हेतुमपेक्षते' इति ॥". Vide for a detailed discussion of this हेतुवन्निगदाधिकरण in connection with the rule of Vasiṣṭha 15. 3-4 (na tvekam putram dadyāt pratigṛhṇīyādvā, sa hi santānāya pūrveṣām) in H. of Dh. vol. III. pp. 676-77 n, 1277. Here the first is a vidhi, 'dadyāt' and 'pratigṛhṇīyāt' being in the optative mood,|हेतुर्वा स्यादर्थवत्त्वोपपत्तिभ्याम् । स्तुतिस्तु शब्दपूर्वत्वादचोदना च तस्य । पू. मी. सू. I. 2. 26-27 ; अथ ये हेतुवन्निगदाः शूर्पेण जुहोति तेन ह्यन्नं क्रियत इत्येवमादयः । तेषु सन्देहः । किं स्तुतिस्तेषां कार्यमुत हेतुरिति । अस्मत्पक्षे पुनः शूर्पं स्तूयते । तेन ह्यन्नं क्रियत इति वृत्तान्तान्वाख्यानं न च वृत्तान्तज्ञापनाय किं तर्हि प्ररोचनायैव । तस्माद्धेतुवन्निगदस्यापि स्तुतिरेव कार्यमिति । शबर on I. 2. 30. शूर्प was used like a juhu ladle to make a homa of Karambhāpātras (pots full of husked grains of yava slightly fried, ground and mixed with curds) in the Varuṇapraghāsa, one of the cāturmāsyas. The position of the P. M. S. is that what Veda declares is authoritative; there is no need to cite reasons for the statements of the Veda. It may cite a reason for its declaration, even though no necessity exists. As stated by the Bhāṭṭacintāmaṇi "अनेन वेदविहितेऽर्थे हेत्वपेक्षा नास्तीति पार्थसारथिप्रतिपादितानपेक्षत्वं हेतुवादस्येति सूचितम् । उक्तं च 'न हि वेदेनोच्यमानं हेतुमपेक्षते' इति ॥". Vide for a detailed discussion of this हेतुवन्निगदाधिकरण in connection with the rule of Vasiṣṭha 15. 3-4 (na tvekam putram dadyāt pratigṛhṇīyādvā, sa hi santānāya pūrveṣām) in H. of Dh. vol. III. pp. 676-77 n, 1277. Here the first is a vidhi, 'dadyāt' and 'pratigṛhṇīyāt' being in the optative mood,]] while the latter part containing a reason (in [['sa hi &c.') is on artbavāda ,|'sa hi &c.') is an arthavāda,]] lauding the importance of a son). This topic of [[Hetuvan-nigadādhikarana|Hetuvan-nigadādhikaraṇa]] figured very much in the case of Beni Prasad vs. Hardai Bibi I. L. R. 14 All 67 (F. B.). The court was misled on two points, by counsel as to the first and by Mandlik's wrong statement (in 'Hindu law' p. 499) as to the 2nd. viz. that Jaimini flourished in the 13th century A. D. and secondly (p. 73, 125) 'it is a rule of the [[Pūrvamimāmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] that all texts supported by the [[assigniing|assigning]] of a reason are to be deemed not as vidhis but simply as arthavādas [[(recommendatory )'.|(recommendatory)'.]] This case went up to the Privy Council as Radha Mohan vs. Hardai Bibi [[L.R.|L. R.]] 26 I. A. p. 153, where the Privy Council was more cautious and did not accept in its entirety the wrong statement of Mandlik.

[[P1240]]whether this and other passages containing words denoting reason should be construed as arthavādas commending what is enjoined in the preceding sentence or whether as stating a reason for the injunction. The established conclusion is that they are commendatory. If the other view were adopted (viz. that śruti gives a reason for the vidhi) then it may be argued that the ladle, pan and other utensils also may be used for making an offering (not merely the [[sūrpa ),|śūrpa),]] as they also are required in preparing food. Raghunandana in Malamāsatattva p. 760 relies upon and explains this maxim with reference to Laghu-Hārīta's words 'cakravat parivarteta sūryaḥ kālavaśād yataḥ'. It should not be supposed that commendation (stuti) is the purpose of all arthavādas. In 'he lays down anointed pebbles, ghee is indeed brilliance' (Tai. Br. III. 2. 5. 12) a doubt arises about the material with which pebbles are to be anointed. That doubt is removed by the remainder of the passage that it is ghee with which they are to be anointed (P. M. S. I. 4. 24)

Arthavādas are of three kinds viz. [[gunavāda,|guṇavāda,]] anuvāda and bhūtārthavāda; 'when an arthavāda text is in conflict with ordinary experience it is metaphorical; when something is definitely ascertained by other means of knowledge and becomes the subject of a text it is called anuvāda and when a text is not opposed to other pramāṇas or is not definitely ascertained otherwise, it is called 'bhūtārthavāda' (statement of an accomplished fact, or of a past event); in this way arthavāda is of three kinds'. [^2016] An example of the first kind is 'during day-time

[^2016]: [[a 1. 16 speaks of cigaigu12: Ĥ para fata gurete: marec resuntai mangarajai a: # q. by A. 97. 4. D. 48; e बादोऽवधारित इत्यत्योदाहरणं तु सिंहाश्रमरुक्तम् । अनिहिमस्य भेषजम्-इति । तत्स्वाण्याचा|निरुक्त 1. 16 speaks of उदितानुवादः स भवति. विरोधे गुणवादः स्यादनुवादोऽवधारिते । भूतार्थवादस्तद्धानादर्थवादस्त्रिधा मतः ॥ q. by मी. न्या. प्र. p. 48; अनुवादाऽवधारित इत्यस्योदाहरणं तु वृत्तिसहामश्रैरुक्तम् । अग्निर्हिमस्य भेषजम्—इति । तस्याप्यपाषा-]] [[1999huiza Tuent Ef. T. 4. . 48.|ध्यायन्तैर्धुर्याद् वैचित्त्याद्वा । मी. न्या. प्र. p. 48.]] It may be noted that [[Ayar et lo qfUTAG, 30e|मधुसूदन-सरस्वती in प्रस्थानभेद, अर्थसंग्रह]] (p. 26, [[Tbibaat)|Thibaut)]] and [[M. M, Jba|M. M. Jha]] cited this very text as an [[anovada|anuvāda]] (in 'Pūrva-Mīmāṁsā in its sources' p 201). A strict definition of [[Tere|अनुवाद]] is [['स नामानुवादो भवति योऽत्यन्तसमानार्थत्वेनावधार्यते ।' (तन्त्रवा० p. 911 on II, 4. 13).|'स नामानुवादो भवति योऽत्यन्तसमानार्थत्वेनावधार्यते ।' (तन्त्रवा० p. 911 on II. 4. 13).]] Medhātithi on Manu II. 227 (=Matsya 211. 22) saying that a man cannot requite even in a hundred years the parents for the troubles they undergo in the birth and upbringing of the child holds that it is a [[young eta.|भूताथौनुवाद.]] [[H aradis io mette and TUFAE|मधुसूदनसरस्वती in प्रस्थानभेद and अर्थसंग्रह]] (p. 26) cites [[et 14 वज्रमुवयच्छत्|इन्द्रो वृत्राय वज्रमुदयच्छत्]] as an example of भूतार्थवाद, and अर्थसंग्रह defines it as [['प्रमाणान्तर e -HITAKIETU are artara:'.|'प्रमाणान्तरविरोध-तत्प्राप्तिरहितार्थबोधको वादो भूतार्थवादः '.]] When the Tai. S. [[1.|I.]] 7. 4. 4. or II. 6. 5. 3 says [['yajamāpaḥ prastarah'|'yajamānaḥ prastaraḥ']] or [['yajamānah yūpaḥ'|'yajamānaḥ yūpaḥ']] the literal meaning is opposed to our perception (there is pratyakṣavirodha), therefore the sentence has to be understood in a metaphorical sense' (as when a boy is called 'fire') and is therefore a [[gunavāda,|गुणवाद,]] that is, the meaning in 'yajamānaḥ yūpaḥ' is that he stands up (or erect) like a sacrificial post and looks brilliant. Where a statement (not being a vidhi) is neither an anuvāda nor a guṇavāda it is called vidyamānavāda or bhūtārthavāda. This is lucidly explained by [[Sabara|Śabara]] in P. M. S. I. 4. 23 and by [[Saṅkarācārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] on Vedāntasūtra I. 3. 33. The first handful of darbha grass cut is called 'prastara'. These passages have to be interpreted in [[sucb & way|such a way]] as to avoid [[coniict|conflict]] with perception and other pramāṇas but also as intended to convey praise (of some vidhi already expressed). Vide भामती 'न च आदित्यो वै यूप इति वाक्यमादित्यस्य यूपत्वप्रतिपादनपरम्, अपि तु यूपस्तुतिपरम्'. The गुण stressed is तेजस्विता (brilliance) since the post is smeared with घृत.

[[P1241]]only the smoke of fire is [[geen|seen]] and not its flame' (this is Tai. Br. II. 1. 2. 10). One sees both smoke and fire during the day and night. What this text desires to emphasize is that by day the light of fire is not so well seen as at night (or not well seen from a distance by day as at night).

'Fire is the medicine [[(antidote )|(antidote)]] for cold' ([[Vaj.|Vāj.]] S. 23. 10 and Tai. S. VII. 4. 18. 2) is cited by some as an example of anuvāda. The M. B. P. finds fault with this on the ground that it is well-known as a mantra and does not form part of a vidhi by syntactical relation, and that it is cited by [[Nṛsimhāśrama|Nṛsiṁhāśrama]] as an example owing to neglect of the study of the Veda or absent-mindedness. A proper example would be 'Vāyu indeed is the swiftest deity' cited above. 'Prajāpati cut off his own fat' is cited by some as an example of bhūtārthavāda, but the M. B. P. does not approve of it and cites the verse 'yan-na duḥkhena sambhinnam' (cited above in note 1969) as an example.

The [[Mimāinsāparibhāṣā|Mīmāṁsāparibhāṣā]] of [[Krspayajvan|Kṛṣṇayajvan]] classifies [[Arthavā. das|Arthavādas]] into four kinds, viz. nindā (censure), stuti (praise), parakṛti (performance by another great person) and purākalpa (what

[[P1242]]happened in bygone ages). [^2016a] Devala states that sages have laid down expiation for the first wrong, for committing a second double of that, for committing a third time thrice as much but there is no expiation for committing it a fourth time. The Prāyaścitta-prakaraṇa of Bhavadeva says that this is not to be taken literally, but it is only nindārthavāda. The P. M. S. itself (VI. 7. 26 and 30) states that parakṛti and purākalpa are arthavādas.

The Vyavahāra-mayūkha quotes [[& verge of Devala|a verse of Devala  'on]] [^2017] the father's death the sons should divide the paternal wealth, for they have no ownership as long as the father who suffers from no blemish (that deprive him of ownership) is alive'. Here the first half lays down a time for partition (it is a vidhi), the latter half is only an arthavāda praising the vidhi and means that, as long as the father is alive, the sons are not independent and not that they have no ownership in ancestral property.

[[Smptis|Smṛtis]] also abound in arthavādas. For example, Medhātithi on Manu V. 56 ([[na māmsabhakṣane doṣaḥ|na māṁsabhakṣaṇe doṣaḥ]]) says that from V. 28 up to V. 56 all verses except two or three are arthavādas. Medhātithi points out in several other places in the Manusmṛti a few vidhis and numerous arthavādas. For example, Manu II. 117 contains a vidhi about abhivādana, while verses II.

[^2016a]: [[(apara:) apieru: Parai-Tai-Ta-greicitari ... otor महता पुरुषेणेदं कर्म कृतमिति प्रतिपादकोर्|स (अर्थवादः) च चतुर्विधः निन्दा-प्रशंसा-परकृति-पुराकल्पभेदात् । ... परण महता पुरुषेणेदं कर्म कृतमिति प्रतिपादकोऽर्थवादः परकृतिः-यथा अग्निर्वा अकामयत-इत्यादिः । परभवक्तृकार्थोऽभिप्रतिपादकः पुराकल्पः-यथा तमज्ञापद्विद्या धिया त्वा वध्यासुः-इत्यादिः । मी० परि. pp. 27-28. On मनु II. 151 where an Āṅgirasa taught his pitṛs and is said to have called them 'putrakāḥ' मेधातिथि remarks 'पूर्वस्य पितृवद्वृत्तिविधेरर्थ-वादोऽयं परकृतिनामा.' The वायुपुराण (59.134-137) defines विधि, स्तुति, निन्दा, परकृति and पुराकल्प. The same four are said to be the constituents of अर्थवाद in न्यायसूत्र II 1. 65. परकृतिपुराकल्पं च मनुष्यधर्मः स्यादर्थाय ह्यनुकीर्तनम् । ... अर्थवादो वा विधिशेषत्वात्तस्मात्स्तुत्यानुवादः स्यात् । पू. मी. सू. VI. 7. 26 and 30. The story of]] Śunaḥśepa whom his father sold to Hariścandra's son and was willing even to kill him as offering to Varuṇa (narrated in the Ait. Br.) is really an अर्थवाद of the परकृति type. Vide मनु X. 105 where this legend is mentioned and मेधा० thereon.

[^2017]: [[यत्त देवल:-पितयुपरते पुत्रा विभजेयुर्धन पितुः । अस्वाम्यं हि भवेदेषां निर्दोष पितरि स्थिते-TITUTS a F RS1 64. #. p. 90. The xy. A. extends this to a sūtra of Saṅkha which forbids partition by sons|यत्तु देवलः-पितर्युपरते पुत्रा विभजेयुर्धनं पितुः । अस्वाम्यं हि भवेदेषां निर्दोषे पितरि स्थिते-तत्र पूर्वार्धं तावद्विभागकालविधायकं विधिप्रत्ययश्रवणात्, उत्तरार्धं तु तस्यैव स्तुत्यर्थतयाऽस्वातन्त्र्यपरं न तु स्वत्वाभावपरम् । व्य. म. p. 90. The व्य. म. extends this to a sūtra of Śaṅkha which forbids partition by sons]] during the father's life-time even of property earned by them after they are born and states that they have no independence as regards wealth and religious ceremonies `during the father's life and that the words about absence of independence are only an arthavāda eulogising the prohibition about non-division.

[[P1243]]118-121 are arthavādas in relation to it. In Manu [[II, 165|II. 165]] a vidhi is laid down for the study of the whole Veda by persons of the three upper classes; when Manu (X. 1) again lays down that the three classes should study the Veda, this is merely an anuvāda. Medhātithi on Manu IX. 135 remarks that many verses in Manu contain arthavādas.

The [[Vasiṣtha-dharmasūtra|Vasiṣṭha-dharmasūtra]] and [[Viṣṇudharmottara W18|Viṣṇudharmottara]] [^2018] provide that cow's urine, excreta, milk, [[ourds,|curds,]] clarified butter, water in which kuśas have been dipped and a fast for a day and night will purify even a śvapāka. A Śvapāka was the lowest amongst untouchables and followed the same avocations as a cāṇḍāla and was governed by the same rules (vide Manu X. 51-56). This verse is not to be taken literally, as nothing could make a cāṇḍāla touchable. Therefore, this is a mere arthavāda meant to praise the purifying effects of pañcagavya and a fast.

It would have been noticed that every Vedic passage is not of the nature of [[&|a]] vidhi (of an injunctive or exhortatory character). There are numerous Vedic passages that are commendatory of a vidhi, condemn an act that is prohibited, give an illustration of some one having performed the vidhi in the past or put forward an easily understood reason for the specific act which is enjoined. These commendatory, condemnatory and illustrative passages are not to be treated as unnecessary or purposeless, but are to be taken along with the vidhi passages to complete their full import. This doctrine of arthavādas saves a huge portion of Vedic texts from being neglected or treated as unnecessary.

The Garuḍapurāṇa states 'Gāndhārī, who observed a fast on [[Ekadasi|Ekādaśī]] mixed with daśamī, lost her hundred sons; therefore [[Ekadasi|Ekādaśī]] mixed with daśamī should be given up'. Here the first part is only [[s|a]] nindānuvāda (i. e. it indirectly supports the positive rule 'tāṁ parivarjayet'), as the maxim is that 'nindā in a text is not meant for the mere sake of condemnation but for prescribing the opposite of what is condemned.' [[Vide.Krtyaratnākara|Vide Kṛtyaratnākara]] p. 635 for this interpretation. The M. B. P. (pp. 50-58) gives another classification of arthavādas into 38 varieties. They are passed over here from considerations of space.

The arthavādas constitute the largest part of the Veda, [[particulary|particularly]] in the Brāhmaṇa texts. About [[ārthavāda|arthavāda]] the

[^2018]: [[गोमूत्रं गोमयं क्षीरं दधि सपिः कुशोदकम् । एकरात्रोपवासश्च श्वपाकमपि situacu afe 27. 3, Page|गोमूत्रं गोमयं क्षीरं दधि सर्पिः कुशोदकम् । एकरात्रोपवासश्च श्वपाकमपि शोधयेत् ॥ वसिष्ठ 27. 3, विष्णुधर्मोत्तर II. 42. 31-32.]]

[[P1244]]Tantravārtika makes [^2019] a general statement that [[arthavada|arthavāda]] passages coming after vidhi passages are weak, while those that come before vidhis are stronger.

The 3rd class of Vedic texts are mantras. They have already been treated above (vide p. 1220, notes 1979 ff.). Some mantras also lay down an injunction as in Ṛg. X. 117. 5 'pṛṇīyād-in-mādhamānāya-tavyān' (the strong must bestow wealth on one who begs), also the mantra 'vasantāya kapiñjalānālabhate' (Vāj. S. 24. 20) cited above on p. 1221. But generally mantras are only expressive or assertive and call to mind things that are connected with the acts enjoined by vidhi-vākyas (injunctive passages). The Tantravārtika [^2020] notes that it is definitely understood that religious acts that are performed with mantras that recall to the mind the things enjoined lead to prosperity (or to [[srarga).|svarga).]] The reader would notice how the Pūrvamīmāṁsā system came to relegate mantras to a secondary place and made them play a passive role in the sacrificial system. The Ṛgveda contained sublime prayers, but in the mīmāṁsā system the most prominent place was given to Brāhmaṇa texts, that contained almost all the vidhis. It has been seen above pp. 950-952 how Ṛgveda mantras are full of faith (bhakti) in God and fervent appeals to God after confession of sin and repentance; vide Ṛg. VII. 86. 4-6 (kim-āga āsa varuṇa jyeṣṭham &c.); Ṛg. III. 39 is a hymn full of beatitude, verse 2 of which says 'this prayer (dhīḥ) born of yore in heaven, sung in the holy assembly with earnestness, dressed in pure (lit. white) and auspicious garb is ours, ancient and inherited from our forefathers'

The 4th part of Vedic texts bearing on Dharma is 'Nāmadheya' (proper names of sacrifices). There are such texts as [[' one|'one]] should sacrifice with the [[udbhid' (Tāpdya|udbhid' (Tāṇḍya]] Br. 19. 7. 2-3), [[*one|'one]] desirous of possessing cattle should sacrifice with citrā' [^2021] (Tai. S. II. 4. 6. 1). The question is whether in these passages what is laid down is some substance to be offered in some rite (as in

[^2019]: [[2019. ये हि विध्युद्देशात्परस्तादर्थवादाः श्रूयन्ते तेषामस|ये हि विध्युद्देशात्परस्तादर्थवादाः श्रूयन्ते तेषामस्ति दौर्बल्यम् । य पुरस्ताच्छ्रयन्ते ते मुख्यत्वाद् बलीयांसो भवन्ति । तन्त्रवा. on III. 3. 2.]]

[^2020]: [[1 00 g. #1. 1. 2. 32 says gergareta o raitancuri यज्ञानप्रकाशनमेव प्रयोजनम्। मन्त्रैरेव स्|शबर on पू. मी. सू. I. 2. 32 says अर्थप्रत्यायनार्थमेव यज्ञे मन्त्रोच्चारणम् । यज्ञाङ्गप्रकाशनमेव प्रयोजनम् ।; मन्त्रैरेव स्मृत्वा कृतं कर्मान्धुदयकारि भवतीत्यवधार्यते । तन्त्रवा. on II. 1. 31 p. 433.]]

[^2021]: [[gimnasta gigan ara perguerra fort med KrA forurai . 19. 7. 2-3. Vide H. of Dh. vol. II. p.|पशुकामो यजेत । यदुद्भिदा यजते बलमेवास्मै विख्यापयति यद्बलभिदा बलमेवास्मै भिनत्ति । ताण्ड्य. 19. 7. 2-3. Vide H. of Dh. vol. II. p. 269 n. 618 for the application of the Udbhidadhikaraṇa (P. M. S. I. 4. 1-2) by संस्कारप्रकाश and above p. 137 by the समयमयूख.]]

[[P1245]]'dadhnā juhoti) or whether they are names of [[sacrificos.|sacrifices.]] No substance is well-known as 'udbhid' (as dadhi is a well-known substance). Citrā means a female animal of variegated colour. If it is guṇavidhi (in the case of 'citrayā yajeta'), there would be the fault of Vākyabheda (one sentence broken up as laying down two vidhis) i. e. the injunction would be that a female paśu was to be sacrificed (not a male) and further injunction that its colour should be variegated. Therefore, Udbhid, Citrā, Balabhid, Abhijit, Viśvajit (Kauṣītaki Br. 25. 14) and Agnihotra (P. M. S. I. 4. 4), Vājapeya (P. M. S. I. 4. 6-8), Vaiśvadeva (P. M. S. I. 4. 13-16) are names of rites and are not substances. Similarly, in 'Śyenēnābhicaran yajeta' (one practising black magic for bringing about the death of an enemy may perform a yāga called śyena). Here 'śyena' is the name of a yāga, because the yāga swoops down upon the enemy and seizes him just as a falcon swoops down upon its victim and catches it (Ṣaḍviṁśa Br. III. 8. 1. 3). The point to be noted is that these names serve the purpose of [^2022] particularising the meaning of what is enjoined. The Vedic text 'svādhyāyo'dhyetavyaḥ' enjoins the study of the Veda consisting of all parts including nāmadheyas of sacrifices and we directly see that in such Vedic vidhis as 'Citrayā yajeta paśukāmaḥ' the name citrā is a part of the vidhi. Therefore, nāmadheya also is a puruṣārtha and is authoritative like other parts of the Veda (vide Śāstradīpikā on Jai. I. 4. 1). In the sentences mentioned above yāga is enjoined with the fruit as the object, since it is not otherwise established. Since it is not intended to lay down a general injunction to sacrifice, a specific variety of sacrifice has to be enjoined. When one wants to know the specific variety enjoined, from the word 'udbhid' it is known that it is the sacrifice called udbhid. Dharmaśāstra writers apply this maxim called 'Udbhid-nyāya' to 'Upanayana' which literally means 'taking (a boy) near an ācārya' (teacher of the Veda)'. The [[Samakāraprakāśa gays so.|Saṁskāraprakāśa says so.]] [^2023]]

[^2022]: [[arauarat foarte TSÚTFTPI June gata TITA इत्यत्र उद्धिन्छन्दो यागनामधेयम्। तेन हि विधेयार्थप|नामधेयानां विधेयार्थपरिच्छेदकतयाऽर्थवत्त्वम् । तथाहि—उद्भिदा यजेत पशुकाम इत्यत्र उद्भिच्छब्दो यागनामधेयम् । तेन हि विधेयार्थपरिच्छेदः क्रियते । अनेन हि वाक्येनाप्राप्तत्वात्फलोद्देशेन यागो विधीयते । यागसामान्यस्य चाविधेयत्वाद्यागविशेष एव विधीयते । तत्र कोऽसौ विशेष इत्यपेक्षायाम् उद्भिच्छब्दादुद्भिद्रूपो यो याग इति ज्ञायते । मी. न्या. प्र. p. 206 (ABORI).]]

[^2023]: [[1994 prez: AUTORI A alongi pratarat sa app व्युत्परया करणयुत्पश्या त्याह भारथिः। स यथा। उप|तत्रोपनयनशब्दः कर्मनामधेयम् । तच्च यौगिकमुद्भिद्वन्न्यायात् । योगश्च भावव्युत्पत्त्या करणव्युत्पत्त्या वेत्याह भारुचिः । स यथा । उप समीपे आचार्यादीनां बटोर्नयनं प्रापणमुपनयनम् । समीपे आचार्यादीनां नीयते बहुर्येन तदुपनायनमिति वा । संस्कारप्रकाश p. 334.]]

[[P1246]]The fifth (and last) part of [[Vedio|Vedic]] texts consists of 'pratiṣedha' (niṣedha, prohibition). Prohibitions[^2024] serve the [[enda|ends]] of man by causing [[mon|men]] to desist from actions which would produce undesirable results. To explain, just as vidhis, which express an urge or prompting (to do something), suggest, in order to effectuate their prompting quality, that the matter to be enjoined such as a sacrifice is the means to attain a desirable result and so urge a man to do it, so also prohibitions such as 'one should not eat [[Kalanja|Kalañja]] (red garlic)' or 'one should not utter what is false' (Tai. S. II. 5. 5. 6) denote a turning away and in order to effectuate their own deterring quality, suggest that the thing to be prohibited viz. eating kalañja or speaking untruth will be the cause of bringing about an undesirable result and so make a man desist from it. The particle 'na' may come before a verbal form, a noun or an adjective and 'na' becomes in certain [[casos|cases]] 'a' (as in 'abrāhmaṇa,' 'adharma') and 'an' in certain others [[whon|when]] it is prefixed to a word [[begind ing|beginning]] with a vowel (as in 'anaśva', '[[anuṣna|anuṣṇa]]'). Pāṇini devotes several sūtras to 'na' and expressly refers to 'pratiṣedha' as one of the meanings conveyed by 'na' (vide [[Pān.|Pāṇ.]] II. 2. 6, VI. 2. 155 &c.)[^2025]; 'na' is said to convey six senses as noted in the [[verge|verse]] quoted below.[^2026]

The first meaning of 'na' is 'abhāva' ([[non-existence ).|non-existence).]] But that meaning would not suit all cases. When one says 'bring [[abrābmana'|abrāhmaṇa']] the meaning conveyed is not [[non-existence'|'non-existence',]] since, if that were the meaning, one could not bring non-existing brāhmaṇa and so might not bring any one at all, or might

I. 5. 17. 26 forbids the eating of 3 , पलाण्ड and परारीक and हरदत्त explains 'कलसं रक्तलशुनम् ' and कल्पतर on Parents p. 280 explains it as syaratang.|अनर्थहेतुकर्मणः सकाशात्पुरुषस्य निवृत्तिकरत्वेन निषेधानां पुरुषार्थानुबन्धित्वम् । तथा हि । यथा विधयः प्रवर्तनामभिदधतः स्वप्रवर्तकत्वनिर्वाहार्थं विधेयस्य यागादेः श्रेयःसाधनत्वमाक्षिपन्तः पुरुषं तत्र प्रवर्तयन्ति, एवं 'न कलञ्जं भक्षयेद्' इत्यादयो निषेधा अपि निवर्तनामभिदधतः स्वनिवर्तकत्वनिर्वाहार्थं निषेध्यस्य कलञ्जभक्षणादेरनर्थहेतुत्वमाक्षिपन्तः पुरुषं ततो निवर्तयन्ति । मी. न्या. प्र. pp. 248-249. Some read प्रेरणा for प्रवर्तनाम्. The meaning of both is the same. आप. ध. सू. III. 1. 12 says 'नञिवयुक्तमन्यसदृशाधिकरणे तथा ह्यर्थगतिः' and explains 'अब्राह्मणमानयेत्युक्तो (के?) ब्राह्मणसदृश आनीयते नासौ लोष्टमानीय कृती भवति'.]]

[[P1247]]bring a clod of earth which would not fulfil any purpose of the person addressing these words and therefore, on hearing this, a person not [[&|a]] brāhmaṇa but resembling a [[brahmana|brāhmaṇa]] (such as a [[ksatriya|kṣatriya]]) is brought. So in this case 'abrāhmaṇa' means [[&|a]] person other than a brāhmaṇa; 'na' also conveys the opposite (virodha) of that to which it is prefixed. It has been seen above that the verb is the main part in a sentence and in the verbal form it is the [[tormination|termination]] that is the principal part. [[80,|So,]] in 'one must not eat [[kalaija'|kalañja']] the negative particle 'na' must be held to be connected with the optative termination in '[[bhak sayet'.|bhakṣayet'.]] In a vidhi[^2027] (or on hearing a vidhi) one perceives that the sentence urges the listener to be active. When [['na'is|'na' is]] prefixed to an optative [[from|form]] it conveys the opposite of [[trging|urging]] viz. [[' nivartana'|'nivartana']] (desisting from something). The phala that one understands from [[&|a]] vidhi is as in 'yajeta [[svargakamah' starga,|svargakāmaḥ' svarga,]] while in a prohibition the phala is cessation of harm (anartha-nivṛtti). The [[adhikari|adhikārī]] in a vidhi is one who desires [[gvarga;|svarga;]] in a niṣedha the [[adhikari|adhikārī]] is one who is afraid of harm and desires to avoid what is undesirable. Therefore, these considerations establish that exhortation and [[probibition|prohibition]] are entirely different in their import.

But when there is an obstacle or difficulty in construing 'na' with the verbal termination, then it is construed with the meaning of the root. These obstacles are of two sorts; one is where the whole passage is introduced by the words 'his vows [[are *|are']] or where an option will emerge if the meaning of prohibition is stuck to. In the case of these two obstacles in sentences where

[^2027]: [[अतो लिहत्वांशेन ना सम्बध्यते। तस्य सर्वापेक्षया प्राधान्यात्। नश्वेष स्वभावो यत्स्वसम्बन्धिप्रतिपक्षपोषकत्वम् । ... तदिह लिबर्थस्तावत्मवर्तना। अतस्तेन सम्बाय: मानो नमवसनापतिपक्ष निवर्तनां गमयति । अतश्च सर्वत्र निषेधेषु निवर्तनैव वाक्यार्थः । एवं च विधिनिषेधयोभिकार्थत्वं सिद्धं भवति। यथाहुः। अन्तरं यादृशं लोके ब्रह्माहत्याश्वमेधयोः। घश्यते तागेवेदं विधानप्रतिषेधयोः॥ इति। तथा! ...सर्वथापि तु नः प्राधान्यात्मत्ययेनान्वयः। पदा तु वदन्वये किशिवाधकं तदागत्या धात्वर्धनान्वयः। तच्च बाधकं द्विविधम् । तस्य व्रताम त्युपक्रमो विकल्पमसक्तिश्च। तेन च वाधकहयेन नञ्युक्तेषु पाक्येषु पहुंदासाश्रयर्ष भवति । सदभावे निषेध एव। पर्युवासः स विज्ञेयो यत्रोत्तरपदेन नञ् । प्रतिषेधः स विज्ञेयः कियया सह यत्र न॥ इति च तयोर्लक्षणम् । तत्र नेक्षतोयन्तमादिस्यम-इत्यादौ पर्युदासाश्रयणम, तस्य वतमित्युपक्रमात् । तथाहि व्रतशब्देन कर्तव्योर्थ उच्यते। मी. न्या. म. pp. 250-253, The न्यायसुधा (or राणक) p. 201 00 तम्यवा. quotes the verse अन्तरं...पेषयो: as from the पृष्हीका (of कुमारिल) and cites verses explaining the five points (फल &c.) in wbicb port and pay differ. The word ma is a technical ATAIT word and means the termination in the verbal form and gaua means the pada minus the termination, विधिः भवर्तमानो हि श्रेयासिडचे प्रवर्तते । प्रतिषेधः पुनः पापानिवर्तयति भेवतः। तन्त्रवा. on पू. मी. स. III. 4. 13p.911.|अतो लिङंशांशेन नञ् सम्बध्यते। तस्य सर्वापेक्षया प्राधान्यात्। नञश्चैष स्वभावो यत्स्वसम्बन्धिप्रतिपक्षबोधकत्वम् । ... तदिह लिङर्थस्तावत्प्रवर्तना। अतस्तेन सम्बध्यमानो नञ् प्रवर्तनाप्रतिपक्षं निवर्तनां गमयति । अतश्च सर्वत्र निषेधेषु निवर्तनैव वाक्यार्थः । एवं च विधिनिषेधयोर्भिन्नार्थत्वं सिद्धं भवति। यथाहुः। अन्तरं यादृशं लोके ब्रह्महत्याश्वमेधयोः । दृश्यते तादृगेवेदं विधानप्रतिषेधयोः ॥ इति। तथा । ...सर्वथापि तु नञः प्राधान्यात्प्रत्ययेनान्वयः। यदा तु तदन्वये किञ्चिद्बाधकं तदागत्या धात्वर्थेनान्वयः। तच्च बाधकं द्विविधम् । तस्य व्रतमित्युपक्रमो विकल्पप्रसक्तिश्च। तेन च बाधकद्वयेन नञ्युक्तेषु वाक्येषु पर्युदासाश्रयणं भवति। तदभावे निषेध एव। पर्युदासः स विज्ञेयो यत्रोत्तरपदेन नञ् । प्रतिषेधः स विज्ञेयः क्रियया सह यत्र न ॥ इति च तयोर्लक्षणम् । तत्र नेक्षेतोद्यन्तमादित्यम्-इत्यादौ पर्युदासाश्रयणम्, तस्य व्रतमित्युपक्रमात् । तथाहि व्रतशब्देन कर्तव्योऽर्थ उच्यते। मी. न्या. प्र. pp. 250-253. The न्यायसुधा (or राणक) p. 201 on तन्त्रवा. quotes the verse अन्तरं...षेधयोः as from the बृहट्टीका (of कुमारिल) and cites verses explaining the five points (फल &c.) in which विधि and निषेध differ. The word उत्तरपद is a technical मीमांसा word and means the termination in the verbal form and पूर्वपद means the pada minus the termination. विधिः प्रवर्तमानो हि श्रेयासिद्ध्यै प्रवर्तते । प्रतिषेधः पुनः पापात्निवर्तयति भेषवत् । तन्त्रवा. on पू. मी. सू. III. 4. 13 p. 911.]]

[[P1248]]'na' occurs, we would have to resort to paryudāsa (exclusion or exception). In the case of the Prajāpativratas[^2028] (which are [[purusārtha|puruṣārtha]] as decided in P. M. S. IV. 1. 3) the passage starts with the words [['his vows aro'|'his vows are']] and then comes the sentence 'he must not see the sun [[irising|rising]] or setting' ([[Kausitaki|Kauṣītaki]] Br. VI. 6). Vrata means a mental act, a resolve not to do a certain thing, the meaning being 'he should make a resolve to act in such a way as not to see the rising or setting sun and stick to it'. This really is niyama (restriction). This passage does not mean that he should never look at the sun (there is no [[probibition of seving|prohibition of seeing]] the sun) but it only excludes the sight of the rising or setting sun; therefore, this is only exclusion and he who observes this niyama (restriction) secures the reward while in the case of eating [[kalanja|kalañja]] there is a total prohibition. 'Paryudāsa (exclusion) is to be understood where the negative particle is taken with another word ([[i e.|i. e.]] with the verbal root or a different word such as a noun); [[probibition|prohibition]] is to be understood where the negative particle is taken with the verbal [[form',|form'.]]

In Dharmaśāstra works, 'na' is frequently explained as laying down a paryudāsa (proviso or exception). In the section on the duties of a [[smūtulu|snātaka]] in Yāj. I. 129-166 'na' occurs very often. The Mit. explains (on [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 129) that in this section wherever 'na' occurs it is meant to [[denoto|denote]] a [[paryudāga|paryudāsa]] ([[sarva trāpi asmin snatakaprakarane nan-sabdaḥ pratyekam paryudi sārtha eva),|sarvatrāpi asmin snātakaprakaraṇe nañ-śabdaḥ pratyekaṁ paryudāsārtha eva).]] To take only one example, [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] in I. 132 provides that one should not speak (to a man or woman) what would be painful without any necessity or cause. This does not altogether prohibit speaking what is painful; what it means is that a [[snūtaka|snātaka]] may say to another what is painful except when there is no proper reason nor proper [[oocasion|occasion]] to do so. One may have to speak what is painful to an erring son or friend or near relative. Aparārka (pp. 206-207) quotes two well-known
