VI. 6 (or शां. ब्रा.) we read 'तस्य व्रतमुद्यन्तमेवैनं नेक्षेतास्तं यन्तं चेति' । मनु IV. 37 has a similar provision 'नेक्षेतोद्यन्तमादित्यं नास्तं यान्तं कदाचन' । Vide अनुशासनपर्व 104. 18, वसिष्ठ XII. 10 (among स्नातकव्रतs), विष्णुधर्मसूत्र 71. 17-18 for almost the same words.]]

[[P1249]]verses on the distinction between paryudāsa and [[pratizedha (the two balvos of whioh|pratiṣedha (the two halves of which]] are quoted and underlined in note 2027) on the interpretation of a verse requiring a man having a son not to observe a fast on certain days &c. The first halves of [[those|these]] verses [[aro;|are;]] 'Pradhānatvaṁ [[vidhauyatra pratigedhespradhā Datā (paryudāsaḥ...nan) |||vidhau yatra pratiṣedhe'pradhānatā (paryudāsaḥ...nañ) \|\|]] aprādhānyaṁ vidhau yatra [[pratizedbe|pratiṣedhe]] pradhānatā [[prasajyaprati...mañ'.ll|prasajyaprati...nañ'.\|\|]]

When the particle [['da'|'na']] is employed in a sentence, it is either [[&|a]] prohibition or a paryudāsa or an arthavāda. These three have to be clearly [[distinguished.)|distinguished.]] In [[Darsapūrpamāsa|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] the two [[ajyabhāgas|ājyabhāgas]] are an aṅga (P. M. S. IV. 4. 30) and [[&|a]] passage states that the two ājyabhāgas are the eyes of the [[Darsapūrpamāsa saorifice. 1029|Darśapūrṇamāsa sacrifice.]][^2029] In connection with this the Veda says 'these two are not performed in an animal sacrifice nor in a Soma sacrifice'. The question [[is;|is:]] is this a prohibition or a paryudāsa or an arthavāda. A pratiṣedha occurs only when there is the possibility of what is prohibited following as a matter of course. As the ājyabhāgas are prescribed in [[Darśapā roaṁāga|Darśapūrṇamāsa]] there is no possibility of these two being required in a Somayāga and there is no real prohibition. Nor is there [[paryudāsu,|paryudāsa,]] for if it be a [[paryudāda|paryudāsa]] there would be no proper connection, since in paryudāsa one would have to say 'there are ājyabhāgas in [[darśapūrṇamāsa except in somayāgs',|darśapūrṇamāsa except in somayāga',]] which would be absurd. Therefore, these words 'na tau paśau karoti na some' contain an arthavāda. A true[^2030] prohibition would occur only when something is first prescribed and then prohibited. The stock example is of the Ṣoḍaśin cup; there are two Vedic sentences of equal authority 'he takes the [[sodasin oup|ṣoḍaśin cup]] in Atirātra' and 'he does not take the [[sodasin|ṣoḍaśin]] cup in atirātra'; owing to this conflict an option is allowed. Similarly, there is a [[Vedio|Vedic]] passage [['Vrihibhir yajeta yayairyā'|'Vrīhibhir yajeta yavairvā']] (one shall offer [[a.|a]] sacrifice with grains of rice or with yavas). Therefore, in the above two cases where the two texts are irreconcilable, there is

X. 8. 6. शबर explains 'यथा नातिरात्रे गृह्णाति षोडशिनमिति । न तत्र शक्यं वक्तुं पर्युदास इति । सम्बन्ध एव हि न स्यात् । अतिरात्रवर्जितातिरात्रे गृह्णाति षोडशिनमिति । नापि कस्यचिदर्थवादत्वेन सम्भवति । ... यत्र पुनरन्या वचनव्यक्तिरस्ति वाक्यस्य तत्र न विकल्पो भवति । एवमेषोऽष्टदोषोऽपि यद्व्रीहियववाक्ययोः । विकल्प आश्रितस्तत्र गतिरन्या न विद्यते । व्रीहिज्ञानप्रवृत्तो हि यवज्ञानेन रुध्यते । श्रोता तत्र स्वयं भ्रान्तो नैकस्मिन् प्रतिष्ठति । तन्त्रवा. on I. 3. 3 p. 175. Vide also प्राप्तिपूर्वो हि प्रतिषेधो भवति । शबर on पू. मी. सू. VII. 3. 20 and VII. 3. 23.]]

[[P1250]]no way except that of allowing an option (vikalpa). But 'vikalpa' is liable to eight faults.[^2031] Therefore, an effort has to be made to avoid option and accept as far as possible a [[paryu dasa|paryudāsa]] or arthavāda, since by accepting an option, the authoritative statement in one case or the other has to be regarded as unauthoritative and not binding. Śabara and Tantravārtika provide that vikalpa should be resorted to only in case there is no other way. The P. M. S. lays down that vikalpa may be accepted when several things having the same object (or sense) are stated by several authoritative texts.

There is one more word that must be explained, viz. [[mityānuvāda.|nityānuvāda.]][^2032] This word occurs in Āpastambadharmasūtra II. 6. 14. 13 (vide n. 1877 above). It occurs frequently in Jaimini (vide II. 4. 26, IV. 1. 5, VI. 7. 30, VII. 4. 5, VIII. 1. 6, IX. 4. 36, X. 2. 38) and Śabara uses it oftener still. He explains that where the Vedic text apparently prohibits something when there is no possibility of what is prohibited as happening, there is nityānuvāda (as in 'the fire altar should not be piled on bare earth nor in the sky nor in heaven'). The Tupṭīkā states the same thing in [[&|a]] different form when it says that where a prohibition amounts to an arthavāda, that is nityānuvāda.

Vikalpas (options) are grouped under three [[headṣ|heads]] viz. (1) those indicated by logic or reasoning, (2) those due to express words and (3) those dependent on the will of the performer. [[Au|An]] example of the first kind is the option about rice grains and barley grains ([[yavair yrihibhir-vā|yavair vrīhibhir-vā]] yajeta). The 2nd may be exemplified from Manu III. 267, which says that the Manes derive satisfaction for a month [[whon|when]] offerings are made of sesame or of rice or of barley or of [[māsa|māṣa]] pulse or of water or of fruits and roots.

XII. 3. 10. The eight faults of allowing an option between two provisions are set out in मी. न्या. प्र. p. 151 and Prof. Edgerton's translation of मी. न्या. प्र. paragraph 318 pp. 163-164, n. 212. The एकादशीतत्त्व (p. 42) discusses the व्रीहियववाक्य, quotes the verse एवमेषोऽष्टदोषोऽपि० and points out the eight doṣas. X. 8. 15 'अपि तु वाक्यशेषत्वादितरपर्युदासः स्यात्प्रतिषेधे विकल्पः स्यात्' which is quoted in the शाङ्करभाष्य on वे. सू. III. 3. 26). The भामती remarks (on this sūtra) that these words are only a summary of the original sūtra which is 'अपि वा वाक्यशेषः स्यादन्याय्यत्वाद्विकल्पस्य विधीनामेकदेशः स्यात् ।']]

[^2032]: [[Tife a nd it aga on I. 2. 18; đang tạm non fac413 414 wala gent on VII, 3, 21; 00 IX. 4. 36 (at foredrama TUTC) no explains : factand Fotografa.|असति प्रसङ्गे प्रतिषेधो नित्यानुवादः । शबर on I. 2. 18; यदर्थवादत्वेन प्रतिषेधस्तत्र नित्यानुवादो भवति। टुप्टीका on VII. 3. 21; on IX. 4. 36 (परो नित्यानुवादः स्यात्) शबर explains : नित्यमेतमर्थं सन्तमनुवदति ।]]

[[P1251]]'An option depending on the will of a person [[osoars|occurs]] in the Jābālopaniṣad[^2033] (4) 'after finishing the period of studenthood one should become a householder, after becoming a householder, one should become a forest hermit and after becoming a forest hermit one may become [[&|a]] wandering ascetic (sannyāsin or [[parivrād );|parivrāḍ);]] or following another method one may become [[&|a]] wandering ascetic after finishing studenthood or after becoming [[an householder|a householder]] or after becoming a forest hermit.' The latter part of this passage gives an option as to āśramas.' This option is referred to by Gautama in the words 'some sages allow an option as to [[asramas|āśramas]] to him (to brahmacārin).' When Yāj. I. 14 provides that the Upanayana of a brāhmaṇa boy may be performed in the eighth year from conception or from birth there is an option depending on the will of the father.

Manu IV. 7 provides that a dvija should amass as much corn as would fill a granary (i. e. as would last for a year) or a [[Kumbhi|Kumbhī]] (for six months) or as much as would suffice for three days or may not care to amass even for to-morrow. These are four options and then Manu IV. 8 provides that a dvija who is [[an householder|a householder]] may choose one of these four, but each [[sucoeed ing|succeeding]] one is superior in point of merit and otherworldly rewards.

A vikalpa may also be vyavasthita (restricted or limited to a certain set of circumstances) or avyavasthita (not restricted). The Āp. Dh. S. provides[^2034] that one should offer bali with his hand in the aupāsana fire or in the kitchen fire with the first six mantras. Haradatta remarks that 'This is a limited option viz. in the aupāsana fire bali should be cast every day by those who have kept up that fire and in the ordinary kitchen fire by

The fau44144 of me III, 4. 18-20 is Jlratra II, 23. 1 ' w : ... PATI Samaa.. and the WHEE mentions the views of जैमिनि and पादरायण on this छान्दोग्य passage and the established conclusion is 'fartai yrtura: III. 4, 20. AYHEF says 'aut श्रमविकल्पमेके अवते III. 1. तस्य referring to ब्रह्मचारिन्. The जाबालोपनिषद does not appear to have been known to the author of the Brahmasūtra while views similar to those propounded by it were known to Gautama. The Mit. on Yaj. III. 56 refers to the Jābālopaniṣad as allowing an option 'aufen जावाल तो भूयते ब्रह्मचर्य ... वनाहा। इति। ... एतेषां समुञ्चयषिकल्पवाधपक्षाणां सर्वेषा अतिमूलस्वादिछया विकल्पः।।.|ब्रह्मचर्यं परिसमाप्य गृही भवेत् । गृही भूत्वा वनी भवेत् । वनी भूत्वा प्रव्रजेत् । यदि चेतरथा ब्रह्मचर्यादेव प्रव्रजेद्गृहाद्वा वनाद्वा । जाबालोप. 4, q. by शङ्कराचार्य in his भाष्य on ब्रह्मसूत्र III. 4. 20 with the remark 'अनपेक्ष्यैव जाबालश्रुतिमाश्रमान्तरविधायिनीमयमाचार्येण विचारः प्रवर्तितः'। The विषयवाक्य of ब्रह्मसूत्र III. 4. 18-20 is छान्दोग्य II. 23. 1 'त्रयो धर्मस्कन्धाः ... ब्रह्मसंस्थोऽमृतत्वमेति'। and the ब्रह्मसूत्र mentions the views of जैमिनि and बादरायण on this छान्दोग्य passage and the established conclusion is 'विधिर्वा धारणवत्' III. 4. 20. गौतमधर्मसूत्र says 'तस्याश्रमविकल्पमेके ब्रुवते' III. 1, तस्य referring to ब्रह्मचारिन्. The जाबालोपनिषद् does not appear to have been known to the author of the Brahmasūtra while views similar to those propounded by it were known to Gautama. The Mit. on Yāj. III. 56 refers to the Jābālopaniṣad as allowing an option 'तथा च विकल्पो जाबालश्रुतौ श्रूयते ब्रह्मचर्यं ... वनाद्वा । इति । ... एतेषां समुच्चयविकल्पबाधपक्षाणां सर्वेषां श्रुतिमूलत्वादच्छिया विकल्पः।।'.]]

[^2034]: [[34r y gara eta fragt: Partea 1 9. 4. . II. 2.3. 16: 'भोपासमवतामापासने विधुरा पचनेतिज्यवस्थितो विकल्पः। हरदच.|औपासने पचने वा षड्भिराद्यैः प्रतिमन्त्रं हस्तेन जुहुयात् । आप. ध. सू. II. 2. 3. 16; 'औपासनवतामौपासने विधुरादेः पचनेति व्यवस्थितो विकल्पः ।' हरदत्तः ।]]

[[P1252]]him whose wife is dead. Manu III. 82 prescribes that one [[ahould|should]] offer Śrāddha everyday with food, water, milk etc. Here there is vyavasthita-vikalpa i. e. first food, then in its absence milk, fruits and roots, and in the absence of these water. When Manu IV. 95 prescribes “Having performed the Upākarma rite according to proper procedure on the Full Moon of Śrāvaṇa or of Bhādrapada a brāhmaṇa should diligently study the Vedas for four months and a half.” Medhātithi says that this is a 'vyavasthita-vikalpa' viz. the Sāmavedins should perform upākarma on Bhādrapada Full Moon, while Ṛgvedins and Yajurvedins on Śrāvaṇa Full Moon. Vide the Mit. on Yāj. I. 254 about the [[supiṇlanı|sapiṇḍana]] of a mother, where the Mit. introduces order among conflicting texts. When Gaut. prescribes (III. 21) that a sannyāsin should completely shave the head or keep only a top-knot, there is an option depending on the will of the person. Gaut. II. 51-53, Āp. Dh. S. I. 2. 11, Manu III. 1 prescribe brahmacarya for Veda study for 48, 36, 24, 12, 3 years. Here there is an option depending on the capacity and wish of the student. It should be noted that the eight faults about accepting an option do not occur in a vyavasthita[^2035] vikalpa nor do they apply where the option is dependent on the volition of the performer nor where option is given by express texts. Those eight faults apply only to an option indicated by reasoning.

The [[Mimāmgābālāprakāsa (pp. 153-165 )|Mīmāṁsābālaprakāśa (pp. 153-165)]] provides a long list of the divisions and subdivisions of Vikalpa.

Śāstra is meant to give definite rules as stated by Patañjali[^2036] and therefore all [[Śāstrik|Śāstrīya]] works try to reduce options to the minimum and assign to apparently conflicting passages separate and definite provinces of application ([[Visayjavyavas thā).|Viṣayavyavasthā).]] Sometimes, the options are so many that commentators give up the task of assigning them different scopes e. g. the Mit. on Yāj. III. 22, after quoting the conflicting passages on impurity on birth and death, for kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and śūdras

[[P1253]]from Parāśara, Śātātapa, Vasiṣṭha and Atri gives up the effort to bring order because it would be of no use as people do not accept the dicta of these in practice.

Two other words require some explanation. They are 'ārādupakāraka' and 'sannipatyopakāraka'. In the 3rd chapter of the PMS the author deals[^2037] with śeṣa, its definition and explains how to determine what things are called śeṣa and what are śeṣin. Kumārila sets out five definitions of the word śeṣa, discards four and accepts one, viz. 'śeṣa' is that which serves the purpose of another. Śabara comments that what serves to help another is called śeṣa and the other is called śeṣin. Śabara adds a qualification viz. that is śeṣa which is of very much help to another. Bādari holds that there are three groups of śeṣa viz. dravya (materials for sacrifice such as rice-grains), guṇa (such as the reddish colour of the cow that is the price of the soma to be purchased), saṁskāras (purificatory acts such as the pounding of the grains in mortar and pestle which make the grains fit for turning them into puroḍāśa). Jaimini adds that rites (karmāṇi) like yāga, fruit or result (of yāga) are śeṣa as regards the performer and the performer is śeṣa with reference to the yāga. According to Bādari, dravya, guṇa, saṁskāra are always śeṣa, while according to the established conclusion, yāga, reward and performer (yāga, phala and puruṣa) may be according to circumstances either śeṣa or śeṣin. After a long discussion the Tantravārtika sums up the discussion that dravya, guṇa, and saṁskāra are with reference to yāga invariably śeṣa, though they may be śeṣin with reference to their own constituent elements, while as regards phala, yāga and the performer they can be both śeṣa and śeṣin with reference to each other. For example, in the Darśa-Pūrṇamāsa sacrifice there are various items, such as taking out handfuls of rice grains (for sacrifice), sprinkling water on them, pounding them; then certain acts are to be performed with reference to ājya (clarified butter) viz. purifying it with two blades of kuśa grass, melting it, also bringing a branch, starting the cows towards the meadows &c. These auxiliaries are of two kinds, viz. (i) that are already accomplished, (ii) that are of the nature

[^2037]: [[her: qarangi g. . III. 1, 2; that man दिमिदुम्यते । इति। त्यादीनिविनियोन कारणानी निकाला । भक्तप पिविनित प्रविनामावलमणम्। प्रयोम्पलमान ये बाविस्वाचा। भनेता सविपनपरिमहाद। सपकारेल मि om III, 1, 2 p. 654.|शेषः परार्थत्वात् । पू. मी. सू. III. 1. 2; कः शेषः केन हेतुना शेषः कथं च विनियुज्यते । इति । श्रुत्यादीनि च विनियोगे कारणानीति वक्ष्यते । तेषां यत् परप्रयुक्तप्रवृत्तितत्त्वं तच्छेषत्वम् । शबरः; शेषत्वं केचिदिच्छन्ति ह्यविनाभावरूपकम् । प्रयोज्यलक्षणं चान्ये तदसिद्ध्यैव चापरे । अन्ये त्वन्यतया तस्य विश्वरूपत्वपरिग्रहात् । उपकारेण शेषत्वं बहुमिश्रैश्च लक्षितम् । तन्त्रवा. on III. 1. 2 p. 654.]]

[[P1254]]of actions. The first comprehend substances, number &c.; those that are of the nature of acts are two-fold viz. sannipatyopakāraka and ārād-upakāraka. In the Paurṇamāsī rite there are such auxiliaries as Prayājas, the Āghāras, the Ājyabhāgas. These are ārādupakāraka. Sannipatyopakārakas are also called sāmavāyika, or āśrayikarmāṇi; they are such as the pounding of grains, prokṣaṇa etc. The Ārādupakāraka are[^2038] rites prescribed without being meant to do something about substances and are directly the aṅgas of the principal rite. These do not give rise to any saṁskāra (embellishment or making fit) in the substance meant to be offered in sacrifice, are helpful in producing the paramāpūrva that yields the fruit of the whole rite. They produce a subordinate apūrva of their own. They are directly aṅgas of the principal rite and are distinguished from sannipatyopakārakas, which are saṁskāraka (bring about some embellishment or fitness). The Sannipatyopakārakas are more powerful than Ārādupakārakas and therefore the Tantravārtika states the proposition that where it is possible to hold an act[^2039] in a rite to be sannipatyopakāraka or sāmavāyika, it is not proper to regard it as ārādupakāraka. It is somewhat remarkable that Prof. Keith in his 'Karmamīmāṁsā' (p. 88) inverts the meaning of these two words. The explanation of sannipatyopakāraka in 'Prābhākara School' (by M. M. Jha) p. 181 also is rather obscure. The Ekādaśītattva (p. 97), while discussing the substitutes (pratinidhis) to be employed on Ekādaśī for ghee, for milk and honey (being milk-powder, curds and jaggery) quotes P. M. S. VI. 3. 18 'na devatāgniśabdakriyam-anyārthasaṁyogāt', explains that in place of a prescribed kriyā like prayājas (that have an unseen or spiritual reward) there is to be no substitute, since what has an invisible result is āradupakāraka, but in place of rice grains (that are the means of preparing puroḍāśa) a substitute may be employed, since the rice grains are sannipatyopakārakas and have a seen purpose viz. preparing puroḍāśa. Śaṅkarācārya in his bhāṣya on

[[P1256]] [[V. S,|V. S.]] [[IV. I.|IV. 1.]] 16 states that the [[performanoe|performance]] of obligatory vedic rites ([[liks|like]] Agnihotra) by a man who has to acquire knowledge of brahma is helpful for attaining it as an ārādupakāraka.[^2040]

Apart from the Veda and [[smrtis|smṛtis]] the [[Pūrvamīmāmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] [[relics|relies]] upon or refers to *loka* or *lokavat* (usage of the ordinary people) [[fa|for]] elucidation. For example, I. 2. 20, [[L. 2. 29|I. 2. 29]], II. 1. 12 ([[Lokarat|Lokavat]]), IV. 1. 6 ('tathā ca lokabhūteṣu' meaning 'lokepi'), VI. 2. 16 ([[loke karmāni vedavat-tatosdhipuruṣajñānam|loke karmāṇi vedavat-tato'dhipuruṣajñānam]]), VI. 5. 34 ([[na bhaldi trād-esa hi loke|na bhaktitvād-eṣā hi loke]]), VI. 8. 26 ([[yāñca-krayanam-avidyamine lokavat|yāñcā-krayaṇam-avidyamāne lokavat]]), VII. 4. 11 ([[Lingahetutvād-alinge|Liṅgahetutvād-aliṅge]] [[laukikam|laukikaṁ]] syāt), VI. 2. 22 ([[payo vā tat-pradhānatvāl-lokavad-dadhnas-tadarthatvat|payo vā tat-pradhānatvāl-lokavad-dadhnas-tadarthatvāt]]) gives the illustration that in order to solidify [[mik|milk]] a little of curds is enough; VIII. 4. 6, ([[da laukikādām|na laukikānām]] &c. where 'laukikānām' means 'lokānām'); X. 3. 44 ([[sabdārthascapi|śabdārthaścāpi]] lokavat),

X. 3. 51, X. 6. 8, X. 7. 66 (Lokavat, [[Sabara|Śabara]] saying [['Yatha matsyāo|'Yathā matsyān]] [[na payasā samaśniyāt|na payasā samaśnīyāt]]), XI. 1. 23, 26, 62. [[Sabara|Śabara]] himself uses the word [['Laukikanyaya'|'Laukikanyāya']] in [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] on PMS [[I. 4. 13|III. 4. 13]] ('[[evam|evaṁ]] varṇyamāne [[laukikayāyānugataḥ|laukikanyāyānugataḥ]] [[sūtrartho varni to bbavisyati'|sūtrārtho varṇito bhaviṣyati']] p. 926).[^2041]

Jaimini dealt with the eternal, self-existent and absolutely authoritative character of the Veda as regards Dharma in the first pāda of the first chapter and also dealt with the means of [[koowledge|knowledge]], the eternal character of the relation of words and senses. In the second [[pada|pāda]] of the first adhyāya he pronounced that arthavādas [[whioh|which]] form [[&|a]] very large portion of the Veda were meant to laud the [[Vidhis|vidhis]] with which they were connected and were not to be treated as useless. He also provided that [[mantrag|mantras]] (that formed part of the [[voda|veda]]) had [[&|a]] purpose to serve viz. that of recalling to the mind the meaning of the rites performed, that some mantras like [['oatvāri srñga'20410|'catvāri śrṅgā']][^2041a] (Rg. IV. 58. 3) are laudatory of yāga, by means of a metaphor, that the [[genge|sense]] of some words in the mantras like 'Jarbhari [[turpharita|turpharītū']] (Rg. X. 106. 6) or 'Indraḥ somasya [[kāṇuka'|kāṇukā']] (Rg. VIII. 77. 4) about which it is argued that no sense can be made out of them are really explicable with the help of Nirukta (etymology) and grammar, that some words like 'Kikata', [['Naicātākha',|'Naicāśākha',]] and 'Pramaganda', that are claimed to refer to a country, [[&|a]] city, a king and therefore are claimed to make the mantra (Rg. III. 53. 14) non-eternal, can be explained in a different way. In this way, no part of the Veda is anarthaka (without sense or useless) or [[non eternal|non-eternal]]. The [[Mimārsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] are very keen on avoiding ānarthakya of any Vedic words or sentences.

In the third [[pāde|pāda]] of the first chapter Jaimini deals with the authoritativeness of [[smrtis|smṛtis]], the usages of respectable and learned people (sadācāra), the Vedāṅgas &c.

It appears that before Jaimini composed his sūtra, [[amitis|smṛtis]] had come into prominence and the usages of the śiṣṭas had been recognised as one of the sources of Dharma. The [[Dharma sutras|Dharmasūtras]] of Gautama, Āpastamba, and[^2042] others had declared that the Vedas, the Smṛtis and the usages of those who know the [[Vodas|Vedas]] are the sources of Dharma. Therefore, the Śāntiparva (137.

[[P1257]] 23, 135. 22 in [[Citrao|Citraśao]] ed.) mentions [[Dharmasastras|Dharmaśāstras]] and Anuśāsanaparva (45. 17) quotes [[gathis|gāthās]] from Yama's [[Dharmasastra.|Dharmaśāstra.]] Therefore, Jaimini had to consider [[whatbor|whether]] [[smrtis|smṛtis]] and usages [[wore|were]] authoritative in the matter of Dharma and, if so, to what extent. Even if [[smrtis|smṛtis]] [[wore|were]] held to be unauthoritative, the authoritativeness of the Veda would not be affected; but the very first sūtra (of P. M. S.) postulated that the work would consider the question about the [[characteristios|characteristics]] of Dharma and, therefore, [[startis|smṛtis]] that were called Dharmaśāstra (Manu II. 10) were connected with the discussion about Dharma. Besides, from VI. 7. 6 it follows that[^2043] Jaimini [[know|knew]] Dharmaśāstras as he [[provide es|provides]] that in [[&|a]] Viśvajit the performer cannot make a śūdra [[&|a]] subject of gift simply because he serves a man of higher caste on account of the ordinances of Dharmaśāstra. Even in the Upaniṣads (as in Tai. Up. I. 11) the teacher after the pupil had completed Veda study impresses upon the latter that whenever he would have a doubt about the rites enjoined or about [[propor|proper]] conduct he should follow the conduct of [[brahmanas|brāhmaṇas]] in his country that act after proper consideration, that are devoted to their duties and are not driven to do anything by another, are not harsh of character, that are intent on doing their duties. This amounts to following [['gadācāra'|'sadācāra']] as a source of [[dharma|Dharma]]. Jaimini employs the word 'smṛti' in the sense of works in several sūtras as in VI. 8. 23 ([[whioh|which]] contains the very words that the Āp. Gṛ. [[sutra|sūtra]] employs), IX. 2. 1, XII. 4. 43. Śabara mentions [['empti'|'smṛti']] and the word 'smarati' and [['Amaranti'|'smaranti']] more than a dozen times.

The following passages may be noted. On[^2044] P. M. [[8.|S.]] I. 3. 2 Śabara says "pramāṇaṁ [[smrti)";|smṛtiḥ";]] on P. M. [[8. L|S. I.]] 3. 3 he

[[P1258]] [[montions|mentions]] three [[smrti|smṛti]] rules, two of which [[oan|can]] be said to correspond with [[ortant srostis;|extant smṛtis;]] on PMS [[VI, L 5,|VI. 1. 5,]] while dealing with the question whether lower animals (dogs &c.) have [[alhikāna|adhikāra]] for Vedic rites, [[Sabara|Śabara]] denies their [[adhikara,|adhikāra,]] since they do not study the Veda nor the [[smrtiśāstras|smṛtiśāstras]] (as [[mon|men]] do); on PMS VI. 2. 21-22 (when the question is whether the smārta rules that a teacher should be followed or obeyed and obeisance be made to him and whether one should get up and honour an old man apply to even children whose [[upanayapa|upanayana]] is not performed) Śabara states that smṛti is equal to Veda ([[Vedatulya hi amritih, vaidikā eva padirtha|Vedatulyā hi smṛtiḥ, vaidikā eva padārthā]] smaryanta ityuktam). On [[VI.8.23|VI. 8. 23]] he quotes one quarter of a [[sloka|śloka]] as [[smrti|smṛti]] ([[smaranti-taṣu kaleṣu|smaranti-teṣu kāleṣu]] daivāni-iti). On VI. 7. 31 he says that [[smrti|smṛti]] speaks of gandharvas living for a thousand years. On VI. [[I, 20|1. 20]] [[Sabara|Śabara]] says a woman owns no wealth, if one [[followe smrti,|follows smṛti,]] but is owner of property if śruti be followed. On IX. 2. 2 [[Sabara|Śabara]] says [['naisa smptib pramāṇam, dçstamālā hyeṣa ';|'naiṣā smṛtiḥ pramāṇam, dṛṣṭamūlā hyeṣā';]] on X. 1. 36 [[Sabara|Śabara]] says that from the usage of respectable people [[smrti|smṛti]] is inferred and from [[smrti|smṛti]] Śruti text is inferred; on X. [[I. 42|1. 42]] [[Sabara|Śabara]] says 'Smṛti is stronger than usage'. On X. 3. 47 Śabara [[saya|says]] 'there is a [[smrti|smṛti]] that one should not sell a horse'. In one place [[Sabara|Śabara]] employs the words [['pramāpāyām emptau'|'pramāṇāyāṁ smṛtau']] instead of [['pramāpe smftau'|'pramāṇe smṛtau']] as one would expect and the Tantravārtika is at great pains to show that this lapse on the part of [[Sabara|Śabara]] may be [[somebox|somehow]] justified (pp. 183-184) on I. 3. 3. The [[Baudhayana|Baudhāyana]] Dh. [[8. (1.1, 19–26 )|S. (I. 1. 19–26)]] sets out five [[practicas|practices]] peculiar to the countries of the South (Southern India) and five others peculiar to the North and says that if one belonging to the South follows any of the usages peculiar to the North and *vice versa* he would be guilty of sin. Vide H. of [[Db.|Dh.]] Vol. III. p. 858 and note 1663 for the passage from Baud. Dh. [[8.|S.]] The objector [[sayg 204|says]][^2045] that [[smrtis|smṛtis]] should be

The words [[कर्तृसामाग्यात्|कर्तृसामान्यात्]] are explained by the [[भाग्यकार|भाष्यकार]] as [[कर्तृसामाग्यात्स्मृति-वैदिकपदार्धयोः|कर्तृसामान्यात्स्मृति-वैदिकपदार्थयोः]] i. e. the people who perform Vedic rites and also observe the [[Susti|Smṛti]] prescriptions are the same; they would not do [[80|so]] unless they firmly believe that [[smrti|smṛti]] prescriptions are based on Vedic [[autho. rity|authority]] though it may not be possible in every case to point out Vedic passages expressly or impliedly [[referriag|referring]] to them. [[Herau op HE|मेधातिथि on मनु]] II. 6 clearly puts it, quoting from [[bis|his]] own work called [[for , the following verso: starea Hot gettarara: aftue: aga aghtatereganat yrit: ajang tae words under Ilaed are taken from g. A. & 1.3.2, H (11.7) has 'q: esfrena|स्मृतिविवेक, the following verse: प्रामाण्यकारणं मुख्यं वेदविद्भिः परिग्रहः । तदुक्तं कर्तृसामान्यादनुमानं श्रुतेः प्रति ॥; the words underlined are taken from पू. मी. सू. I. 3. 2. मनुस्मृति (II. 7) has 'यः कश्चित्कस्यचिद्धर्मो मनुना परिकीर्तितः। स सर्वोऽभिहितो वेदे सर्वज्ञानमयो हि सः॥. मेधातिथि, गोविन्दराज and some other commentators take सः as referring to Veda, while Kullūka refers it to मनु. Vide H. of Dh. III. p. 828 n. 1612 for another meaning of these words.]]

[[P1259]] discarded, since [[smrtis|smṛtis]] were composed by men (i. e. they are [[pauruçeya|pauruṣeya]] and not [[apaurugeya,|apauruṣeya,]] as the Veda is) and men are often deluded and forgetful. This is the main basis of the objector. To this the reply is that for prescriptions of the [[smtpis,|smṛtis,]] there are passages in the Veda that suggest some of the [[smrti|smṛti]] rules [[8. g,|e. g.]] that the [[Astakā srāddha|Aṣṭakā śrāddha]] was [[provalont|prevalent]] long before the [[smrtis|smṛtis]] is indicated by the Vedic mantra [['yām janaḥ pratinan danti'.|'yāṁ janāḥ pratinandanti'.]][^2046] The [[Usages|usages]] about obeying the teacher, about [[provid ing|providing]] cisterns for travellers have a [[seon|seen]] purpose, viz. the benefit of others. Even the Veda mentions 'prapā' (Rg. IX. [[4.1|4. 1]] [['dhanya nniva|'dhanvanniva]] prapā [[asi meaning'|asi' meaning]] [[' O Agnil|'O Agni!]] thou art like a prapā in a desert'. The Tantravārtika is most exhaustive on this and the following sūtras, differs from the Bhāṣyakāra in many places, finds fault with him and gives alternative matters as the subjects for discussion. He [[puta|puts]] forward two theories about [[smrti|smṛti]] provisions for which it is impossible to suggest Vedic indications. He says (firstly) that [[Smrti 2017|Smṛti]][^2047] prescriptions may be based on lost Vedic [[sākhas,|śākhās,]] or (2) they may be based on texts contained in the very parts of the [[Voda|Veda]] that are available at present. If any one asks [['bow|'how]] is it that they are not found', Kumārila gives the reply: the several branches of the Veda are [[soattered|scattered]] about (in many distant territories), [[mon|men]] are negligent, and the texts are contained in different sections of the [[Voda;|Veda;]] on account of these one cannot point out the texts that are the basis of the [[emptis,|smṛtis.]]

That many Vedic texts were lost or are unavailable now was the view even before the days of the [[Ap. Dh. 8. (I, 4. 12. 10),|Āp. Dh. S. (I. 4. 12. 10),]] which states 'rites were promulgated in the Brāhmaṇa texts, but the exact words [[( of the Brābmana texts)|(of the Brāhmaṇa texts)]] are lost and have to be inferred from the performance of the rites (or from procedure [[presoribed in Smrtis).|prescribed in Smṛtis)'.]][^2048] There was danger in relying on the

I. 7]] p. 14 (without name).

[^2048]: [[बाहाणोका विघयस्तेषामुत्सनाः|ब्राह्मणोक्ता विधयस्तेषामुत्सन्नाः]] पाठाः [[प्रयोगावभुमीयन्ते।|प्रयोगादनुमीयन्ते।]] यत्र [[प्रीत्युपलब्धिता atro APETI S 4. 8. &|प्रीत्युपलब्धितः प्रवृत्तिर्न तत्र शास्त्रमस्ति। आप. ध. सू.]] I. 4. 12. 10–11.

[[P1260]] theory that [[Smrtis|Smṛtis]] were based on Vedic texts that are lost (or [[disappeared ),|disappeared),]] because that very argument might be urged by heterodox sects like the Bauddhas.[^2049] Therefore, Kumārila put forward the other theory viz. the basis of smṛtis are texts that are not found in the existing vedic texts because the Vedic [[sūkhās|śākhās]] are scattered about (as said in the verse quoted above).

This whole subject about Smṛtis according to the views of the [[Mimamsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] has been dealt with at great length by the author in H. of Dh. vol. [[II,|III.]] pp. 827–841. Therefore, only a few examples and conclusions may be mentioned here. Śabara himself proposes that PMS I. 3. 4[^2050] should form a separate [[Adhikarana|*Adhikaraṇa*]] by itself and puts forward an important maxim viz. where one can find out or ascribe a visible motive for an act one should not postulate an unseen Vedic text or a transcendental [[motive. 2051|motive.]][^2051] The result of Śabara's dealing with PMS I. 3. 3–4 is neatly put by the [[Sāstradipikā|Śāstradīpikā]] as follow; Smṛti rules that are opposed to Śruti rules and such smṛti provisions as can be shown to [[bave|have]] a clear worldly motive are not authoritative or binding, while the rest of smṛti texts are authoritative. This doctrine is older than the [[Ap. Dh S. (1. 4. 10. 12)|Āp. Dh S. (I. 4. 10. 12)]] which provides 'where men act because they find pleasure in doing that act there Śāstra has no scope'. [[Kunıārila|Kumārila]] does not agree with Śabara. He says that visible and invisible or spiritual motives are often inextricably mixed up. [["Unbusking|'Unhusking]] rice grains' has a visible purpose in order that they may be well boiled and the boiled rice may be offered in a sacrifice. In this act then there is a visible purpose and [[get|yet]] this act is prescribed by the Veda. In a trenchant, finely worded and very accommodating passage redolent of a cosmopolitan outlook and the desire to give [[avon|even]] the devil his due, Kumārila examines (in pp. 166–168) all the known classes of Sanskrit works with regard to their usefulness

[^2049]: यदि तु प्रलीनशाखामूलता [[कल्येत सतस्तासां पदादिस्पतीनामपि|कल्प्येत ततस्तासां बुद्धादिस्मृतीनामपि]] तद्द्वारा [[stallere Hocā i 2.|प्रामाण्यं प्रसज्येत। तन्त्रवा.]] on I. 3. 1 p. 163.

Vide H. of Dh. vol. III. [[P.|p.]] 837 [[3. 1628.|n. 1628.]] It appears that [[FAITI|कुमारिल]] thought that Śabara stated his case [[el|as]] merely a counter blast and his own opinion is that a smṛti text [[wbich|which]] has a visible purpose may still be [[Vedawūla.|Vedamūla.]]

[[P1261]] and relation to Veda and ordinary worldly experience. Only a few sentences will be set out here and in the notes. Therefore, he provides that all Smṛtis are authoritative according to the [[pur pose they servex|purpose they serve.]] Those parts of Smṛtis that are concerned with Dharma and [[Moksa|Mokṣa]] have the Veda as their source, while [[what over|whatever]] parts of [[Smstis|Smṛtis]] are concerned with wealth (artha) and worldly pleasures ([[kūma)|*kāma*)]] are based on worldly practices. The same rule applies to the hortatory passages of Itihāsa (the Mahābhārata) and Purāṇas, both of which are often spoken of as Smṛti. The episodes and tales in these two should be taken [[ag|as]] arthavādas. Then he refers to the descriptions of the divisions of the earth and the [[vamsas (dynastio lists)|vaṁśas (dynastic lists)]] (both of which are among the subjects treated of in the [[Puranas)|Purāṇas)]] and sets out the purposes they might serve. The six Vedāṅgas (grammar, [[metrios,|metrics,]] etymology, astronomy &c.) are useful as kratvartha and puruṣārtha, and [[Mimārgā|Mīmāṁsā]] and Nyāya (logic) have been [[establi- - shed|established]] (worked up) from worldly experience by means of [[pratyaksa|pratyakṣa]] (direct perception) and inference based upon the technique evolved by a continuous and unbroken line of learned [[mon;|men;]] that no single person would have been able to put together for the first time such a large collection of arguments[^2052] (as in the [[Mimāṁsāśāstra ).|Mīmāṁsāśāstra).]] For the necessity of [[Nyaya ( in the interpreta tion|Nyāya (in the interpretation]] of the Veda) he relies upon Manu XII. 105–106. Kumārila [[ig|is]] prepared to concede that philosophical systems in which [[aro|are]] postulated pradhāna and puruṣa ([[Saṅkhya)|(Sāṅkhya)]] or the [[supremo|supreme]] Lord or [[atonas (Vaiseṣika)|atoms (Vaiśeṣika)]] should be deemed to explain the creation and dissolution of the world, should be looked upon as exhibiting perceived, minute and gross substances divided into causes and effects due to the knowledge derived from mantras and arthavādas. The purpose of these is to make known the distinction of svarga and yāga as result and cause. The purpose of the description of creation and dissolution is to show the

I. 3. 2); मीमांसा तु लोकादेव प्रत्यक्षाद्यनुमानादिभिरविच्छिन्नसंप्रदायपण्डितव्यवहारे प्रवृत्ता । न हि कश्चिदपि प्रथममेतावन्तं युक्तिकलापमुपसंहर्तुं क्षमः। एतेन न्यायविस्तरं व्याख्यातम्। तन्त्रवा.]] p. 167 on I. 3. 2. [[M, M. Jba|M. M. Jha]] in his translation of Tantravārtika (p. 121) translates [['mimāsisā|'mīmāṁsā]] is based on the Veda, upon [[ordioary|ordinary]] experience &c. But [[fo|in]] the [[Ānan,|Ānan.]] edition there are no Sanskrit words corresponding to the words [[onderlined.|underlined.]] This passage indicates that Kumārila firmly held that many authors had tried their [[bands|hands]] at composing sūtra works on [[Mimārsa|Mīmāṁsā]] before the present P. M. S. [[emerged..|emerged.]]

[[P1262]] distinction between the power of [[luok|luck]] and human effort. He [[goos|goes]] even a step further and is prepared to admit that even heterodox hypotheses of Bauddhas such as the theory that only [[Vijñana|Vijñāna]] (cognition) exists, that everything is in an eternal flux and that there is no (permanent) soul, [[whicb|which]] (theories) sprang from the [[arthavada|arthavāda]] passages in the Upaniṣads, 'serve the purpose of inducing people to give up extreme attachment to sensual pleasures and are useful and authoritative in their own ways.

He sums up the distinction by stating that as regards those [[smrtis|smṛtis]] (or portions of them) in which the fruit or result promised cannot possibly be experienced in this life, but where the result is promised as fructifying in the remote future [[(i. o.|(i. e.]] after death), an [[inferenco|inference]] as to their being based on Veda may be drawn, but, as in the case of scorpion [[lore 2013|lore]][^2053] (i. e. incantations for curing the sting of a scorpion), works dealing with matters that can be seen are authoritative only because the result can be perceived in other men similarly stung.

The medieval Dharmaśāstra works take up this discussion about the distinction between smṛtis based upon the Veda and those on perceptible motives or purposes. For example, the [[Kalpa taru (Brahma-cari-kāṇda|Kalpataru (Brahma-cāri-kāṇḍa]] p. 30) and Aparārka ([[pp. 626-627)|pp. 626–627)]] quote verses from Bhaviṣyapurāṇa (Brāhmaparva chap. 181, [[22-31)|22–31)]] that divide the contents of [[smrtis|smṛtis]] in five categories and exemplify that division. The Sm. C. II. p. 24 quotes two of [[those|these]] and the Paribhāṣāprakāśa of Mitramiśra (p. 19) quotes all of them. Vide H. of Dh. Vol. III. p. 840 note 1634 for all the verses.

Kumārila (pp. 194–196 of Tantravārtika) holds that the passages quoted by [[Sabara|Śabara]] on [[1. 3. 3|I. 3. 3]] are not really opposed to Veda at all and that the proper subject for discussion under I. 3. 3–4 is the authoritativeness in matters of Dharma of the schools[^2054] of [[Saṅkhya,|Sāṅkhya,]] Yoga, [[Pasupata,|Pāśupata,]] Pāñcarātra and [[Sakyas|Śākyas]]

[[P1263]] and he holds that all these are outside the pale of the three Vedas and are to be [[disoarded|discarded]] as unauthoritative, although they contain a few [[mattors|matters]] such as [[ahimsa,|ahiṁsā,]] truthfulness, self-control, charity and compassion, that agree with [[śrubi|śruti]] and [[smrti.|smṛti.]] It will have been seen from the above that Kumārila was aware of the emphasis placed by Buddhists on some moral values but he differed from them in many other matters, that he was ready to admit that Buddhist works had some value and he did not preach that they should be burnt or destroyed. Therefore, it appears that his attitude was far from being one of total hatred and persecution of the Buddhists, as alleged by Taranatha cited above on p. 1010 n. 1645.

[[Sabara|Śabara]] explains that [[sutras 5-7 of P. M, S, I. 3|sūtras 5–7 of P. M. S. I. 3]] are[^2055] concerned with certain religious acts such as ācamana (sipping water) when one [[Breezes|sneezes]] in the midst of [[8 rito,|a rite,]] the wearing of [[. Yajõopayita|Yajñopavīta]] (the sacred thread), the use of the right hand alone in all acts. These interfere with the sequence and quick performance of the subordinate items in a religious rite, says the objector. [[Sabara|Śabara]] establishes that there is no substance in the [[objection|objection.]] Kumārila feels that [[Sabara|Śabara]] is not right as regards the three instances cited by him. He splits the three sūtras into two [[adhikaranas|adhikaraṇas]] (topics for consideration); sūtras 5 and 6 deal [[(aco, to Kumārila)|(acc. to Kumārila)]] with the contention that those precepts of Buddha and other founders of unorthodox sects, such as the construction of monasteries and parks, the insistence on desirelessness ([[vairāgya),|*vairāgya*),]] the continuous practice of meditation, [[ahimsa,|ahiṁsā,]] truthfulness, restraint of senses, charity and kindness are such as are laid down also by the Veda, are not opposed to the ideas of [[sistas|śiṣṭas]] and cannot rouse the antipathy of those who know the Vedas and that therefore those parts of the unorthodox systems should be deemed to be authoritative. This contention is [[ropelled by Kumarlla|repelled by Kumārila]] with the remark that only 14 (four Vedas, 6 [[Vedāógas,|Vedāṅgas,]] Purāṇas, Nyāya, [[Mimārsā|Mīmāṁsā]] and Dharmaśāstra) or 18 (adding four Upavedas) have been accepted by the Vedic śiṣṭas as authoritative in matters of Dharma and the works of [[Bauddhas, and other sects|Bauddhas and other sects]] are not included therein.[^2056] Kumārila gives [[&|a]] telling example viz. milk, though by itself pure and useful,

[^2056]: Vide Yāj. I. 3 for the 14 [[vidyas|vidyās]] and H. of Dh. [[vol, III. p. 10 note 17 for the four Upavedas vix. Ayurveda, Dhanurveda, Gandharvaveda nod Arthadastra.|vol. III. p. 10 note 17 for the four Upavedas viz. Āyurveda, Dhanurveda, Gāndharvaveda and Arthaśāstra.]]

[[P1264]] becomes useless and unacceptable, when poured into a bag of dog-skin.

Kumārila holds that [[sutra 7 of PMS I. 3|sūtra 7 of PMS I. 3]] is an [[adhikarana|adhikaraṇa]] by itself and is concerned with the authoritativeness of sadācāra (the customs and usages of [[sistas).|śiṣṭas).]] His position as elaborated in the Tantravārtika is that those usages alone are authoritative that are not opposed to express Vedic texts, that are practised by śiṣṭas under the belief that they are right conduct (dharma) and for which no visible motive (such as pleasure or the satisfaction of [[desiros|desires]] or the acquisition of wealth) can be predicated. Śiṣṭas are only those that perform the religious [[ritos|rites]] and duties expressly enjoined by the Veda. The practices traditionally handed down from generation to generation that are observed by [["sistas|śiṣṭas]] (as defined above) with the conviction that they form part of Dharma must be regarded as Dharma and as leading to heaven. The Tantravārtika remarks that practices do not become authoritative merely by the fact that no visible motive or purpose can be postulated for them; but they become so only when they are observed by śiṣṭas as part of [[Dharma. 2057|Dharma.]][^2057] Many activities such as agriculture, service or trade, that are the means of securing wealth and pleasures and such actions [[28|as]] eating sumptuous food, drinking, sleeping on soft beds, [[possoso ing|possessing]] a charming house or garden, all of which are common to Āryas and [[Mlecchag|Mlecchas]] are not deemed by any one to be part of Dharma and it does not follow that because a few actions of śiṣṭas are accepted as Dharma, all their actions are to be looked upon as Dharma. Kumārila quotes the advice that a man should follow the path by which his father, grand-father and other ancestors went, provided it is the path of the good, following which he will not come to harm.[^2058]

Very intricate questions arise with regard to the relative force of śruti (Veda), [[smrti|smṛti]] and sadācāra (usages observed by [[śigtas|śiṣṭas]] as defined above and in Manu XII. 109). The Mit. on Yāj. [[17|I. 7]] (laying down five sources of Dharma viz. śruti, [[smrti,|smṛti,]]

[[vol,|vol.]] III. p. 857 [[2.|n.]] 1661.

[[P1265]] sadācāra, and two more) states the general rule that in case of conflict each preceding one is more powerful than each [[suoceed ing|succeeding]] one. Manu (I. 12) states that, for those who desire to know Dharma, [[Sruti (Veda)|Śruti (Veda)]] is the highest authority. Therefore, in case of conflict between śruti and [[smrti|smṛti]] the former [[prevaila|prevails.]] There are a [[fow|few]] exceptions [[oven|even]] to this [[olear|clear]] rule which may be illustrated later on. But where two [[smptis|smṛtis]] are in conflict, the ordinary rule, following the [[Sodasi-nyāya|Ṣoḍaśī-nyāya]] and the words of Gautama [[I 5|I. 5]] (Tulyabalavirodhe [[vikalpah),|vikalpaḥ),]] should be an option. Many works on Dharmaśāstra must have been composed long before 500 B. C., since Gautama mentions Manu (in [[21.7)|21. 7)]] and 'ācāryāḥ' (in 3. 35, 4. 18) and [[Ap. Dh. 8. (I. 6. 19. 2-12)|Āp. Dh. S. (I. 6. 19. 2-12)]] mentions no less than nine individual authors' [[cpinions|opinions]] on the single question as to whose food may be eaten. Manu ([[in IL 16)|in III. 16)]] [[montions|mentions]] three views held by four sages on the position of a brāhmaṇa who marries a śūdra wife or has a son or child from her. A striking example of a conflict between [[smrtis|smṛtis]] is the rule of Manu (III. 13), [[Baud. Dh. 8. I. 8. %,|Baud. Dh. S. I. 8. 2,]] Viṣṇu Dh. [[8.|S.]] 24. 1-4, Vas. I. 25, Pār. [[Gļ. L.|Gṛ. I.]] 4 allowing anuloma marriages and [[permit ting|permitting]] a brāhmaṇa to have a [[gūdra|śūdra]] woman as [[&|a]] wife and the [[emphatio|emphatic]] statement of Yāj. [[I,|I.]] 56-57 that it does not agree with those that declare that persons of the three higher classes [[oan|can]] take a śūdra woman as wife. Later writers of [[smrtis|smṛtis]] and nibandhas should have said that on account of this conflict there is an option. But they do not say so. They struck upon [[vari ous|various]] devices to wriggle out of such apparent contradictions. The first device was declared by [[Bphaspati|Bṛhaspati]] (about 500 A. D.) that the [[Manusmrti oocupies a pro-eminent|Manusmṛti occupies a pre-eminent]] position as it [[correct ly represents the songo|correctly represents the sense]] (the real view) of the Vedas and that a [[smrti|smṛti]] which is opposed to the teaching of Manu is not held in [[esteem. 2039|esteem.]][^2059] But this was not satisfactory and some other devices [[wore|were]] resorted to. One was to insert passages in [[Manusmrti|Manusmṛti]] itself and in other works opposing what had already been declared as the law. Two examples may be [[oited.|cited.]] As against Manu [[III, 13|III. 13]] (referred to immediately above) we find in the extant text (III. 14–19) verses very strongly [[condemping|condemning]] persons of the three

[^2059]: [[वेदार्थोपनिवन्धत्वात् मामाण्यं तु मनुस्मृतो।|वेदार्थोपनिवन्धृत्वात् प्रामाण्यं तु मनुस्मृतौ ।]] मन्वर्थविपरीता या स्मृतिः सा न [[TETÈN Tusa|प्रशस्यते ॥ बृहस्पति]] q. by [[po|अपरार्क]] p. 628 on [[7.|या.]] II. 21 and by [[S OD AZ I.1. Harta 11. 7|कुल्लूक on मनु I. 1. मनुस्मृति II. 7]] (quoted above in note 2045) claims that whatever it says on dharma has all been declared in the Veda. The [[worla|मनुस्मृति]] frequently echoes the very words of the Veda; e. g. Manu I. 31 and Ṛg. X. 90. 12, [[Mann|Manu]] II. 2 and [[77..|वाज. सं.]] 40. 2, [[Mano|Manu]] IX. 8 (about [[Jaya)|Jāyā)]] and Ait. Br. 33. 1 (7th [[gathā), Mang|gāthā), Manu]] IX. 32 and Ait. Br. 33. 3 (4th [[gathā).|gāthā).]]

[[P1266]] higher [[wrnais|varṇas]] taking a śūdra woman as wife. Manu allowed the practice of *niyoga* (in IX. 59-62), but the extant Manusmṛti (IX. 64-68) condemns it in no measured terms. These [[contra.. dictory|contradictory]] statements were known to Bṛhaspati who expressly says that Manu permits *niyoga* and himself forbids[^2060] it and assigns [[&|a]] reason viz. that in former ages ([[Kșta and Tretë)|(Kṛta and Tretā)]] people practised [[tagay|tapas]] and were endowed with knowledge while in the Dvāpara and Kali ages men have lost the powers possessed by men of the past ages and therefore *niyoga* is forbidden. [[Yājñar valkya|Yājñavalkya]] himself proposes ([[11. 21)|(II. 21)]] that when two [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are in conflict [['Teasoning baged|'reasoning based]] on the practices of elders was of greater force'. Nārada has a similar rule.[^2061] Nārada has a similar rule. Another device was to declare that the nature of Dharma differed in each of the four yugas and that in the [[krta, tretā, dvāpara and kali|kṛta, tretā, dvāpara and kali]] ages the Dharmas respectively promulgated by Manu, Gautama, [[Saṅkha-Likbita|Śaṅkha-Likhita]] and Parāśara were to [[provail. 2062 įThis|prevail.]][^2062] This also could not solve all difficulties, since medieval commentators and digests found that even what was allowed by Parāśara (such as permitting a brāhmaṇa to eat food at the [[2463|house]][^2063] of such [[sūdras|śūdras]] as his [[dada,|dāsa,]] cowherd, barber, family friend and [[& cultiva: tor|a cultivator]] of his land for a half share of the crops, permission for a married woman to re-marry in certain circumstances), came to be disapproved of or condemned by people. Another expedient in cases of conflict among [[smrtis|smṛtis]] was provided by Gobhila viz. where there is a conflict of ([[smrti)|smṛti)]] passages, the opinion of the majority should prevail.[^2064]

[^2064]: [[Panahon at TTATI FT THIG i storsegrat q. by TEATHEW|विरोधा यत्र वाक्यानां प्रामाण्यं तत्र भूयसाम् । गोभिलस्मृति q. by मलमासतत्त्व]] p. 767.

[[P1267]] As shown above [[smrtis|smṛtis]] had been composed before 500 B. C. and they continued to be compiled till about 900 or 1000 A. D. [[1. e.|i. e.]] for over 1500 [[yeara.|years.]] Yāj. (I. 4-5) mentions 19 [[smrtia|smṛtis]] including his own. Vide H. of Dh. Vol. I. [[pp. 132-135|pp. 132–135]] for the enumeration of different numbers of smṛtis in different works. At least a hundred [[smrtis|smṛtis]] if not more can be named. During the long period of over 1500 years vast changes in the religious and social ideas of the Indian people, in their customs and usages had occurred. Buddhism arose, flourished and [[disappea red|disappeared]] from India, the caste system became rigid in the matter of food, marriage and social behaviour; Vedic [[ritos,|rites,]] divinities worshipped and [[languagó|language]] underwent great transformations, animal sacrifices, though occasionally performed, had ceased to be looked upon as meritorious. The religious literature had therefore to be recast to suit new ideals, new worship and new patrons (viz. the common people who had [[coased|ceased]] to follow ancient ritual and worship). [[Smptis|Smṛtis]] reflected the changes in ideas, beliefs, worship and usages that occurred from time to time and naturally gave rise to great conflicts among [[them selves.|themselves.]] At last it appears that the learned men of the 10th and following [[conturies|centuries]] of the Christian era hit upon the plan of providing that certain customs and practices, though formerly allowed and practised, were harmful in the Kali age. A theory was put forward that great sages came together at the beginning of the Kali age and declared that certain [[ribes,|rites,]] customs and [[usagos,|usages,]] though allowed in past ages, should be prohibited in the Kali age.[^2065] The actions prohibited or to be avoided in the Kali age (about 55 in number and therefore called *Kalivarjya*) have been set out and discussed at length in H. of [[Dh: VOL|Dh. vol.]] III. [[pp. 926-967.|pp. 926–967.]] It is clear from Medhātithi's bhāṣya on Manu (IX. 112) that long before his time (9th century A. D.) there were writers who had condemned *govadha* (in Madhuparka [[&o.).|&c.),]] *niyoga* and the giving of a larger share to the eldest son and expressed the view that those practices were only allowed in bygone ages.

This subject of *Kalivarjya* requires some serious [[considera tion.|consideration.]] Three of the Kalivarjyas are the prohibition of *niyoga*, of

[^2065]: [[एतानि लोकस्य कलेरादौ महामभिः। निवतितानि कर्माणि व्यवस्थापूर्वक Gū: 1 lines 39-40 from the appendix (pp. 1014-1015) to vol. III. (of H. of Db.); algata-rain-saugau gufet TIET: I AM. ON HE IX. 112; पथा नियोगधों नो नानुपायावधोऽपि च। तपोद्धारविभागोपि नैव संप्रति वर्तते । १. by FHETTATI OD TT|एतानि लोकगुप्त्यर्थं कलेरादौ महात्मभिः। निवर्तितानि कर्माणि व्यवस्थापूर्वकं बुधैः ॥ lines 39-40 from the appendix (pp. 1014–1015) to vol. III. (of H. of Dh.); गोवध-ज्येष्ठभाग-नियोगप्रभृतय उपाधिना नानुष्ठेयाः। मेधा. on मनु IX. 112; यथा नियोगधर्मो नो नानुबन्ध्यावधोऽपि च। तथोद्धारविभागोऽपि नैव संप्रति वर्तते ॥ q. by मिताक्षरा on या]] II. 117.

[[P1268]] the offering of the [[anubandhya|anubandhyā]] cow (after avabhṛtha) in [[Jyoti stoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] and of the allotment of the largest share (of ancestral wealth) to the eldest son. All these three were either enjoined or permitted by the Veda. From Ṛg. X. 40. 2 it follows that [[&|a]] sonless widow raised a son for the sake of the spiritual benefit of her husband's soul by sexual intercourse with the husband's brother.[^2066] The Tai. [[8.|S.]] (III. 1. 9. 4) has two contradictory passages viz. 'Manu divided his wealth among his sons' (without making any distinction among them) and 'therefore they endow the [[oldest|eldest]] son with (the paternal) wealth' (Tai. S. [[II, 5. 2. 7).|II. 5. 2. 7).]] In this last case it may be argued that as two Vedic passages are in conflict there is an option. But from very early times there is a prohibition against allowing the whole [[ances tral|ancestral]] wealth or a major portion of it to the [[oldest|eldest]] son. Āpastamba quotes both[^2067] Vedic passages, holds that equal division among sons is the proper rule and remarks that allowing the eldest son to take the whole or a major portion of ancestral estate is [[pro hibited|prohibited]] by the [[Sastras.|Śāstras.]] Among actions forbidden in the Kali age but practised in Vedic times some striking ones (apart from the three already cited above) may be mentioned here: (1) being initiated for Sattras (which were sacrifices extending from [[1%|12]] days to 12 years or even more and which could be performed by [[brāhmapas|brāhmaṇas]] alone); Jaimini[^2068] deals with them at some length in VI. 6. 16–32 and other places. It is remarkable that neither Śabara nor [[Kumarila|Kumārila]] says anything against [[Sattredikṣā|Sattradīkṣā]] as prohibited in Kali. So it was not among generally recognized Kalivarjyas till at least the 8th century A. D. (2) killing of a cow or bull. There were several occasions on which this was

[^2066]: [[et af et fut a t a 19 qua furu N *|को वां शयुत्रा विधवेव देवरं मर्यं न योषा कृणुते सधस्थ आ ॥ ऋ.]] X. 40. 2. For the treatment of *niyoga* from ancient times, vide H. of Dh. vol. II. pp. 599–607. Some regard this verse as indicating re-marriage and [[got niyoga.|not niyoga.]] But that does not appear to be correct. [[Maou|Manu]] (IX. 65) states that *niyoga* [[la|is]] nowhere mentioned among the mantras relating to marriage nor is the remarriage of a widow referred to in the procedure of marriage. But Gautama and some other sūtrakāras [[aod|and]] even Yāj. [[(1. 68-69 )|(I. 68–69)]] prescribe in detail the procedure and conditions governing [[oiyoga ;|niyoga;]] all writers are entirely silent about the procedure of the re-marriage of widows. Therefore, it has to be said that Ṛg. X. 40. 2 was rightly held by ancient [[sortis es referriog|smṛtis as referring]] to the practice of *niyoga*.

[^2067]: [[vugt arare che ... Forfatarang... Ag: gerait ari gare दित्यविशेषेण भूयते । अथापि तस्माज्येष्ठ पुत्रं धनेन निरवसाययम्तीत्येकाच्यते। ... सर्वेहि viguit mifta: 1 24. 8. &.|ज्येष्ठो दायाद इत्येके । ... तच्छास्त्रैर्विप्रतिषिद्धम् । ... मनुः पुत्रेभ्यो दायं व्यभजदित्यविशेषेण श्रूयते । अथापि तस्माज्ज्येष्ठं पुत्रं धनेन निरवसाययन्तीत्येकवच्छ्रूयते । ... सर्वे हि धर्मयुक्ता भागिनः। आप. ध. सू.]] II. 6. 14. 10–12, 14.

[^2068]: Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. [[Pp. 1239-1246|pp. 1239–1246]] for details about Sattras.

[[P1269]] done in the Vedic age. Vide H. of Dh. Vol. III. pp. 939–940. As flesh-eating came to be looked down upon, cow-sacrifices became most abhorrent and medieval [[Kallvarjya|Kalivarjya]] texts only register a prohibition which had been acted upon for centuries before them. (3) The employment of cups of wine in the [[Sautrāmaṇl|Sautrāmaṇī]] sacrifice.[^2069] Jaimini, Śabara and the [[sup-tikā|Ṭup-ṭīkā]] of Kumārila describe it and both Śabara and Kumārila refer to the offering of cups of wine in it. Therefore this rite must have become Kalivarjya after Kumārila's day. (4) Offering an animal with the recital of Vedic mantras to the bridegroom, to a guest and in honour of [[pitys.|pitṛs.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. 542–546 for Madhuparka in which a bull or a cow was offered according to the Ait. Br. and Vol. [[II, pp. 945-46|III. pp. 945–46]] for this Kalivarjya. Manu (V. 41–44) allows the killing of animals in Madhuparka, in sacrifices and in rites for the [[pitrs (Manes)|pitṛs (Manes)]] and gods and emphasizes that sacrificing an animal as laid down by the Veda is no [[hiñsā|hiṁsā]] but is [[ahiinsa.|ahiṁsā.]] Yāj. (I. 258–260) prescribes the periods of time for which [[pitrs|pitṛs]] are gratified by the offering of sacrificial food (such as rice or sesame), various kinds of fish and the flesh of several animals. The Mit. [[Wag 2070|was]][^2070] constrained to observe that, although Yāj. shows that sacrificial food (rice [[&c),|&c.),]] flesh and honey are offerings in [[Sraddha|Śrāddha]] for all varṇas, yet (in its day) the rule established by Pulastya should be followed viz. food fit for muni (i. e. rice) should be offered by brāhmaṇas, flesh by kṣatriyas and [[vaisyas|vaiśyas]] and honey by śūdras.

According to the [[Pūrvamimāmsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] Veda is eternal, self-existent and of absolute authority; one fails to understand how sages could have authority at the beginning of the Kali-yuga to prohibit what the Veda enjoined or permitted. [[This-geerns|This seems]] to be a fiction invented to accommodate as Dharma the changes in people's ideas and practices that had occurred. It would have been honest and straightforward if the writers on Dharmaśāstra had

[^2069]: For reference to [[Sautrāmaṇi,|Sautrāmaṇī,]] vide above p. 1079 [[aote|note]] 1750. Jai. III. 5. 14–15 and IV. 3. 29–31 deal with it. The Tai. S. V. [[6,3-4,|6. 3-4,]] states [['Agaim citvā sau trāmagyā yajeta'.|'Agnim citvā sautrāmaṇyā yajeta'.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. II. pp. 1224–1228 for a description of this sacrifice, which is a combination of an iṣṭi with animal [[sacriṛce,|sacrifice.]] The peculiarity of this rite is that, along with [[cape of mallk,|cups of milk,]] cups of [[wide|wine]] are offered to [[Afvins,|Aśvins,]] [[Sarasvati|Sarasvatī]] and Indra.

I. 260-261.|याज्ञ. I. 260–261.]]

[[P1270]] said that changed [[ciroumstances|circumstances]] required that the words of the Veda or of the old smṛtis should not be followed. And there is nothing novel or revolutionary in saying this. Both Manu[^2071] and [[Yājāavalkya|Yājñavalkya]] prescribe that one should not observe but give up, what was (once) deemed to be dharma, if it had become hateful to the people and if it would end in unhappiness and not lead to the attainment of heaven. Even the Mitākṣarā follows this precept of the two [[smrtis|smṛtis]] and expressly says that unequal distribution (at partition) of ancestral wealth, though found in the Śāstras, should not be followed because people had come to hate it. It may be noted that the word used by Yāj. and others is 'lokavidviṣṭa' or [[ 'lokavikrusta'|'lokavikruṣṭa']] (hated or reviled by the people) and not [['sista-ridvista',|'śiṣṭa-vidviṣṭa',]] the idea being that even if orthodox learned [[pandits|paṇḍits]] insist that people must follow what the Veda and [[smrtis|smṛtis]] declare to be Dharma, common people [[way|may]] give up practices condemned by them or hateful to them. This attitude recognizes the historical facts that practices change in the course of centuries and common people are entitled to ignore the [[diota|dicta]] even of the Veda (much more of the [[smrtis).|smṛtis).]] To the question why should one not be allowed to marry one's mother's sister or

[^2071]: [[परित्यजेवर्धकामौ यो स्यातां धर्मवर्जितो। धर्म चाप्यसुखोदकै लोकविकटमेव च। मनु. IV. 176: विष्णुपुराण III. 11.7 reads धर्मपीडाकरी चुप and विहिष्ट कर्मणा मनसा वाचा यस्ना धर्म समाचरेत् । अवार्य लोकविद्विष्टं धर्पमप्याचरेत तु॥ याज्ञ. I. 156. Vide विष्णुधर्मसूत्र 71. 17-21 (परिहरेत् ) धर्मविरुद्धौ चार्थकामो लोकविद्विष्टं च धार्म्यमपि। बृहनारदीयपु. I. 24. 12 has the verse कर्मणा मनसा ... चरेस त सर्वलोकविरुद्ध धर्ममप्याचरेन तु । कूर्म• I. 2.54. 'विषमो विभागः शानहष्टस्तथापि लोकविशिष्टत्वासाइडेयः। मिता. on या. II. 117; on याज्ञ. I. 156 the मिता. says 'धये विहितमपि लोकपिदिष्टं लोकाभिशास्तिजननं मधुपर्कगोवधादिकं नाचरेत् यस्भावस्वर्यमग्रीषोमीयवस्वर्गसाधनं न भवति।।. Vide also मिता. on या. III. 8, which says about the different days of impurity for sapindas of the 4th, 5th, 6th or 7th degreoprescribed by a Sarti that it should be discarded 'तद्विगीतत्वासादरणीयम् । यमप्यविनीत तथापि मधुपकाइपलालम्भनवल्लोकविद्विष्टलाजानुडेयम् ।': the स्मतिच. (I. p. 71) says 'न अमः शाशतो न पारणेयेति किंत लोकविरुद्धत्वात्। पञ्च धर्म्यमपि लोकपिरवं तमादयम् । यदुक्तं मनुना-अस्वार्य, पराहमिहिरोपि लोकाचारस्तावदादो विचिन्यो देशे देशे या स्थितिः the Firetra. Vide above p. 555 note 825 b for this verse which is there quoted from राजमार्तण्ड. The शतपथब्राह्मण (III. 4. 1-2) has 'तस्मै (सोमाय) एतथा राजे पा ब्राह्मणाय वा महोतं महाजं वा पञ्चेचवह मानुषं हविर्देवानामेवमस्मा एतदातिथ्यं करोति.' वसिष्ठधर्मसूत्र (4.8) and याज्ञI, 109 have the same provision as in Satapatha. Medieval writers could not subscribe to this practice. Viśvarūpa says that an ox or goat is killed only it the guest desires it. कल्पतरू (नियतकाल section p. 190) quotes both वसिष्ठ and याज्ञ. and remarks अत्र यहागतम्रोत्रियतप्त्यर्थं गोषधः फर्तध्य इति प्रतीयते तथापि कलियुगे नायं धर्मः किं त युगान्तरे, while thoमिता. explains "उपकल्पयेत्, भववर्थमयमस्माभिः परिकल्पित इति तत्मीत्यर्थ मतदानाय ग्यापादनाया, 'अस्वग्य... ' इति निषेधाच."|परित्यजेदर्थकामौ यौ स्यातां धर्मवर्जितौ। धर्म चाप्यसुखोदर्कं लोकविक्रुष्टमेव च ॥ मनु. IV. 176; विष्णुपुराण III. 11. 7 reads धर्मपीडाकरौ नृप and विगर्हितम्; कर्मणा मनसा वाचा यत्नाद् धर्मं समाचरेत् । अस्वर्ग्यं लोकविद्विष्टं धर्म्यमप्याचरेन्न तु ॥ याज्ञ. I. 156. Vide विष्णुधर्मसूत्र 71. 17–21 (परिहरेत् ) धर्मविरुद्धौ चार्थकामौ लोकविद्विष्टं च धर्म्यमपि। बृहन्नारदीयपु. I. 24. 12 has the verse कर्मणा मनसा ... चरेत् तु सर्वलोकविरुद्धं धर्ममप्याचरेन्न तु । कूर्म० I. 2. 54. 'विषमो विभागः शास्त्रदृष्टस्तथापि लोकविद्विष्टत्वानाऽऽदर्तव्यः।' मिता. on या. II. 117; on याज्ञ. I. 156 the मिता. says 'धर्म्यं विहितमपि लोकविद्विष्टं लोकाभिशास्तिजननं मधुपर्कगोवधादिकं नाचरेत् यस्मादस्वर्ग्यमग्नीषोमीयवत्स्वर्गसाधनं न भवति' ।. Vide also मिता. on या. III. 8, which says about the different days of impurity for sapiṇḍas of the 4th, 5th, 6th or 7th degree prescribed by a Smṛti that it should be discarded 'तद्विगीतत्वात्नादरणीयम् । यद्यप्यविगीतं तथापि मधुपर्काङ्गपश्वालम्भनवल्लोकविद्विष्टत्वात्नानुष्ठेयम् ।'; the स्मृतिच. (I. p. 71) says 'न ब्रूमः शास्त्रतो न पारिणेयेति किंतु लोकविरुद्धत्वात्। यच्च धर्म्यमपि लोकविरुद्धं तन्नादर्तव्यम् । यदुक्तं मनुना—अस्वर्ग्यं० । वराहमिहिरोऽपि लोकाचारस्तावदादौ विचिन्त्यो देशे देशे या स्थितिः सैव कार्या' ॥. Vide above p. 555 note 825 b for this verse which is there quoted from राजमार्तण्ड. The शतपथब्राह्मण (III. 4. 1-2) has 'तस्मै (सोमाय) एतद्वा राज्ञे वा ब्राह्मणाय वा महोक्षं वा महाजं वा पचेयुस्तद्ध मानुषं हविर्देवानामेवमस्मा एतदातिथ्यं करोति.' वसिष्ठधर्मसूत्र (4. 8) and याज्ञ. I. 109 have the same provision as in Śatapatha. Medieval writers could not subscribe to this practice. Viśvarūpa says that an ox or goat is killed only if the guest desires it. कल्पतरु (नियतकाल section p. 190) quotes both वसिष्ठ and याज्ञ० and remarks अत्र यद्द्यागतश्रोत्रियतृप्त्यर्थं गोवधः कर्तव्य इति प्रतीयते तथापि कलियुगे नायं धर्मः किं तु युगान्तरे, while the मिता. explains "उपकल्पयेत्, भवदर्थमयमस्माभिः परिकल्पित इति तत्प्रीत्यर्थं न तु दानाय व्यापपादनाय वा, 'अस्वग्य... ' इति निषेधाच्च."]]`

[[P1271]] [[other's|mother's]] sister's daughter if a maternal uncle's daughter can be married, the [[Smśticandrikā replies'|Smṛticandrikā replies ']]we do not say that she could not be married according to [[Sāstra,|Śāstra,]] but she should not be married as people abhor such a [[marriage '|marriage']] and it quotes Yāj. I. 156 (by oversight it ascribes the verse to Manu). In modern times when any change or reform in religious or social practices is suggested, [[Pandits|Paṇḍits]] calling themselves Sanātanis put forward the pleas that the proposed change or reform is opposed to the [[sāstras,|śāstras,]] that the controversy should be carried on according to the [[Mimāmsā|Mīmāṁsā]] rules, that all Smṛti and other [[diota|dicta]] should be so construed as to lead to *samanvaya* (consistent arrangement of apparently discordant texts), that the historical approach leads nowhere and should not be resorted to. All these objections are [[hero|here]] briefly dealt with. It has been shown how from Vedic times to the present day great changes have taken place in religious ideas, worship and practices, how [[emptis|smṛtis]] from [[Gau tama, Apastamba,|Gautama, Āpastamba,]] Manu downwards espouse conflicting views [[Bo|so]] much so that as early as the [[Mahabharata|Mahābhārata]] [[Vyāsa 2077|Vyāsa]][^2072] had to protest that 'rationalisation is unstable, Vedas are in [[con flict|conflict]] with each other, there is no single sage whose opinion is held to be authoritative (by all), the truth about Dharma is enveloped in a cave (i. e. it cannot be clearly discerned) and that therefore the path (to be followed) is the one followed by the great mass of people.' The [[Mimāmsā|Mīmāṁsā]] does not often lead to certain conclusions and it has already been seen how great [[Mimāṁsā|Mīmāṁsā]] writers like [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] Kumārila, Prabhākara differ among themselves on numerous topics and it would be further illustrated later on how even great [[Mimāṁsakas|Mīmāṁsakas]] evolve highly conflicting conclusions in the interpretation of simple [[smrti|smṛti]] passages. Change is the one absolute in the long history of our religious and social ideas and what those who appeal to the [[historioal|historical]] approach mean is that the [[smftis|smṛtis]] were composed by human authors during a period of 1500 or 2000 years and were greatly influenced by the then prevailing religious and social atmosphere, that many of their [[diota|dicta]] cannot be fully reconciled [[80|so]] as to form a consistent code of conduct, that they cannot be

[^2072]: [[तक प्रतिष्ठः श्रुतयो विभिक्षा नैको मुनिर्यस्थ मतं प्रमाणम् । धर्मस्य वर्ष निहित gurgt alleen te ta: \# grui: il apiga cbap. 313. 117 (in *).|तर्कोऽप्रतिष्ठः श्रुतयो विभिन्ना नैको मुनिर्यस्य मतं प्रमाणम् । धर्मस्य तत्त्वं निहितं गुहायां महाजनो येन गतः स पन्थाः ॥ वनपर्व chap. 313. 117 (in यक्षप्रश्न).]] But this [[verso|verse]] does not occur in the [[Cri, ed. of gaica|Cri. ed. of वनपर्व]] chap 297, though several other questions and answers do occur. The words [['AEI... grunt:'|'महा... पन्थाः']] may also mean 'the path to be followed is the one that some great [[dan (or men) takes ; Arren|man (or men) takes'; महाजन]] in the sense of 'mass of [[people is|people' is]] employed by [[a on. & IV. 2.7 in the words 'PaPA YARGITATA Taneetgereta.|शङ्कराचार्य on वे. सू. IV. 2. 7 in the words 'एवमियमप्युत्क्रान्तिर्महाजनगतैवाङ्गीकर्तव्या'.]]

[[P1272]] regarded as forever binding on all Hindus, that in the 20th [[contury|century]] our people are free to introduce or recognize such changes as are required or have already [[takes|taken]] place among the people in the changed circumstances and that this course is sanctioned even by Manu and [[Yājāavalkya|Yājñavalkya]] and medieval [[emi nent|eminent]] authors and works on the Dharmaśāstra such as the Mitākṣarā and the Kalpataru. It is of course clear that changes in practices and principles should not be made merely for the sake of change or for the whims of some leader, but the [[gover ning|governing]] principle of conscious change should be the feelings and needs of common people, while retaining the foundations on which society has been based for thousands of years.

It has further to be noted that the [[Mimāmsā|Mīmāṁsā]] rules were concerned only with the interpretation of the Vedic passages on sacrificial rites and matters relating thereto and had very little to do with the practices of people unconnected with sacrifices or religious [[rites, 2013|rites.]][^2073] The [[Mimāmsāsūtra|Mīmāṁsāsūtra]] nowhere says that the same rules should be applied to the interpretation of [[smptis.|smṛtis.]] On the contrary, the P. M. [[8.|S.]] is very critical about smṛtis and usages (vide P. M. [[8.|S.]] I. 3. [[3-4|3–4]] and 7). There are fundamental differences between the Veda and the [[smrtis.|smṛtis.]] [[The. Voda|The Veda]] was deemed to be self-existent, eternal and of absolute authority, while [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are pauruṣeya (works of human authors), [[bave|have]] only a derived authority (as being presumed to be based on Vedic passages most of which are not now available), their number is

[^2073]: For an example, where [[Dbarmaśāstra|Dharmaśāstra]] refused to allow the application of [[Midāmsā|Mīmāṁsā]] rules to vratas and utsavas vide above p. 133 [[a. 337.|n. 337.]] For examples of medieval digests [[aad|and]] commentaries [[sayiog|saying]] that [[certaio|certain]] inconvenient smṛti texts referred to previous Kalpas and Yugas, vide Sm. C. I. 24 and Par. M. [[I 2|I. 2]] p. 83 about [[Hārita|Hārīta]] speaking of [[Upangyana|Upanayana]] for women (H. of Dh. Vol. II. p. 295); Par. M. I. part 2 p. 97 refers to Manu [[III, 13|III. 13]] allowing the marriage of a brāhmaṇa with a śūdra woman and [[II1. 14|III. 14]] forbidding it and to Yāj. quoted above in [[aote 2071|note 2071]] and remarks that this [[condict|conflict]] is to be resolved by saying that there is difference of opinion among [[smstis|smṛtis]] on that point or that they refer to different yugas. Vide also above [[p 89|p. 89]] n. 222 on the divergence about Yugādi [[Titbis.|Tithis.]] The V. M. [[(f. 99|(p. 99]] of my edition, Poona, 1926) states [[tbat|that]] the verse of Nārada which says 'as to those sons who are separated by their father [[bimself|himself]] by giving to each of them less or more, it is their duty to abide by it since the father [[bas|has]] power over all' is declared as referring to another Yuga. Vide also V. M. [[Pp. 242-243|pp. 242–243]] about killing an [[atatayin|ātatāyin]] [[brābmaṇa sta: est magret styreparat विमो न वध्यः । युगान्तरेत वष्य एव।. This conclusion is due to the verse in कलिवज्ये section 'आततायिधिजायचाणां धर्मयुन सिमम्। ... इमान् धर्मान कलियुगे rajan hitiya:a.'|brāhmaṇa अतः कलौ स्ववधोद्यतोऽप्यातायी विप्रो न वध्यः । युगान्तरे तु वध्य एव ।. This conclusion is due to the verse in कलिवर्ज्य section 'आततायिद्विजाग्र्याणां धर्मयुद्धेन हिंसनम् । ... इमान् धर्मान् कलियुगे वर्ज्यानाहुर्मनीषिणः ॥'.]]

[[P1273]] very large, they often differ so much from each other that even eminent authors and works like the Mitākṣarā had sometimes to give up in despair the idea of bringing order out of the welter of [[smrti pasaages|smṛti passages]] and in their efforts at *samanvaya* had to say that certain [[smrtis|smṛtis]] refer to a former Kalpa or Yuga [[(i, e.|(i. e.]] to [[&|a]] society of several thousands or millions of years ago). One of the well-known maxims of the P. M. [[8.|S.]] is the one called [['Sarvaśa khāpratyayanyāya '|'Sarvaśā-khāpratyayanyāya']][^2074] or [['Sākhāntarādhikarananyaya'|'Śākhāntarādhikaraṇanyāya']] (II. 4. [[8-33).|8–33).]] In the several recensions of the Veda and the [[Brāhma pas|Brāhmaṇas]] attached to the Vedic recensions the [[game|same]] rite is mentioned and is dilated upon with details some of which occur in one or more recensions but are absent from [[othera.|others.]] Jaimini and [[Sabara|Śabara]] establish that all the [[Sākhās|Śākhās]] of the Veda and the [[Brahma nas|Brāhmaṇas]] form one corpus, that such rites as Agnihotra and [[Jyotiṣ toma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] are one and the same in all recensions of the [[Veda. though|Veda, though]] the details may vary here and there, and this is the proper conclusion because the name ([[Jyotiṣtoma|Jyotiṣṭoma]] and the like) is the same in all recensions, the promised [[roward|reward]] of the rite is the same, the materials of the sacrifice and the devatā are the same and the codanās (the exhortatory sentences) are the same. This very maxim was extended to [[smftis|smṛtis]] from very early [[times, Vigya rūpa, Medhātithi,|times. Viśvarūpa, Medhātithi,]] the [[Mitakṣarā, 3075 Apararka|Mitākṣarā,]][^2075] Aparārka and other commentators extend this to [[smrtis|smṛtis]] and provide that where [[smrtis|smṛtis]] are in conflict there is to be an option but that in other [[CABOS|cases]] all the details are to be added up. But as an option was liable to eight faults the tendency developed that all [[smsti|smṛti]] passages on a topic were to be so interpreted as to give no rise to conflict or to allow no option by means of various devices

IA I.L.R.|'एतेषां (धर्मशास्त्राणां) प्रत्येकं प्रामाण्येऽपि साकाङ्क्षाणामाकाङ्क्षापूरणमन्यतः क्रियते विरोधे विकल्पः ।' q. in I. L. R.]] 39 Bom. 373 at p. 379.

[[P1274]] such as [[. Visaya-vyavasthā',|'Viṣaya-vyavasthā',]] reference to another Kalpa or Yuga and so on. For example, even as regards the stock example of [[mkalpa|vikalpa]] (viz. taking or not taking the [[Sodasin|Ṣoḍaśin]] cup in [[Attrātra)|Atirātra)]] the Mit. says that it is proper to assume that it should be taken if it is possible to do so, or it may be assumed that by taking the Ṣoḍaśin cup in Atirātra the attainment of [[scarga|svarga]] is hastened &c.[^2076] The result of regarding all [[smrtis|smṛtis]] as constituting one Śāstra was that many simple rites became in course of time [[Overloaded|overloaded]] with details, complicated and taxing to the [[perfor. mer.|performer.]] But sometimes it is necessary to apply this doctrine. For example, Yāj. I. 135 requires the [[snūtaka|snātaka]] not to look at the sun [[(nekṣetārkam );|(*nekṣetārkam*);]] this would mean that to look at the sun is prohibited at all times, but the dictum of Yāj. should be read along with Manu [[IV.37|IV. 37]] which forbids a person to look at the rising or setting sun or at the sun when eclipsed or as reflected in water or when it is at the meridian. So the rule will be as stated by Manu.

While on this subject of the authoritativeness of [[Smrtis|Smṛtis]] it would be useful to glance at what Jaimini and particularly Kumārila say about the [[Vedāngas.|Vedāṅgas.]] As regards [[Siksā (Phone tics )|Śikṣā (Phonetics)]] Kumārila says that the account given in that work about the organs employed in pronunciation and the rules about Vedic accents have their use in the correct recitation of mantras. On the Kalpa-sūtras Jaimini[^2077] has a separate [[adhikarang|adhikaraṇa]] (I. 3. 11-14). [[Sabara|Śabara]] mentions by name the Māsaka, [[Hastika|Hāstika]] and [[Kaundinyaka|Kauṇḍinyaka]] Kalpasūtras and the Tantravārtika draws [[&|a]] distinction between kalpa (ritual of [[Srauta|Śrauta]] sacrifices ) and [[Kalpa. sūtras,|Kalpasūtras,]] and refers by name to eight.

These sūtras (PMS [[1. 3. 11-14)|I. 3. 11-14)]] have been interpreted by Kumārila in several different ways, firstly as referring to the authoritativeness of Kalpasūtras ( as done by [[Sabara ), Becondly|Śabara ), secondly]]

III. 243.|न च षोडशिग्रहणाग्रहणवद्विषमयोरपि विकल्पोपपत्तिरिति वाच्यं, यतस्तत्रापि सति सम्भवे ग्रहणमेवेति युक्तं कल्पयितुम् । यद्वा षोडशिग्रहणानुगृहीतेनातिरात्रेण क्षिप्रं स्वर्गादिसिद्धिरतिशयितस्य वा स्वर्गस्येति कल्पनीयम् । मिता० on याज्ञ. III. 243.]]

[^2077]: [[ga: 74T: Fra Carrotia geyhi for 54: grurit si form ata ते कल्पा लक्षणार्थोनि खुवाणीति प्रचक्षते। कल्पनाद्धि प्रयोगाणां कल्पोऽनुठानसाधनम्। वर्ष सुचनाचेयां स्वयं कल्प्यप्रयोगकम् । कल्पाः पठितसिद्धा हि प्रयोगाणां प्रतिऋतु। तन्त्रवा. on I. 3,11 ( \nq a ra )|के पुनः कल्पाः कानि सूत्राणीति उच्यते । सिद्धरूपः प्रयोगो यः कर्मणामनुगम्यते । ते कल्पा लक्षणार्थानि सूत्राणीति प्रचक्षते । कल्पनाव्र्हि प्रयोगाणां कल्पोऽनुष्ठानसाधनम् । सूत्रं तु सूचनाचेष्टां स्वयं कल्प्यप्रयोगकम् । कल्पाः पठितसिद्धा हि प्रयोगाणां प्रतिक्रतु । तन्त्रवा. on I. 3. 11 (प्रयोगशास्त्रमिति चेत्)]] p. 229. The principal distinction is that Kalpas about each vedic sacrifice lay down simply the procedure that is ready made as handed down by oral tradition, while Kalpasūtras like those of [[Aivalāyana, Baljavāpi, Drāhyāyaṇa, Lāyāyana|Āśvalāyana, Baijavāpi, Drāhyāyaṇa, Lāṭyāyana]] and Kātyāyana contain [[sanfras, B guns bhāsās,|sañjñās, paribhāṣās,]] general rules, exceptions and definitions etc.

[[P1275]] [[As|as]] referring to all the [[siz Vadangas|six Vedāṅgas]] and thirdly as referring to the [[80-oalled smrtia|so-called smṛtis]] of Buddha and others. That the Buddhist works claimed to be [[smrtis|smṛtis]] is clear from the Manusmṛti[^2078] 'the [[smrtis|smṛtis]] that are beyond the [[palo|pale]] of the Veda and whatever other false systems may exist, they are all fruitless as to the [[(soul's ) Hereafter|(soul's) Hereafter]] (destiny after bodily death), since they are all based on [[tamas (ignorance).|tamas (ignorance).']] Here some words will be said about the [[Vedāngas|Vedāṅgas]] according to Kumārila. According to both [[Sabara|Śabara]] and Kumārila, [[vyakarana|vyākaraṇa]] is dealt with in Jaimini I. [[3.24-29.|3. 24-29.]] In the Tantravārtika Kumārila launches an elaborate and telling (pūrvapakṣa) onslaught against Pāṇini himself, [[aganiet|aganist]] Kātyāyana (the author of the Vārtikas) and Patañjali (author of the Mahābhāṣya) in which some very interesting points are made but they have to be [[pagsed|passed]] over for reasons of space. Kumārila says that the proper subject of grammar is to [[detera mine|determine]] what words are correct or incorrect. It is [[intersting|interesting]] to note that in the prima facie attack against grammar two [[Pūrvamlmāṁsā|Pūrvamīmāṁsā]] sūtras are said to offend against grammar.[^2079]

The Nirukta of [[Yaska,|Yāska,]] which is one of the six [[angas|aṅgas]] of the Veda, is an extensive work and deals with the derivation of words, semantics, explanations of several hundred Vedic verses in whole or in part. Jaimini agrees with several conclusions of the [[Nirakta,|Nirukta,]] which claims that without its help one cannot [[under stand|understand]] the meaning of the Veda, that it has a purpose of its own and is a complement of grammar, and it controverts at great length the view of [[Kautsa 2010|Kautsa]][^2080] that Vedic mantras have no meaning

[^2080]: [[अथापीदमन्तरेण मन्त्रेभ्यर्थप्रत्ययो न विद्यते सदिवं विधारथानं ज्याकरणस्य काल्यै स्वार्थसाधकं च। यदि मन्त्रार्थप्रत्ययायानर्थक भवतीति कौत्सोऽनर्थका हिमन्त्राः。... अर्थतन्तः शन्दसामाग्यावेत यज्ञस्प समुद्धं पपसमुच यत्कर्म क्रियमाणमृग्यजुर्वाभिषदतीति|अथापीदमन्तरेण मन्त्रेष्वर्थप्रत्ययो न विद्यते तदिदं विद्यास्थानं व्याकरणस्य कार्त्स्न्यं स्वार्थसाधकं च । यदि मन्त्रार्थप्रत्ययायानर्थकं भवतीति कौत्सोनर्थका हि मन्त्राः । ... अर्थवन्तः शब्दसामान्यादेतद्वै यज्ञस्य समृद्धं यद्रूपसमृद्धं यत्कर्म क्रियमाणमृग्यजुर्वाभिवदतीति]] [[irport PET I. 15.|षे ब्राह्मणम् । निरुक्त I. 15.]] The word [[produsura|विद्यास्थानं]] which is employed by [[Yaj, also (1.3)|Yāj. also (I. 3)]] may be noted. Vide notes 1780-81 for the passage [[the ... pera.|एतद्वै ... वदति.]] The Ait. Br. ([[cbap. III. 5)|chap. III. 5)]] states that 13 verses are repeated at the time of [[producidg ite|producing fire]] by attrition and examines them to show how they [[art H .|are रूपसमृद्ध.]] All that it says on these 13 verses is [[aot|not]] quite [[convinciog,|convincing,]] but [[so|in]] the case of several of them such as [[Rg VI. 16,13-15|Ṛg VI. 16. 13-15]] it is [[conviociog;|convincing ;]] vide 'Our Heritage' [[(Calcolta|(Calcutta]] Sanskrit [[College )|College)]] vol. V. part 2 (for July-December 1957) for a lengthy paper [['on the justification of rūpasamrddha verses in the Ait. Br.'|'on the justification of rūpasamṛddha verses in the Ait. Br.']] by Vimana Chandra Bhattacharya (pp. 119-146).

[[P1276]] (or purpose) and emphatically [[deserts|asserts]] that Vedic mantras have [[&|a]] meaning (and a purpose) because the words they contain [[aro|are]] the same as those employed [[ip|in]] popular speech and that [[& Bra. bmana|a Brāhmaṇa]] passage states that sacrifice has a perfect form when a [[rk|ṛk]] verse or yajus formula refers to the rite that is being [[perform ed.|performed.]] Jaimini (in [[I. 2. 4. f|I. 2. 4 ff]] and I. 3. 30) holds that Mantras are meaningful and that Vedic words and words in popular Sanskrit are the same and the things denoted by them both are the same (except that some difference may be caused by accentuation in the Veda).[^2081] The very first sentence of [[Sabara's bhasya|Śabara's bhāṣya]] says the same thing. Jaimini further[^2082] agrees with the Nirukta about the denotation of verbs and [[noung.|nouns.]] [[Sabara|Śabara]] frequently quotes either the very words of the Nirukta or pointedly refers to them.[^2083] Jaimini agrees with the Nirukta in the matter of the nature and functions of the Devatās in sacrifices.[^2084]
