[[P1277]] [[Kumarile|Kumārila]] makes a general remark that all the six [[angas|aṅgas]] and the [[Dharmasastres|Dharmaśāstras]] are comprehended under [[Smrti, 2005|Smṛti.]][^2085]

It appears that Jaimini did not attach much importance to [[Smśtis,|Smṛtis,]] since out of 915 (or 1000) [[Adhikarapas|Adhikaraṇas]] barely about a dozen are concerned in any way with smṛtis alone, viz. I. [[3, 1-2, I. 3,3-4,|3. 1–2, I. 3. 3–4,]] I. 3. 11-14, I. 3. 24-29, VI. 2. 21-22, VI. 2. 30, VI. 8. 23-24, VII. 1. 10, IX. [[%. 1-2,|2. 1–2,]] XII. 4. 43. [[Sabara,|Śabara,]] however, refers to [[emptis|smṛtis]] in many more places such as on VI. [[I. 5|1. 5]] and 13, IX. [[1.6-9.|1. 6–9.]]

We are here concerned with the attitude of Jaimini and his early commentators like [[Sabara|Śabara]] and Kumārila towards [[Smrtis.|Smṛtis.]] The established conclusion arrived at by Jaimini (in [[1. 3.2)|I. 3. 2)]] is that in case of conflict with the Veda [[Smrti|Smṛti]] is to be discarded, but if there be no [[confliot|conflict]] (of Smṛti with Veda), it may be inferred (that Smṛti is based on some Vedic text).

This amounts to saying that there is always [[&|a]] presumption that smṛtis are based on Veda, if their prescriptions are not directly in conflict with the Veda. [[Sportis proscribe|Smṛtis prescribe]] the [[obser vance|observance]] of Aṣṭakā śrāddhas, the digging of [[tanke,|tanks,]] following and obeying a teacher. These are authoritative because they do not conflict with any Vedic text of opposite import. The Smṛtis do claim that they are based on the Veda. Gautama says so (in XI. 19 in note 2042) and Manu states (II. 7) 'whatever Dharma is proclaimed by Manu for anybody has all been ([[already)|(already)]] declared in the Veda, since Veda possesses all knowledge'.

In connection with smṛtis and usages an important question [[ariges|arises]] viz. if there is conflict between smṛtis and usages of śiṣṭas, which is to be regarded as more authoritative. Kumārila holds that if the practice of good men is not opposed to what is taught in the Veda and Smṛti, such practices can be [[undorstood|understood]] to be authoritative but if there is contradiction between what is taught by Veda and by Smṛti and by usages, then their [[authori tativeness|authoritativeness]] would be opposed (i. e. would be lost) and further that [[1086|]][^2086] smṛti is more authoritative because it is based directly

[^2085]: [[Free TRI NÁTETTI arraigeria. p. 285 on 1. 3. 27.|स्मृतित्वं त्वङ्गानां धर्मसूत्राणां चाविशिष्टम् । तन्त्रवा. p. 285 on I. 3. 27.]]

[^2086]: [[2086, fate qafeterora un prodi anaeratui ALU regel यदि शिष्टस्य कोपः स्याद्विरुध्येत प्रमाणता। तवकोपात नाचारममाणत्वं विरुष्यते ॥ सन्त्रबा. on I.3. 8 p. 216 and again on p. 220 ' a gitmi a farranca: F** समस्पयमणीता हि स्मृतिः सोपनिवन्धना। तथा अत्यनुमान हि निर्षिामुपजायते। आचारात स्मुर्ति ज्ञात्वा अतिर्विज्ञायते ततः । तेन ग्यन्तरित तस्य पामाण्यं विकायते.' प्रत्यय means pra fa rit (as Manu sed others were sages).|शिष्टं यावच्छ्रुतिस्मृत्योस्तेन यच्च विरुध्यते । तच्छिष्टाचरणं धर्मे प्रमाणत्वेन गम्यते । यदि शिष्टस्य कोपः स्याद्विरुध्येत प्रमाणता । तदकोपाच्च नाचारप्रमाणत्वं विरुध्यते ॥ तन्त्रवा. on I. 3. 8 p. 216 and again on p. 220 'उभयोः श्रुतिमूलत्वं न स्मृत्याचारयोः समम् । सम्प्रत्ययप्रणीता हि स्मृतिः सोपनिबन्धना । तथा श्रुत्यनुमानं हि निर्विशङ्कमुपजायते । आचारात्तु स्मृतिं ज्ञात्वा श्रुतिर्विज्ञायते ततः । तेन दृश्यन्तरितं तस्य प्रामाण्यं विप्रकृष्यते ॥.' प्रत्यय means ज्ञानं विश्वासो वा (as Manu and others were sages).]]

[[P1278]] on the Veda, while in the case of usages, one has to infer that śiṣṭas must have based their conduct on some [[smrti, whioh|smṛti, which]] in its turn has to be deemed to be based on some Vedic text [[1. e.|i. e.]] usage is removed from the Veda by one step further than Smṛtis and besides it is well-known that [[smstis|smṛtis]] are compositions by [[porsons|persons]] who were deeply versed in the Veda, while the origins of usages are vague and unascertainable.

Though this is the [[theoretioal|theoretical]] rule, supported by [[Dharma Śāstra|Dharmaśāstra]] works [[guch|such]] as Vas. I. 5, Mit. on Yāj. I. 7 and II. 117, Kullūka on Manu II. 10, one finds that usages opposed to [[smśtis|smṛtis]] have been in vogue from ancient times (such as the usage of marrying one's maternal uncle's daughter that is condemned by Manu and authoritative [[smrtis).|smṛtis).]] The [[Vyavahāramayūkla|Vyavahāramayūkha]] (p. 98 of the text in B. G. S.) was constrained to observe that in the Purāṇas one often finds usages opposed to smṛtis. The courts have held that 'immemorial usage is transcendental law' and that this is the translation by Sir William Jones of [['Scāraḥ|'ācāraḥ]] paramo [[dharmaḥ' in Madu|dharmaḥ' in Manu]] I. 108. Vide H. of Dh. Vol. III. [[pp 874-876|pp. 874-876]] for two interpretations of this verse of Manu I. 108 in modern times. Manu in (II. [[10 )|10)]] asserts that Veda and [[smrti|smṛti]] are in all matters not to be subjected to [[(hostile ) reaso ning,|(hostile) reasoning,]] since Dharma shone forth from these two. Manu further provided that as [[rogards|regards]] matters not specifically provided for, whatever brāhmaṇas that have studied the Veda together with the supporting lores (viz. the six [[aigas, mimārsā,|aṅgas, mīmāṁsā,]] Purāṇas &c.) and that make others realize what [[Sruti gays are sistas|Śruti says are śiṣṭas]] and whatever they declare to be the Dharma in such unprovided cases is undoubtedly the Dharma.

It was declared by the Privy Council that 'under the Hindu system of law clear proof of usage will outweigh the written text of the [[law. 2087|law.']][^2087] From very ancient times customs and usages have been held to be authoritative. For [[examplo,|example,]] Gautama XI. 20 says 'the usages of countries, castes and families are authoritative, provided they are not [[(directly )|(directly)]] opposed to Vedic texts.' Manu I. 118 states that he has embodied in his Śāstra the long-standing customs and usages of [[coun tries, castos,|countries, castes,]] families, heretical sects and guilds. In some matters

[^2087]: In Collector of Madura v. Mootoo Ramlinga, 12 M. I. A. 397 at p. 436 the Privy Council say so.

[[P1279]] modern legislation in India allows to customs and usages over-riding[^2088] [[authority,|authority.]]

In the examination of certain Kalivarjyas mentioned above, (p. [[1268-1270)|1268–1270)]] it has been shown how several actions prohibited in the Kalivarjya texts were practised in Vedic times or were [[onjoined|enjoined]] by Vedic texts.

Kumārila points out that even [[brāhmapa|brāhmaṇa]] women of [[Ahi. cohatrā|Ahicchatrā]] and Mathurā drank[^2089] liquor in his day, that northern [[brābmaṇas|brāhmaṇas]] engage in such transactions as the gift and sale of horses that have a [[mano,|mane,]] of mules, camels and animals that have two rows of teeth and eat from the same plate with their wives, children and friends, that brāhmaṇas of the South enter into matrimony with the maternal uncle's daughter and take their meals while seated on a couch of [[wiokorwork,|wickerwork,]] that both (brāhmaṇas of the North and [[South )|South)]] take cooked food (kept in pots) that remains after their friends or relatives have [[partakon|partaken]] of it or that has been touched by them [[at the time of eating),|(at the time of eating),]] that they chew [[tāmbula|tāmbūla]] of [[botol|betel]] leaves, betelnut, catechu and lime made into a roll that has been touched (in a [[botel-seller's|betel-seller's]] shop) and do not sip water at the end of the chewing, wear clothes cleaned by washermen and brought on the back of asses, that they do not avoid the contact of persons guilty of grave sins except of the murder of a brāhmaṇa; there are also an infinite number of obvious violations of the subtle dictates of Dharma laid down for each man, caste or family, that these [[transgressions)|(transgressions)]] are contrary to [[Sruti|Śruti]] and Smṛti and have a visible purpose and that it is not possible to regard such adulterated practices as Dharma inculcated by sadācāra. The requisites of valid usages according to the [[Pūrvamlmārsā sohool|Pūrvamīmāṁsā school]] are that they must be ancient, not opposed to the express texts of the Veda or of [[Smrti,|Smṛti,]] must be such as are deemed by [[śistas|śiṣṭas]] to be obligatory and must

[^2088]: For [[examplo,|example,]] the Hindu Marriage Act (25 of [[1955 )|1955)]] by section 5 provides that the parties to a marriage must not be within the degrees of prohibited relationship and [[most|must]] not be [[sapindas|sapiṇḍas]] of each other and yet adds 'unless the custom or usage governing each of them [[peraits|permits]] of a marriage between the two'.

[^2089]: For the original text of this interesting passage of the [[Tantra vārtika,|Tantravārtika,]] vide H. of Dh. Vol. III. [[P.|p.]] 848 [[aote|note]] 1645 ; for different views on marrying a maternal uncle's daughter vide H. of [[Db.|Dh.]] Vol. II. pp [[458-463 ;|458–463;]] for eating from the same plate with wife and children, vide H. of [[Db.|Dh.]] Vol. II. p. 765, and about gift or sale of horses and animals with two rows of teeth, vide [[H, of Dh.|H. of Dh.]] Vol. II. p. 181 and [[Jai,|Jai.]] III. 4. 28-31.

[[P1280]] be observed by them with the consciousness that they are [[sq.|so,]] they must not [[havo & visible purpone|have a visible purpose]] and must not be [[immoral,|immoral.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. [[III,|III.]] p. [[853-855|853–855]] for discussion on valid [[ous toms|customs]] and usages.

The general rules laid down by Dharmaśāstra works about the binding character of customs and usages are in line with the rules of the [[Pūrvamimāmgā.|Pūrvamīmāṁsā.]] Vide H. of Dh. Vol. [[IIL pp. 871 884.|III. pp. 871–884.]] But deviation from Vedic texts and smṛtis gradually arose [[88|as]] noted above.

Even all actions done by great men cannot be called [[sadācūru,|sadācāra,]] according to [[Kumārija,|Kumārila,]] if they can be attributed to greed or some such base motive and should not be looked upon as Dharma. Gautama,[^2090] Āp. Dh. S. and the [[Bhagavata-pura ṇa|Bhāgavata-purāṇa]] remark that even great men were guilty of violations of Dharma and defiant excesses, that those great men incurred no sin (got over the effects of such violations) on account of their being endowed with eminent [[tapas ( spiritual merit due to auster itios)|tapas (spiritual merit due to austerities)]] but that others of later ages following their examples and treading the same path would sink (into hell). [[Kumarila|Kumārila]] cites twelve such instances of lapses, explains them away or says they were due to wrath [[of|or]] other passions and were not intended by the authors (of those acts) as dharma and are not looked upon as śiṣṭācāra by modern men. The twelve instances are: [[Praja pati|Prajāpati]] who became enamoured of his own daughter (Uṣas, explains Kumārila), Indra is described as the [[jāra|*jāra*]] (paramour) of [[Abalya|Ahalyā]] (night, according to Kumārila's explanation); [[Vasiṣtha|Vasiṣṭha]] through grief for the slaughter of his hundred [[song|sons]] by a demon [[contemp lated|contemplated]] suicide; Viśvāmitra who became officiating priest to [[Trisanku|Triśaṅku]] that had become a [[capdāla through a ourse;|cāṇḍāla through a curse;]] [[Nabusa|Nahuṣa]] who, when he occupied the position of Indra, made approaches to [[Saci,|Śacī,]] wife of Indra and was transformed into an [[ajagara|*ajagara*]] (huge reptile); Purūravas, when separated from [[Urvasi,|Urvaśī,]] thought of dying (by hanging &c.); [[Krṣpa-dvaipāyana|Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana]] who was [[naiṣthika (perpe tual)|*naiṣṭhika* (perpetual)]] brahmacārin raised sons on the widows of his uterine brother, [[Vicitravlrya;|Vicitravīrya;]] [[Bhiṣma,|Bhīṣma,]] though unmarried, performed [[Asy amedba|Aśvamedha]] sacrifices; [[Dhrtarāstra,|Dhṛtarāṣṭra,]] though congenitally blind, performed

[^2090]: [[2090, EU raf: MEHH REATTI HICUCI 1. 3-4; धर्मव्यतिक्रमः साहसं च पूर्वेषाम् । तेषां तेजीविशेषेण प्रत्यवायो न विद्यते। सबन्धीक्ष्य \ngara: aitasunt: 1 314. 4. II. 6. 13. 7-9; vide per X. Pārvārdha 33, 30 धर्मव्यतिक्रमोट ईश्वराणां च साहसम् । तेजीयसन दोषाय बो सर्वजो पथा. That by prānāyāma and other practices the impurities of the santos and mlod are cleared away is stated by Manu VI, 71.|दृष्टो धर्मव्यतिक्रमः साहसं च महताम् । अवरदौर्बल्यात् । गौ I. 3-4 ; दृष्टो धर्मव्यतिक्रमः साहसं च पूर्वेषाम् । तेषां तेजोविशेषेण प्रत्यवायो न विद्यते । तदन्वीक्ष्य प्रयुञ्जानः सीदत्यवरः । आप. ध. सू. II. 6. 13. 7-9; vide भागवत X. Pūrvārdha 33. 30 धर्मव्यतिक्रमो दृष्ट ईश्वराणां च साहसम् । तेजीयसां न दोषाय वह्नेः सर्वभुजो यथा. That by prāṇāyāma and other practices the impurities of the senses and mind are cleared away is stated by Manu VI. 71.]]

[[P1281]] sacrifices which blind men are not authorised to do (compare Jai. VI. [[I.|1.]] 42); five [[Pāṇdavas|Pāṇḍavas]] marrying one [[Draupadi;|Draupadī;]] [[Yudhi. ṣthira|Yudhiṣṭhira]] who prevaricated and thereby caused the death of Droṇa, his brāhmaṇa guru; [[Krona|Kṛṣṇa]] and Arjuna are described in the Mahābhārata as drunk with wine ('ubhau [[madhyāsavakṣibau dṛstau me Keśayārjunau'|madhvāsavakṣībau dṛṣṭau me Keśavārjunau']] in Udyogaparva 59. 5) and married their maternal uncle's daughters; Rāma performed [[Asyamedha|Aśvamedha]] by associating a golden image of [[Bitā|Sītā]] with him.

Kumārila displays great ingenuity in explaining away most of [[thege|these]] transgressions by plausible and specious [[argu ments,|arguments,]] by relying on the great tapas in some cases (such as about Viśvāmitra) and in some cases by downright denial (as in the case of Subhadrā being the sister of [[Krona ). 2091|Kṛṣṇa).]][^2091] Those [[interès ted|interested]] may refer for all these instances and their explanations by Kumārila to H. of Dh. Vol. [[III, pp. 845-848.|III. pp. 845–848.]]

One interesting [[adhikarana|adhikaraṇa]] is the Holākādhikaraṇa (Jai. I. 3. 15-23). It is said that Holākā should be observed by the easterners, [[Anninaibuka|Āhninaibuka]] by [[Southernors,|Southerners,]] [[Udyrsabhayajña|Udvṛṣabhayajña]] by northerners. The established conclusion is that such [[observancos|observances]] are meant for all (and not merely for persons of the East or South &c.), if they are fit to be done by[^2092] [[easternors or souther ners|easterners or southerners]] (there is no reason why these should not be observances fit for all). The general rule about Vedic vidhis is that they are applicable to all [[Aryas;|Āryas;]] no cogent reason exists why a [[restrioted|restricted]] Vedic text should be inferred in the case of the above [[observa. ncas.|observances.]] Vide H. of Dh. [[VOL|Vol.]] III. pp. 851-853 for detailed [[explana tion|explanation]] of this maxim and above pp. 237 ff. The Dāyabhāga (II. 40 and [[VI, 22-23)|VI. 22-23)]] refers to this maxim in connection with a double share to be allotted to the acquirer and with reference to a person acquiring property without detriment to the ancestral estate and explains the purpose of the Holākādhikaraṇa.

The [[Holākādhikarspanyāya|Holākādhikaraṇanyāya]] is frequently mentioned by writers on Dharmaśāstra. Viśvarūpa on [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] I. 53 quotes the

[^2091]: [[2091. The आदिपर्व expressly says about सुभता 'दुहिता पनुदेवस्य वारवेषस्य TAHTI snage 219, 18 ( cr, ed. chap. 211. 18). The ithtarany of you states 'एवमर्जुनस्य मातुलकन्यकापा: सुभद्रायाः परिणयेऽपि सुभद्राया पडदेवकन्यावस्थ fara sfarceuti? (p. 48, Chowkhamba S, S. 1924). This is a sample of what to expect from some orthodox Sangkrit writers, when bard pressed by facts contrary to their pat theories or vlews,|The आदिपर्व expressly says about सुभद्रा 'दुहिता वसुदेवस्य वासुदेवस्य च स्वसा । आदिपर्व 219. 18 (cr. ed. chap. 211. 18). The मीमांसाकौस्तुभ of खण्डदेव states 'एवमर्जुनस्य मातुलकन्यकायाः सुभद्रायाः परिणयेऽपि सुभद्राया वसुदेवकन्यात्वस्य साक्षात् क्वचिदप्यश्रवणात् ।' (p. 48, Chowkhamba S. S. 1924). This is a sample of what to expect from some orthodox Sanskrit writers, when hard pressed by facts contrary to their pet theories or views.]]

[^2092]: [[2092. perearea un/13-- ft ... Farapu . I. 3, 16) i sa: कार्यत्वे सति सर्वधर्मत्वम्। विश्वरूप on या. 1.53.|न्यायविद्धश्च शाब्दिकाः - अपि वा ... विधानस्य-इति (जै. I. 3. 16) । अतः कार्यत्वे सति सर्वधर्मत्वम् । विश्वरूप on या. I. 53.]]

[[P1282]] [[siddhāntasutra|siddhāntasūtra]] 'api [[va|vā]] sarvadharmaḥ [[gyāt|syāt]] [[tannyayatvid vidhānagya'|tannyāyatvād vidhānasya']] (Jai. I. 3. [[16 )|16)]] and adds that it must be regarded as [['Sarvadbarma'(as|'Sarvadharma' (as]] applicable to all) if it is recommended as [[som thing|something]] to be observed by some people. There is a difference of opinion between medieval writers on Dharmaśāstra as to the exact import of this [[adhikarana.|adhikaraṇa.]] The [[Dayabhāga|Dāyabhāga]] says[^2093] that [[tho|the]] Śruti to be inferred from the practice of Holākā by easterners is simply the general one [[(sāmābya-śruti)|(sāmānya-śruti)]] that [[Holaka|Holākā]] rites should be observed. On the other hand [[Govindananda|Govindānanda]] in his Com. on the [[Prayascitta-viveks|Prāyaścitta-viveka]] of Śūlapāṇi says that the [[Holakādhikarana|Holākādhikaraṇa]] leads to this only that the Śruti to be inferred from this practice is not in the form 'the easterners should [[por form Holāki'|perform Holākā']] but it is in a general form viz. 'the usage of a country should be observed by those in the country'.

[^2093]: [[2093. सरमायस्मादेवाचारात् मंतिवाक्याहा या अतिरपश्य कल्पनीया तव तदतल्या चारासस्य पतिपदस्य चोपपत्ते तत्राधिककल्पनेति होलाकाधिकरणस्पार्थः । दायभाग II. 42; माहॉलाका कर्वन्यति विशेषहतिर्न कल्ल्यते किंतु वेशधर्मः कर्तव्य इति सामान्यत एक, अपचा देशान्तरेभाचारान्वपद अत्यन्तरकल्पनागौरवं स्थादिति शेलाकाधिकरणग्यापः। mandatet on 49 paraan p. 142 (Jiv. ed.).|तस्माद्यस्मादेवाचारात् स्मृतिवाक्याद्वा या श्रुतिरवश्यं कल्पनीया तयैव तत्तद्रूपाचारांशस्य स्मृतिपदस्य चोपपत्तेर्न तत्राधिककल्पनेति होलाकाधिकरणस्यार्थः । दायभाग II. 42 ; प्राच्यैर्होलाका कर्तव्येति विशेषश्रुतिर्न कल्प्यते किं तु देशधर्मः कर्तव्य इति सामान्यत एव, अन्यथा देशान्तरे आचारान्तरात् श्रुत्यन्तरकल्पनागौरवं स्यादिति होलाकाधिकरणन्यायः । तत्त्वार्थकौमुदी on प्रायश्चित्तविवेक p. 142 (Jīv. ed.).]]
